- [x] **PT-39 [FIXED — empty name now blocks Start with a hint (no silent "My Club").] (stage: menu/settings regression): [low-med] New Game with an empty name field silently founds a bloodline called "My Club" — no prompt, no guard.** The name field can be left blank: START stays enabled and clicking it (or pressing Enter) runs `startNewGame`, which does `const name = rawName.trim() || 'My Club'` — so a fat-finger or a player who expected to be prompted skips the signature "name your bloodline" beat entirely and gets a generic "My Club" that then carries down every generation, directly undercutting the core hook ("the family name carries down the generations"). why it hurts: the whole premise is the family name, and it's the one thing silently discardable at the very first screen · fix: disable START (or show a gentle "name your bloodline first" nudge) while `mm-name` is empty/whitespace, instead of defaulting silently (`client/src/main.ts:495`, listeners `:652-653`).
- [x] **PT-40 [FIXED — family name is capitalised (rossi → Rossi) in preview + saved club.] (stage: menu/settings regression): [low] the family name is never capitalised — a lowercase entry yields "rossi's Club · the rossi bloodline" in the live preview AND in the saved club name.** The preview echoes the raw trimmed value verbatim (`crest(raw + "'s Club")` / `<b>${raw}'s Club</b>` / `the ${raw} bloodline`) and `startNewGame` stores the raw trimmed string as the club name, so a naive player who types "rossi"/"a" gets a permanently lowercase, unpolished club + bloodline that reads as a bug on a premium title. why it hurts: club/family names are expected title-cased; the un-capitalised name persists into the hub, league table and every heir · fix: title-case the display and the stored name (`client/src/main.ts:646` preview, `:495` stored `name`).
- [x] **PT-41 [FIXED — name maxlength 24→18 so the header wordmark no longer wraps/cramps.] (stage: menu/settings regression): [low] at the 24-char max the New-Game club-name wordmark grows large, wraps to two lines and nearly collides with the crest and the flanking "Your club:" / "· the … bloodline" labels.** The maxLength cap correctly prevents true overflow (scrollWidth==clientWidth), but the preview wordmark uses a fixed large size that doesn't shrink for long names, so a max-length family name renders cramped/tight against its neighbours (visible with "Wolverhamptonshire-Feath's Club"). why it hurts: the preview is the New-Game screen's hero element and looks unfinished at long names · fix: scale the `<b>…'s Club</b>` font-size down as the name lengthens (or reduce the max a little) so the wordmark fits comfortably on one row (`client/src/main.ts:646`, preview typography in the stylesheet).

<!-- ═══ REBALANCE VALIDATION PASS 2026-08-29 (fresh-player critic, browser, localhost:5173) ═══
Fresh new game "Ferreira" → signed Dane Ferreira (FW, ★4, pot ★★★★★) → Super-Agent → played the WHOLE 120-turn
career (age 10→23) always answering the demanded tag with a matching card, then took the handoff and simmed
TWO manager seasons. 100 graded card turns recorded. Zero JS console errors across the whole run.

MEASURED GRADE DISTRIBUTION (100 graded turns, correct/best-available card every time):
  71 ⭐ Brilliant · 22 ✓ Solid · 5 ◦ Unlucky · 1 ◦ Scrappy · 1 ✗ Poor
  BY PHASE — turns 1–30: 15 Brilliant / 11 Solid / 1 Unlucky / 1 Poor  (54% Brilliant — a real spread)
            turns 31–54: 15 Brilliant /  7 Solid / 1 Unlucky            (65% Brilliant)
            turns 55–120: 41 Brilliant / 4 Solid / 3 Unlucky / 1 Scrappy (84% Brilliant — back to the old problem)

1. CARD DIFFICULTY — PARTIAL PASS / LATE-GAME FAIL. The early game is genuinely fixed: over the first 30 turns
   only 54% of correct plays graded Brilliant, with Solid the modal result and a Poor and an Unlucky landing.
   But the spread decays with the star's stats: over the last 65 turns 84% graded Brilliant, essentially the
   pre-fix 89% (33/37). Filed as PT-1001.
   SECOND DEMANDED TAG — PASS (weak evidence). Every one of the 6 turns where the chosen card covered two
   demanded tags graded ⭐ Brilliant (6/6), vs ~70% at one tag. Directionally right, but because single-tag
   coverage is already ~84% Brilliant late on, the reward is invisible in the half of the game that matters most.

2. THE RIVAL — FAIL (fixed the opening only). Turner genuinely led early: "▼ 5 behind" at turns 8–12, still
   "▼ 3 behind" at turn 13. After that the gap inverted and grew monotonically and never once closed:
   ▲ 20 by turn 28, ▲ 48 by turn 45, ▲ 87 by turn 54, ▲ 202 by turn 80. ~100 of the 120 turns had no contest
   at all. Filed as PT-1000.

3. STARTING TIER — PASS (with a caveat). The handoff put me in TIER 7 of 10 — "the Regional North/South",
   9th of 10 — not the Sunday League basement. The fix demonstrably scales. Caveat: this was a near-optimal
   career (71% Brilliant, rival 202 behind) and it still only bought tier 7, because the star graduated at
   OVR 11 and startingTierFor treats OVR<14 as a "weak graduate" and applies its -1 rung penalty. See PT-1002.

4. MATCH SCORELINES — PASS. Season 1 (18 games): 1-1 3-3 2-0 0-0 1-0 1-2 2-2 1-1 1-0 2-0 0-0 1-1 3-2 1-2 2-0
   3-1 0-1 1-2 = 10 distinct scorelines, max repeat 3. Season 2: 8 distinct, max repeat 6. Across 36 games,
   13 distinct scorelines; nothing like the old "one scoreline 14 times in 18". Residual narrowness filed as PT-1003.

5. STAR POSITION/OVR AT HANDOFF — POSITION PASS, OVR FAIL. Dane Ferreira, signed as a forward, arrived in the
   squad as "FW · Poacher" and auto-picked into a forward slot in the 4-4-2; the key-player line reads
   "★ Key player: Dane Ferreira (FW, OVR 11)". Position is correct. His OVR is the problem — PT-1002.

OVERALL VERDICT — better, but not yet a game with stakes. The first ~2 hours (turns 1–30) now feel like a real
game: grades vary, Solid is the honest baseline, and the rival is genuinely ahead of you and worth chasing.
That opening is a big improvement and it is NOT overcorrected — it never felt punishing. But every one of the
three tension systems (grade variance, rival, star development) flattens out at roughly the same point,
turn ~30, and the remaining 90 turns are the same no-stakes ride the previous playthrough reported: an
unbroken run of Brilliants against a rival 200 points behind. Two of the five fixes fixed the tutorial and
left the game. The manager side, by contrast, felt competitive from the first season (4th, then 6th, of 10,
with varied scorelines) — that half is in good shape.
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

- [x] **PT-1000 (stage: rebalance validation): [high] the rival rate raise only bought ~20 turns of tension — after that the gap grows monotonically and never closes again.** Playing a full 120-turn career and always answering the demanded tag, Turner genuinely led early ("you're ▼ 5 behind" at turns 8–12, ▼ 3 at turn 13) but the gap then inverted and ran away without a single reversal: ▲ 20 clear by turn 28, ▲ 48 by turn 45, ▲ 87 by turn 54, ▲ 202 by turn 80. Roughly 100 of the 120 turns had no contest whatsoever, and by turn 54 the "reel him in" framing is absurd. why it hurts: the rival is the game's only explicit opponent and the one thing giving a card turn stakes; if he is 200 behind by the halfway mark, the back two-thirds of a career is pure number-go-up with nothing to lose. fix: scale the rival's per-turn gain to the player's own recent gain (a rubber band / catch-up term) so he tracks a few points behind or ahead rather than accruing a flat rate, and cap the displayed gap so a runaway lead reads as "he's chasing you" rather than a blowout. Ref: rival progression in the career loop; the "🆚 RIVAL · <name> — you're ▲/▼ N" widget on the card screen.
- [x] **PT-1001 (stage: rebalance validation): [high] the grade-threshold retune fixed the first 30 turns and then decays — the back half of a career is still a near-guaranteed ⭐ Brilliant.** Across 100 graded turns of correct card play the overall split was 71 Brilliant / 22 Solid / 5 Unlucky / 1 Scrappy / 1 Poor, but it is not evenly spread: turns 1–30 graded 15 Brilliant / 11 Solid / 1 Unlucky / 1 Poor (54% Brilliant — a real, satisfying spread), while turns 55–120 graded 41 Brilliant / 4 Solid / 3 Unlucky / 1 Scrappy (84% Brilliant, essentially the pre-fix 89%). The demand normalisation evidently doesn't keep pace with the star's growing attributes, so the same correct play that graded Solid at age 11 grades Brilliant at 20. why it hurts: the spread is what makes a card turn feel like a decision, and it evaporates exactly when the stakes should be highest (senior football, big games, the run-up to the handoff) — the final 65 turns are a formality. fix: make the demand target scale with the star's current attribute level (normalise against his own OVR/attribute, not an absolute), or raise the Brilliant threshold as chapters advance so a senior pro needs a genuinely strong or double-tag play to earn one. Ref: demand normalisation + grade thresholds in the card-resolution path (shared/src engine); the "🎯 Right card / ⭐ Brilliant" outcome block.
- [x] **PT-1002 (stage: rebalance validation): [high] a near-optimal 120-turn career graduated the star at OVR 11 against a listed potential of 15, so the new career-scaled handoff still applies its "weak graduate" penalty to a career that could hardly have gone better.** He was FW OVR 10 with "pot 15 ★★★★★" at age 13 (turn 12); after 100 graded turns of which 71 were ⭐ Brilliant, the rival left 202 points behind and a career score in the 700–950 band, he arrived at the handoff at **OVR 11** — one point of growth across ten in-game years. `startingTierFor` (client/src/main.ts:1143-1149) scores `ov >= 16 ? 1 : ov >= 14 ? 0 : -1`, so this run took the `-1` weak-graduate rung and landed tier 7/10, 9th of 10. why it hurts: it silently breaks the reward the tier fix was written to deliver — the code's own bands say a good career should produce a 14–16 OVR graduate, and playing well produces 11, so nobody will ever see the top of the tier ladder and the whole 120-turn career reads as having barely developed the kid. fix: check the attribute-gain curve per graded moment against the 120-turn career length (a Brilliant should visibly move an attribute), and/or recalibrate `startingTierFor`'s OVR bands to the OVR range careers actually produce. Ref: client/src/main.ts:1143-1149 (`startingTierFor`) and the per-turn development/attribute-gain code in the shared engine.
- [x] **PT-1003 (stage: rebalance validation): [low-med] the seed-mixing fix worked but the scoreline distribution is still narrow — 42% of 36 simmed games were 2-0 or 2-2, and no side scored 4+ in any of them.** Simming two full seasons produced 13 distinct scorelines across 36 games (vastly better than the reported "one scoreline 14 times in 18"), but 2-0 appeared 8 times and 2-2 seven times; season 2 alone returned 2-2 six times and 2-0 five times in 18 games, and the highest single-team score in either season was 3. why it hurts: after a couple of seasons the fixture list starts to look samey again and there is no thrashing to remember — the emotional peaks of a league season (a 5-0, a 4-3) are absent from the distribution. why it isn't higher severity: the pathological repetition is genuinely fixed and the tables read plausibly. fix: widen the goals-per-team distribution (a longer tail on the scoring model) so blowouts occur at a realistic low rate. Ref: season sim / match result generation; the FIXTURES list on the club-season screen.
- [x] **PT-1004 (stage: rebalance validation): [low] the season summary's "Biggest win" ignores goals scored when margins tie — it reported "Biggest win 2-0" in a season containing a 3-1.** Season 1 finished with results including 3-1 (H, derby) and 3-2, yet the summary line read "📋 Biggest win 2-0 · Longest unbeaten 7". Both 2-0 and 3-1 are two-goal margins, but a 3-1 derby win is plainly the better story and the higher-scoring result. why it hurts: it is the one line the player reads to remember the season, and it picks the duller of two tied games — and after PT-1003 lands (wider scorelines) the tie-break will matter more often. fix: break margin ties on goals scored, then on opponent strength / derby flag. Ref: the "📋 Biggest win … · Longest unbeaten …" line on the club-season screen.

<!-- Late-game difficulty-decay audit 2026-08-29 (CODE/SIM only, no browser). Scale: 250 auto-played careers x 120 turns
     = 30,000 turns per policy, x2 policies (SKILLED = always the max-fit card in hand, coach/draft picked to match the
     deck's identity; MEDIOCRE = best-fit 45% of turns, else a random hand card, random coach/draft), plus a 300-career
     chapter-level run and a 200-career form-source run. Every term of career.ts:1325 was recorded BEFORE each play and
     averaged per 10-turn window. SKILLED decomposition (mean per term; det = f+form+bigGame+coaching-fatigue; var± is the
     nerve SPREAD, mean contribution 0):

       turns    f     var±   form   bigGame coaching fatigue   det    success  Bril%  Solid%  <0.4%  deck  coachMatch%  f>=0.99%
        0- 9  0.754  0.246  0.024   0.000   0.000    0.000    0.779   0.779    46.1   47.6    0.3    7.0     0.0        0.0
       10-19  0.774  0.246  0.106   0.000   0.041    0.000    0.922   0.888    78.6   19.3    0.0    9.6    37.0        6.6
       20-29  0.778  0.251  0.172   0.001   0.054    0.022    0.982   0.924    87.7   10.0    0.4   10.8    47.9       10.8
       30-39  0.757  0.271  0.191   0.005   0.058    0.065    0.946   0.893    80.2   15.3    1.5   13.4    51.0       10.7
       40-49  0.771  0.271  0.201   0.006   0.058    0.088    0.948   0.897    80.0   16.1    1.1   14.7    51.4       12.3
       50-59  0.773  0.271  0.377   0.005   0.060    0.113    1.101   0.952    91.3    7.0    0.4   16.7    52.0       12.9
       60-69  0.778  0.273  0.384   0.005   0.057    0.089    1.134   0.959    93.3    5.8    0.5   18.0    50.2       14.4
       70-79  0.782  0.280  0.291   0.004   0.060    0.112    1.025   0.931    87.4   10.5    0.8   19.9    53.6       16.6
       80-89  0.786  0.280  0.313   0.004   0.060    0.088    1.075   0.948    91.4    6.8    0.5   21.2    52.8       16.4
       90-99  0.794  0.276  0.283   0.005   0.062    0.111    1.034   0.932    87.9    9.8    0.7   23.2    54.3       18.1
      100-109 0.792  0.278  0.263   0.005   0.063    0.078    1.044   0.939    89.8    8.8    0.1   25.1    55.5       16.9
      110-119 0.791  0.277  0.282   0.005   0.064    0.116    1.026   0.930    86.6   11.3    0.2   26.4    56.9       18.4

     MEDIOCRE shows the SAME shape (Bril% 24 -> 37/40 -> 50 -> 38-43; form 0.013 -> 0.05 -> 0.19 -> 0.13), so the decay is
     a formula property, not a bot artefact. The bot is ~15pts hotter than the live human playthrough (54/65/84) at every
     stage, so read the SHAPE, not the absolute level; the predicted curves below carry the same offset.
     ONLY term that grows materially: form (+0.26 to +0.36). f grows +0.037, coaching is a one-off +0.06 step at turn 12,
     bigGame is +0.005, fatigue is the sole (weak) counterweight at -0.11. No proposal below adds or removes an rng draw. -->

- [x] **PT-1300 (audit: late-game difficulty decay): [critical] `form` is the term that inflates — mean applied form runs 0.024 (turns 0-9) → 0.191 (30-49) → 0.377/0.384 (50-69) → 0.28 (70-119), a +0.26-to-+0.36 permanent lift against a 0.80 Brilliant bar, while every other term is flat.** Over 250 skilled careers the deterministic sum `f+form+bigGame+coaching−fatigue` climbs 0.779 → 1.13 (it clamps out) purely on form; `f` moves 0.754 → 0.791 (+0.037), `coaching` is a single +0.06 step at turn 12, `bigGame` averages 0.005, and `fatigue` (−0.11 late) is the only thing pushing back. The decisive measurement: the share of turns where Brilliant is ARITHMETICALLY GUARANTEED (`det − variance/2 ≥ 0.80`, i.e. the worst possible nerve roll still grades ⭐) is 1.5% in turns 0-9 and 67.7-82.3% from turn 50 on — late-career nerve cannot reach the bar. why it hurts: from ~turn 50 the card loop stops being a decision and becomes a formality, which is exactly the 84%-Brilliant tail the live playthrough graded and the reason the PT-700 retune "worked early and decayed late". fix: clamp every write to `formBonus` into [−0.30, +0.15] and COOL positive form every played turn (`if (formBonus > 0) formBonus *= 0.85`) instead of only on `success < 0.45` (which fires on 0.2-0.8% of skilled turns). Simulated over the same 250 careers, skilled Brilliant% per window becomes 41/69/66/53/47/53/55/48/58/52/63/51 (mean 55 vs 83 today; turns 50-119 land 48-63 instead of 87-93) and mediocre 23/32/32/26/23/26/29/24/28/26/30/24 (mean 27 vs 39). Determinism-safe: magnitudes only, no change to rng draw count or order. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1319`, `shared/src/career.ts:1342`.
- [x] **PT-1301 (audit: late-game difficulty decay): [high] positive form is a one-way ratchet inside a chapter — story arcs pour form in with no cap and nothing takes it back out.** `formBonus` has no clamp anywhere; arcs add +1.132 form per career across 20.6 form-bearing beats (mean +0.045/beat early rising to +0.062/beat late; the arc banks hold 860 `form:` effects, modal +0.05, up to +0.14) and the between-chapter offer adds +0.39/career, while the only downward path for POSITIVE form is −0.03 on a sub-0.45 turn. Measured within-chapter drift (form at first play → form at last play, 300 careers): Grassroots +0.058, Academy +0.091, Scholar +0.014, then **Youth Team +0.353, Breakthrough +0.245, First Team +0.208, Establishing +0.170** — the second half of a career spends every chapter climbing. why it hurts: this is the mechanism behind PT-1300's numbers, and it also means the more STORY a player sees, the easier the game gets — the two systems fight each other. fix: cap the total arc+offer form a chapter may accumulate at +0.10 (track a per-chapter `formGained`, reset in `advanceSeasonEvent`), and halve the arc form magnitudes (modal +0.05 → +0.03, max +0.14 → +0.07). With PT-1300's cool-down this removes the +0.17-to-+0.35 late-chapter drift; alone it flattens skilled late windows to roughly 65-70% (still needs PT-1300). Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1249`, `shared/src/career.ts:1229`.
- [x] **PT-1302 (audit: late-game difficulty decay): [high] the chapter-START form baseline ratchets too, because the relationship consequences saturate and then pay out every chapter forever.** `computeConsequences` grants +0.06 for authority > 70 and +0.04 for peers > 70; measured over 300 careers, the count of those two meters above 70 at a chapter's first turn is 0.00 at Grassroots, 0.55 at Academy and **2.00 of 2 at Scholar and every chapter after** — the full +0.10 is banked automatically for the last five chapters. Chapter-start form therefore runs 0.000 / 0.087 / 0.166 / 0.174 / 0.180 / 0.174 / 0.159, i.e. a career never again opens a chapter neutral after turn 28. why it hurts: it converts a "your relationships paid off this year" beat into an unconditional late-career stipend, and it stacks under PT-1301's drift so late chapters start high AND climb. fix: scale the payout bar with the stage — thresholds 70 → 78 (and the penalty bar 32 → 38) from Breakthrough on, or halve the reward to +0.03/+0.02 there; measured effect is ≈ −0.05 mean form across turns 66-119, worth ~4pts of Brilliant on its own. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1442`, `shared/src/career.ts:1511`.
- [x] **PT-1303 (audit: late-game difficulty decay): [medium] `coaching` is not the growth driver people assume, but it is a free +0.06 that the deck learns to farm.** Mean coaching runs 0.000 (turns 0-11 — no coach exists until the first appointment at turn 12) → 0.041 → 0.054-0.064, and the specialty-MATCH rate climbs 0% → 37.0% → 56.9% as drafting into one identity makes the appointed coach's specialty ever more likely to be on the played card (deck 7 → 26 cards). So it contributes a permanent step, not a slope — but it also creates the sharp chapter-1-vs-rest cliff (turns 0-9 grade 46% Brilliant against 79% at 10-19, before any form has built). why it hurts: it is a reward for a decision the player already made once, paid on every subsequent turn, and it makes the opening chapter read as a different (harder) game than the one that follows. fix: coach bonuses 0.10-0.15 → 0.06-0.09 (`COACHES[]`, e.g. psych 0.15 → 0.09, fitness/gk/leadership 0.14 → 0.08, the 0.12 block → 0.07); simulated at 0.6× the current values this removes 5-8pts of Brilliant from every window past turn 30 and pulls the mean from 67 to 58 when stacked on PT-1300's cap. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1321`, `shared/src/career.ts:451`.
- [x] **PT-1304 (audit: late-game difficulty decay): [medium] nerve and the ask stay still while the player's floor rises — variance grows +13% over a career against a +0.27 growth in the deterministic sum, and a maturing deck answers ever more moments perfectly.** Mean nerve spread is 0.246 in turns 0-9 and only 0.277-0.280 in turns 70-119 (it moves at all only because stakes creep 1.00 → 1.21), so the random term becomes decorative exactly when it is needed most; meanwhile the share of plays achieving a MAXIMAL fit (`f ≥ 0.99`, i.e. covering two demanded tags) grows 0.0% → 18.4% as the deck goes 7 → 26 cards, even though mean `f` barely moves. why it hurts: the two levers designed to keep the loop honest — nerve and the demand — are the only things that do not scale with the player, so a late-career deck faces a turn-1 test. fix: chapter-scale both — `variance = (0.26 + 0.02*chapterIdx + 0.16*(stakes-1)) * personality.variance` (0.26 → 0.38 by Establishing) and `TOP_DEMAND = 0.78 - 0.015*chapterIdx` (0.78 → 0.69, so late moments need two tags covered, not one). Measured separately on top of PT-1300's cap: the variance slope removes ~3pts per late window, the demand slope ~10pts by turn 119; together with PT-1300 + PT-1303 the skilled curve simulates to 45/67/68/54/45/40/45/37/43/36/42/33, so apply them at HALF these slopes if the target floor is 45%. Both are magnitude-only — no rng draw-count change. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1317`, `shared/src/career.ts:850`. **[Closed on measurement: the per-quarter Brilliant curve is 16/18/21/18 — a 5-point spread across a career, now guarded at 18 in analyze_player_career. Nerve staying flat is only a defect if difficulty decays, and it does not.]**

<!-- ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     Browser difficulty-validation playtest 2026-08-29 (fresh new game "Kestrel",
     signed Ravi Kestrel FW ★4, Super-Agent, skilled play = always play the card
     whose tag matches the green demanded tag). Played from turn 1 to the forced
     manager handoff. NOTE: the dev server was rebuilding continuously during the
     run (client/src/main.ts, shared/src/career.ts, shared/src/tokens.ts all
     touched mid-session), so the page full-reloaded to the main menu every
     ~20-40s; the save always resumed correctly and no progress was lost, but
     ~10 of the ~106 card turns went unlogged. n = 93 graded card plays.

     PER-PHASE GRADE DISTRIBUTION (skilled play, "Right card" on 90 of 93 turns):
       turns   1-30  n=37   ⭐41%  ✓43%  ◦16% (4 Unlucky, 2 Poor)
       turns  31-60  n=26   ⭐54%  ✓35%  ◦12% (3 Unlucky)
       turns  61-90  n=20   ⭐50%  ✓40%  ◦10% (2 Unlucky)
       turns 91-106  n=10   ⭐50%  ✓20%  ◦30% (2 Unlucky, 1 Scrappy)
       career total  n=93   ⭐47%  ✓38%  ◦15%
     By chapter: Grassroots 24% ⭐ · Academy 53% · Scholar 29% · Youth Team 80%
     (a "Purple Patch" form streak) · Breakthrough 50% · First Team 53%.

     VERDICTS ON THE FIVE QUESTIONS
     1. Is difficulty flat?              PASS. 41/54/50/50 across the four
        phases. The 54%→84% decay is gone; turn 100 is no easier than turn 40.
     2. Is it FUN?                       MIXED / PARTIAL FAIL. The story arcs,
        summer screen, drafts and the manager handoff are genuinely good. The
        card loop itself is not a decision (see PT-1407) and the rival stops
        mattering by turn 12 (PT-1408), so the flat difficulty is flat at a
        level where nothing is ever at stake.
     3. Is it overcorrected?             NO. Brilliant is reachable at ~50% for
        the whole career and Solid is never the ceiling. If anything the ⭐ bar
        is still slightly generous — half of all correct plays are Brilliant, so
        Brilliant reads as "the normal result" rather than a reward.
     4. Commentary varied?               FAIL — see PT-1400/PT-1401. 679 lines in
        one 90-minute match, three sentences firing 52/51/49 times.
     5. Age-appropriateness?             FAIL on three counts — PT-1402 (driving
        lessons sold to a 15-year-old), PT-1403 (a 19-year-old's partner is 15
        years into a surgical career), PT-1404 (a veteran-twilight arc, number
        retirement and "a decade has passed" fired at age 21).

     PLAIN VERDICT: the difficulty fix worked and did not overcorrect. The game
     is not yet fun in the moment-to-moment loop — it is fun in the chapters
     around it. The remaining fun problem is decision density, not difficulty.
     ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

- [x] **PT-1400 (stage: difficulty validation): [high] the match-commentary repetition fix FAILED — one `cpick` bank of three lines still fires ~152 times in a single match.** Watched a full live match in the default 🎙️ Full mode (Kestrel's Club 0-3 Ilfordby United) and captured all 679 feed lines: after normalising player/team names the top three templates were "⚡ {team} spring the press — {p} robs him in the final third!" ×52, "⚡ Won high up! {p} presses and steals it for {team} — dangerous!" ×51 and "⚡ High turnover! {p} nicks it right on the edge of the box!" ×49 — 152 of 679 lines (22%) from three sentences, with an exact duplicate already visible at the 34-second mark. Only 186 distinct templates carried 679 lines. why it hurts: the previous pass took one phrase from ~190 to ~52 but left the bank at three entries against the game's second-heaviest event, so the headline symptom (the feed visibly looping) is unchanged for a first-time viewer. fix: the `tackle_won` + `zone==='att'` branch at `client/src/main.ts:3816` needs the same ~12-line treatment the other banks got (it currently has 3 against `pass`'s post-PT-1201 bank), and the whole feed needs a per-match cap on how often any one template may repeat before its bank is forcibly rotated. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:3816` (pressing-turnover bank), live match ticker.
- [x] **PT-1401 (stage: difficulty validation): [medium] the player epithet is attached to nearly every name in the commentary and breaks sentence capitalisation when it lands first.** "lightning-quick" appeared in 287 of 679 lines (42%) of one match, and "in the final third" in 364 (54%) — `descriptor()` grants an epithet to any player whose top attribute is ≥14, which in a normal squad is most of them, and it always picks the single highest stat so the same player is "the lightning-quick Timo Santos" every time he touches the ball. Because the epithet begins with a lowercase "the", lines read "🧤 Beaten away! the lightning-quick Timo Santos is foiled!" and "⚡ Won high up! the lightning-quick Timo Santos presses…". why it hurts: two words carry 42% of the match's prose, which makes an already-repetitive feed read as a template dump, and the mid-sentence lowercase after "!" looks like a straight string bug. fix: only apply an epithet on a fraction of that player's mentions (e.g. every 4th, seeded), rotate among his top TWO attributes, and capitalise when the substitution lands at the start of a clause. Also vary `zoneWord('att')` — "in the final third" is currently the only phrase for the busiest zone. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:3735-3740` (`descriptor`), `client/src/main.ts:3733` (`zoneWord`).
- [x] **PT-1402 (stage: difficulty validation): [medium] the age-gating pass missed the summer SPEND tiles — "🚦 Driving Lessons" is offered to a 15-year-old.** At age 15 / Scholar (turn 28) the "💷 Spend your earnings" step listed "🚦 Driving Lessons — Freedom on the open road is finally within reach. 💷 420c 👥+5 ❤️+4" alongside Nicer Digs, Wine & Dine Your Agent and A Private Tutor; the same tile was still on the board at 17, where it belongs. 28 senior arcs were age-gated off minors in the last pass (driving at 15 explicitly named) but the parallel spend-tile bank was not gated. why it hurts: it is the exact issue the gating pass was meant to close, on a screen every player sees every single summer, and it undercuts the child/senior tone split the rest of the game now maintains carefully. fix: apply the same chapter/age gate the arcs use to the spend-tile bank — Driving Lessons and Your First Car from 17, Your Own Place / Designer Wardrobe from 18. Ref: summer Step-1 spend screen, spend-tile bank (`client/src/main.ts`).
- [x] **PT-1403 (stage: difficulty validation): [medium] the "📅 TWO CAREERS, ONE CALENDAR" relationship arc fires at 19 with a partner who is demonstrably twice his age.** At age 19 the arc opened "She's a surgeon on the brink of a post she's worked fifteen years for — in a city eight hundred miles from his club", and resolved with "neither can be the moon to the other's planet forever". Fifteen years into a surgical career puts her at roughly 40 against a 19-year-old. why it hurts: this is the same class of defect as PT-145 (hardcoded "twenty years of resentment") — an authored number that the arc's own firing window makes impossible, and it lands in the most-read line of a relationship arc in a game whose pitch is a coherent personal story. fix: either raise the arc's minimum chapter to Establishing, or replace the hardcoded fifteen years with an age-derived profession ("a junior doctor two years into a rotation") so the partner scales with him. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/relationship.ts` (two-careers arc).
- [x] **PT-1404 (stage: difficulty validation): [medium] the "🏡 THE RETURN" veteran-twilight arc fires at age 21, complete with a decade of hindsight and a retired shirt number.** All three beats landed on a 21-year-old: "comes calling in the autumn of his career"; "His second debut, back in front of the terrace that once chanted him as a teenager. But a decade has passed, the legs are slower…"; "the club offers to retire his number, hang his shirt in the concourse, name him something more than a player." He was chanted as a teenager two years earlier, and the arc ends his career symbolically eleven turns before the game itself hands him to the manager. why it hurts: it is the emotional payoff arc of a whole career being spent on someone who has not had one yet, and the "a decade has passed" line is verifiably false in the save that printed it. fix: gate THE RETURN behind the Establishing/Veteran chapter (or an age ≥ 29 check) the way the senior arcs now are, and derive the elapsed span from `age - debutAge`. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/` (THE RETURN arc), observed at turn 90.
- [ ] **PT-1405 (stage: difficulty validation): [low-med] PT-144's second half is unfixed — the stale "cannot buy that" error follows the player across every subsequent screen, including the manager handoff.** After a failed spend on a summer screen the terse "cannot buy that" line was still rendered at the bottom of the page many turns later, and was still there on the "🏆 Ravi Kestrel — a first-team regular / 🧢 TAKE THE REINS AS MANAGER" milestone screen — the single most ceremonial screen in the player career. PT-144's affordability-gate half is fixed (locked tiles now show "🔒 need 380c more" and drop out of the tab order, and "Back the Club" is correctly styled as an invest tile), but the error line is never cleared on screen transition. why it hurts: an error string from a summer screen hours earlier sits under the game's biggest celebration beat, which reads as a rendering bug on the one screen that should feel like a reward. fix: clear the transient error string in the screen-transition path, not just on the next spend attempt. Ref: `client/src/main.ts`, milestone/handoff screen.
- [ ] **PT-1406 (stage: difficulty validation): [low-med] the player career is seized into manager mode at age 22 / ~turn 106, while both the progress bar and the Academy copy promise age 10→25 and /120.** The header counted "/120" all career (last logged card turn 103) and the Academy screen states "young players to develop through a full career (age 10→25) … At 25 they graduate into a pro for your squad", but the "first-team regular" milestone fired at age 22 with a forced "🧢 TAKE THE REINS AS MANAGER →" and no alternative; afterwards the Academy read "No prospects yet" and the hub showed Ravi at 23 in the manager squad. So a player is told he has 120 turns and 15 years and gets ~106 turns and 12 years. why it hurts: the whole card career is framed by that bar, and being cut off ~14 turns early with no warning turns the intended reward (becoming the manager) into something that was taken rather than earned. fix: either let the card career run to the advertised 120/age 25 with the handoff as an offer rather than a gate, or change the bar and the Academy copy to state the real end condition ("until he's a first-team regular"). Ref: Academy screen copy, career progress bar, milestone handoff.
- [x] **PT-1407 (stage: difficulty validation): [medium] difficulty is now flat, but the card loop has no decision in it — a single mechanical rule scored "🎯 Right card" on 90 of 93 turns without ever reading the fiction.** I played the whole career with one rule: play the card whose tag matches the green demanded tag. That produced 90 "Right card" and 3 "Partial match" (the 3 only where no dealt card carried the demanded tag), and 47% Brilliant / 15% failure, flat across all four phases. The demanded tag is printed in plain text ("🎯 This calls for: creativity"), each card prints its own tag, and the four dealt cards almost always carry four different tags — so the correct answer is visually unambiguous on essentially every turn and the outcome then rides entirely on a hidden nerve roll. why it hurts: this is the fun problem the difficulty fix has now exposed. A Brilliant does not feel earned because no judgement produced it, and an Unlucky does not sting because nothing could have prevented it — the tension the flat curve was meant to protect has nowhere to live. The real decisions (summer focus, drafts, coach, arcs) are good and are already carrying the game alone. fix: make the moment cost something — e.g. deal cards with an energy/risk price so the perfect fit is sometimes the wrong call, hide one demanded tag until after the play, or let two cards match with a genuine trade-off (a safe Solid vs a boom-or-bust Brilliant/Poor). Ref: card moment screen (`client/src/main.ts`), demand/`f` fit term in `shared/src/career.ts`.
- [x] **PT-1408 (stage: difficulty validation): [low-med] the rival stops being a rival by the second chapter and never threatens again.** The rival panel read "🆚 RIVAL · Turner — you're ▲ 0 clear" at turn 1, "▲ 5" at turn 3, "▲ 19" at turn 12 and "▲ 64" at turn 28, and the gap only widened for the rest of the career; I was never behind after roughly turn 6. Meanwhile his news ticker kept insisting he was thriving ("Turner is being talked about as the standout of his year group", "Turner has been handed a longer academy deal") while the number said he was 64 behind. why it hurts: the tutorial explicitly sells the rival as the thing that gives progress something to chase ("Out-develop him to pull ahead"), and by age 13 he is a decoration — one of the two systems that could supply late-career tension is inert, which compounds PT-1407. fix: make the rival track the player (rubber-band his development toward the player's score with a lag) so the gap oscillates, and gate the "he's thriving" headlines behind him actually being close or ahead. Ref: `cg-rival` panel, rival simulation in `shared/src/career.ts`.
- [x] **PT-1409 (stage: difficulty validation): [low] the live match clock only advances while the tab is actually painting, so a match left in a background tab freezes mid-game.** With the browser pane backgrounded the clock sat at 00:18 through 35 seconds of wall time and at 00:36 through another 40; it resumed as soon as the tab was fronted or a screenshot forced a paint, and "SKIP ▶▶" jumped straight to 90:00 correctly. The match sim is evidently driven off requestAnimationFrame, which browsers throttle to zero for hidden tabs. why it hurts: a player who tabs away mid-match to check something comes back to a match that has not moved, which reads as a hang — and it makes the 1X/4X/12X speeds meaningless as a promise about elapsed time. fix: drive the match clock off a wall-clock delta (or setInterval with a timestamp check) rather than raw rAF ticks, so a hidden tab catches up on the next frame. Ref: match engine tick loop, `client/src/main.ts`.

## Fixed (validated by the harness)

- [x] **PT-2 — top-flight titles too rare (~5%/season).** Top-tier transfer market now offers title-contender
  signings (headroom +3 at tiers 1–2) so an investing dynasty can build a squad that exceeds the league — an
  earned, coin-gated reward. Probe: title rate **5% → 18%**. `shared/src/transfermarket.ts`.
- [x] **PT-1 — ~24% of turns were a "bad hand"** (no fair-fit card, forced weak play). `ensurePlayableHand()`
  now swaps in a fair-fit card from the deck when the draw is dead; a dead hand only remains when the DECK
  itself can't answer (a deck-building signal). Probe: bad-hand **24% → 2%**, skilled Solid+ **71% → 94%**.
  `shared/src/career.ts`.
- [x] Core card loop: skilled play rewarded (Solid+ 71→94% vs random ~33%) — demand normalized by max not
  sum + variance cut. (was: perfect play routinely graded "Poor")
- [x] Rival beatable by good play (100% of skilled careers) after rate 6–9 → 3–5/turn. (was: impossible)
- [x] **PT-82 [FIXED] (audit: manager first-season-2): [medium] full-time match report is competition- and venue-blind with 3-phrase scoreline pools, so a cup final reads like a Tuesday league game.** `renderMatchReport` (client/src/main.ts:2996-3026, `lead` at :3007-3014) picks the headline purely from the goal-margin band (3 variants each for draw/1/2/3+), never referencing `spFixture.comp`/round or venue; every played match routes through it (:3061). why it hurts: for a text-first game the marquee post-match prose reads identical across a season and gives a cup final no more weight than a mid-table draw. fix: pass comp/round + venue into renderMatchReport and add occasion-aware lead lines + widen the pools (cf. PT-57/71). Ref: client/src/main.ts:2996.
- [x] **PT-83 [FIXED] (audit: manager first-season-2): [medium] league-table tiebreak is goals-for then club-name alphabetical, but GF isn't shown — promotion can be decided invisibly or alphabetically.** `liveTable` sorts `Pts || GD || GF || name.localeCompare` (shared/src/clubseason.ts:168) while `spTableHtml` renders only P/W/D/L/GD/Pts (main.ts:1139/1143 — no GF/GA). Since promotion is pure top-2 position, a hidden GF or alphabetical order can decide the final promotion place. why it hurts: the player can be denied promotion on a tiebreak they can't see (or on alphabetical order rather than head-to-head), reading as a bug at the season's biggest moment. fix: show a GF column and use head-to-head as the deep tiebreak (not localeCompare). Ref: shared/src/clubseason.ts:168, client/src/main.ts:1139.
- [x] **PT-84 [FIXED] (audit: manager first-season-2): [low-med] any formation change silently wipes every manager-assigned duty.** The formation change handler calls `rebuildDuties()` (main.ts:2765) which resets all slots to `defaultDuty` (:2896), discarding hand-set duties with no warning (contrast the SP swap which resets only one slot, :2833). why it hurts: a manager who carefully sets duties then tweaks 4-4-2→4-3-3 loses the whole setup unknowingly. fix: preserve each retained player's current duty across a formation change (reset only slots whose player changed), or warn first. Ref: client/src/main.ts:2765.
- [x] **PT-85 [FIXED] (audit: manager first-season-2): [low-med] the lineup editor lets you field an invalid XI (no GK / players out of position) with no validation or warning.** Every slot's dropdown lists all players regardless of role (main.ts:2800-2803, no role filter), `validateLineup` checks only "11 distinct owned" (shared/src/game.ts:31-36), and `buildXI` drops each player onto the slot keeping his own role (teams.ts:148-151) with no penalty. why it hurts: a naive manager can kick off with no keeper or strikers at CB and get zero signal, and the slot role label then contradicts the player there. fix: warn (or block) when a slot player's role mismatches the slot / when no GK is fielded, and flag out-of-position picks. Ref: client/src/main.ts:2800.
- [x] **PT-86 [FIXED] (audit: manager promotion/relegation-2): [high] the pyramid climb is invisible to manager prestige — a Sunday-League title scores identical to a top-flight one.** The offline `prestige()` facade hardcodes `highestTierIdx = 0` and every honour's `tierIdx = 0` (client/src/api.ts:617-618), so both tier-reward paths in `prestigeScore` are dead: `tierMult = 1 + h.tierIdx*0.5` (shared/src/prestige.ts:39) is always 1 and `highestTierIdx*40` (prestige.ts:43) always 0. why it hurts: the entire multi-season climb — the core of this arc — never registers in the dynasty's legacy or reputation. fix: thread the real tier through spSeasonReward.addHonour and into the prestige facade (`tierIdx = TIERS - clubTier`, `highestTierIdx = max seen`) so the climb weights titles and lifts the rank. Ref: client/src/api.ts:617, shared/src/prestige.ts:39.
- [x] **PT-87 [FIXED] (audit: manager promotion/relegation-2): [medium] board expectations never rise as you climb the pyramid.** `deriveExpectation` keys off `prestige.levelIdx` (client/src/main.ts:1645), but because prestige is tier-blind (PT-86) a manager in the top flight carries the same prestige level (and expectation baseline) as one grinding equal titles in the Sunday League. why it hurts: reaching a higher tier doesn't raise the standard the board holds you to, so the difficulty/expectation curve is flat across the whole climb and promotion's reward is undercut. fix: fold current tier into the expectation input (or fix PT-86 so levelIdx reflects the climb). Ref: client/src/main.ts:1645.
- [x] **PT-88 [FIXED] (audit: manager promotion/relegation-2): [low] the promotion/relegation reveal banner is one fixed sentence, identical at every tier and on every repeat move, and the relegation variant says "welcome to".** main.ts:1264 emits the same "…reward for the climb" / "…climb starts again" string each time, and the relegation branch opens "welcome to <lower division>" before calling it "a setback." why it hurts: over a 3-4 season climb the marquee reveal reads word-for-word the same each promotion, and celebratory "welcome to" framing clashes with a demotion. fix: vary the banner by tier/direction (seed a small line bank off tier+season) and reword the relegation copy. Ref: client/src/main.ts:1264.
- [x] **PT-89 [FIXED] (audit: manager promotion/relegation-2): [low] the season-complete header flags promotion (PT-76) but never relegation.** The `done` summary (main.ts:1254) appends "⬆️ PROMOTED to X!" for pos≤2/tier>1 but has no relegation branch, so a just-relegated club's "finished 9th" line is indistinguishable from a safe mid-table finish; the drop is only acknowledged on the next season's banner. why it hurts: the fall lands with no in-context weight at the moment it happens — an asymmetry with the PT-76 promotion flourish. fix: add a "⬇️ RELEGATED to X" branch to the done header mirroring the promotion one. Ref: client/src/main.ts:1254.
- [x] **PT-90 (audit: manager transfers-2): [high] bought squad players never age, decline, or carry a contract — the squad can't rot and the SELL column has no purpose.** Only `m.starId` is developed/aged each rollover (client/src/main.ts:1654 in nextSeason); `api.buyPlayer` pushes the signing into `club.players` with a fresh id and no token/contract/age (client/src/api.ts:307-308), and nothing decays it. why it hurts: the market's promise "buy/sell to strengthen over the seasons" (transfermarket.ts:2, main.ts:1178/1215) is false — buys are permanent wage-free non-decaying power, and SELL can never offload "deadwood" because no bought player becomes deadwood. fix: age & decline non-star squad players each season (mirror the star's developPlayer decline) so overall/sellValue fall and they must be churned. Ref: client/src/api.ts:307, client/src/main.ts:1654.
- [x] **PT-91 [FIXED] (audit: manager transfers-2): [medium] the star's "contract lapsed — benched" rule is toothless — starGuarded force-fields a lapsed star, contradicting its own UI.** `availableClub()` excludes a lapsed star, but `starGuarded` (main.ts:1088-1102) swaps him back from the full squad and only bails for INJURY (PT-73), never a lapse; it runs on every auto-pick/formation change (main.ts:696/2241/2808), while the card still prints "⛔ contract lapsed — benched" (main.ts:745). why it hurts: re-signing is never actually required to play him, so the wage×length negotiation is an optional cosmetic coin sink and the "benched" copy is a visible lie. fix: make starGuarded respect the lapse (leave him out, like injury) so re-signing has teeth, or auto-skip the lapse and delete the "benched" copy — pick one so mechanic + UI agree. Ref: client/src/main.ts:1088.
- [x] **PT-92 (audit: manager transfers-2): [medium] the retention-wage economy is inverted — the wage×length negotiation targets only the one player you CAN'T sell, while the whole bought squad is retained free.** `starContractInfo`/`negotiateStar` gate on a `state==='pro'` token (api.ts:333/346) which exists only for the bloodline star/heirs; bought players have no token (api.ts:308) so never incur a wage or renewal. why it hurts: the expensive recurring-retention decision applies to exactly the wrong (unsellable, dynasty-locked) player and never to the 15+ purchased players it should. fix: attach a wage/contract to bought players too (a recurring, lapsing cost feeding the same negotiation), or drop the star-only wage so retention isn't concentrated on the one asset you can't move. Ref: client/src/api.ts:333.
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-90/PT-92) — fixed by THE LIVING SQUAD (Phase 2). Squad players now carry an age and a real contract: signings keep the age the listing advertised and sign for SQUAD_CONTRACT_SEASONS from the current season (api.buyPlayer -> signSquadContract). New shared/src/squad.ts rolls the whole squad forward each season (advanceSquad, called from nextSeason via api.advanceSquadSeason): under-30s DEVELOP on the star's own developAttrs curve (better Training Ground = faster growth), 30 is a plateau, 31+ DECLINE via ageSquadAttrs (pace/stamina fade fastest), and players retire at a durability-derived 34-37. A per-season WAGE BILL is charged for the whole squad (squadSeasonWage), expiring deals force a renew (squadRenewCost, wage x length) or let-him-walk decision, and the sell column now uses the age-aware squadSaleValue so a fading veteran is genuinely worth less. This closes both the 'squad can't rot / SELL has no purpose' gap (PT-90) and the 'retention wage targets only the unsellable star' inversion (PT-92) — the whole squad now costs money to hold. Surfaced in a new season-screen report (improved / fading / retired / deal-up + the wage bill). New shared/qa_squad_lifecycle.ts (28 checks incl. determinism, no-mutation, and 'an unrefreshed squad decays 15.3 -> 12.7') added to npm run verify. -->
- [x] **PT-93 [FIXED] (audit: manager transfers-2): [low] transfer/bid prose is fixed-template across a window, and "Sign/Signed … −{fee}c" implies a wage that never exists.** The incoming-bid banner is one hardcoded sentence reused verbatim (main.ts:1261); the buy confirm/toast say "Sign him"/"Signed … −{fee}c" (main.ts:1185/1193) quoting only a one-off fee — no wage ever — while the star modal talks in c/season (main.ts:912). why it hurts: over a multi-season dynasty the bid drama reads as canned repetition, and "signing" a player for a lump sum with zero wage reads nonsensical beside the star's per-season framing. fix: vary the bid-banner copy and label buys as a one-off "transfer fee (no wage)" so the two systems read consistently. Ref: client/src/main.ts:1261.
- [x] **PT-94 [FIXED] (audit: manager continental-2): [high] cup prizes are booked through the LEAGUE reward endpoint, corrupting the honours ledger + the season counter.** `resolveContinental` (client/src/main.ts:1415), `concludeWorldCup` (main.ts:1534) and the WC group-exit (main.ts:1475) all call `api.spSeasonReward(...)`, which hardwires `addHonour(..., 'league')` and unconditionally runs `model.profile.season = season + 1` (api.ts:475/479). So every continental/WC win: (a) shows as a LEAGUE trophy in the Trophy Room, (b) counts as a league title toward prestige (pos===1→title:1), and (c) skips profile.season forward MID-season, so the real season's later league honour lands under a different season_number and the distinct-season/longevity count inflates. why it hurts: a European campaign is misattributed as a league championship and silently pads prestige/longevity. fix: give cups a dedicated reward call that banks coins with kind:'continental'/'world' and does NOT advance profile.season (a noSeasonBump/kind param on spSeasonReward). Ref: client/src/main.ts:1415, client/src/api.ts:475.
- [x] **PT-95 [FIXED] (audit: manager continental-2): [medium] an in-progress World Finals knockout run is silently forfeited by "Next season".** The PT-75 guard (main.ts:1307) only checks `contElig && !contOut && contRound<3`; it never checks `m.wcStage`, and nextSeason blindly resets wcStage/wcEdition/wcRun (main.ts:1677). Since "done" is league-fixtures-only, you can finish the league on a WC-edition season and roll over with QF/SF/Final unplayed. why it hurts: the exact silent-drop PT-75 closed for the continental strand is still open for the World Finals. fix: extend the sf-next-season guard to also confirm when `m.wcStage && m.wcStage !== 'done'` (or auto-sim the outstanding WC ties). Ref: client/src/main.ts:1307/1677.
- [x] **PT-96 [FIXED] (audit: manager continental-2): [medium] the flagship continental trophy is economically trivial and back-loaded.** Winning the Final pays a single spSeasonReward({pos:1,size:10}) = 800c (main.ts:1415), identical to a domestic league title, while QF and SF wins pay 0c. why it hurts: three knockout wins against the best clubs on the continent yield the same 800c as topping a 10-team league (and less than a league win with a performance sponsor) — the marquee European run is a coin no-op relative to its effort. fix: pay escalating per-round prizes (QF/SF/Final) and set the title payout at a clear premium over a league championship. Ref: client/src/main.ts:1415.
- [x] **PT-97 [FIXED] (audit: manager continental-2): [low] the continental "how it felt" line is scoreline- and opponent-blind.** `contTieBlurb`'s pick hash `hash32(seed, season*977+41, round*131, 8801)` (shared/src/intl.ts:225) omits both the goals and the opponent, so a 4-0 rout and a 1-0 squeak in the same season/round draw the identical sentence, and the line never names who was beaten. why it hurts: over a dynasty the same round always narrates the same generic line regardless of the tie, undercutting the "every match is a story" hook at the continental peak. fix: fold the margin + the opponent name (contOpponent) into the blurb seed/text. Ref: shared/src/intl.ts:225.

<!-- Code audit 2026-08-29 (manager first-season stage, rotation 22) — read the handoff→first-managed-season path: renderHandoff/cg-takereins (main.ts:2299), showSeason + managerHelpCard + starLine + board line (main.ts:1205-1331), openLineup/renderLineupEditor incl. starGuarded/duty preservation/invalid-XI warn (main.ts:2826-3009), teamTalk + startSpMatchWith kickoff (main.ts:1875-1954), renderMatchReport scorers/assists/POTM/star spotlight (main.ts:3153-3216), nextSeason board verdict + promo/releg + star ageing (main.ts:1667-1713), expectationLabel tier clamps (main.ts:1731-1744), simFixtureResult/simRemainingFixtures (main.ts:1648-1663), and shared teams.ts/clubseason.ts/contracts.ts/engine.ts. VERIFIED-HELD: PT-17/20 starGuarded force-includes the star on every build path AND re-guards at the kickoff chokepoint (main.ts:1090,1921) while correctly declining to force an injured/lapsed star; PT-21/74 star season line + goals-AND-assists FT spotlight + POTM tie-break toward the star; PT-23 one-time managerHelpCard; PT-64/65/66/67 expectationLabel clamps title/promotion/survival at tier1 and the basement; PT-82/83/84/85 occasion leads, GF tiebreak column, formation-change duty preservation, invalid-XI warn; PT-33/63 haggle presets. Three genuinely-new findings below (PT-117..119), deduped against PT-1..116 + M1–M9. -->
- [x] **PT-117 (stage: manager first-season audit): [medium] the full-time report keys scorers, assists and Player of the Match by player NAME only, so any two players who share a name (common — both squads draw from the same 18×18 name pool) are merged into one entry on the wrong side.** `renderMatchReport` builds `goalsBy` (`client/src/main.ts:3156-3160`), `assists` (`:3188-3190`) and `contrib`/POTM (`:3194-3214`) all keyed on `e.playerName`/`e.playerName2` strings with no team qualifier; `bump()` even overwrites `e.team = team` on each hit (`:3195`), so if a home scorer and an away scorer are both e.g. "Jan Berg" their goals combine under a single scorer line attributed to whichever scored last, and their pooled contribution points can hand POTM to a merged phantom. Opponent squads are generated from the identical `FIRST`/`LAST` arrays (`shared/src/teams.ts:6-7`, `generateClub`), and with ~40 names on the pitch drawn from 324 combinations a namesake is likely most matches (even two of the manager's own teammates can collide). why it hurts: on a text-first game the marquee post-match summary can silently misattribute goals to the wrong club, double-count a scorer, or crown a POTM that doesn't exist — an immersion break at the one screen the manager reads every matchday. fix: key `goalsBy`/`assists`/`contrib` by a `teamIdx + '|' + name` composite (or by player id if events can carry one) and render the name from that; determinism-safe (no rng, no draw-count change — pure map keying). Ref: `client/src/main.ts:3156-3214`.
- [x] **PT-118 (stage: manager first-season audit): [low-med] "Sim the rest of the season" discards every match-strength edge a PLAYED match applies (home/away venue, team talk, training-ground & fan-zone facilities, backroom staff), so investing in the club is worthless the moment you sim.** `simFixtureResult` derives a scoreline purely from `clubLeagueStrength` vs `opp.strength` with a flat `+0.25` home term baked in for EVERY fixture (`client/src/main.ts:1648-1652`), and `simRemainingFixtures` never reads `fixtures[i].venue` (`:1653-1660`) — so away games are simmed as if at home. Meanwhile a played match layers venue-gated home boost, the team-talk tone, training-ground conditioning, fan-zone home edge and hired staff onto the XI (`startSpMatchWith` `:1938-1947`), none of which feed `clubLeagueStrength` (= squad avg + age mod only, `:1108-1119`). The season help card actively pushes "⏩ Sim to jump ahead" (`:1210`) and staff are sold as "less fitness drain / home attack edge" (`staffHtml`/BACKROOM_STAFF), yet a simmed season ignores all of it and gives every away game an unearned home bias. why it hurts: a manager who hires staff, upgrades facilities and picks team talks then sims (the game's own recommended shortcut for the multi-season climb) gets none of that value and a systematically distorted table — the club-building economy silently no-ops. fix: fold the facility/staff edges into a strength delta and pass `fixtures[i].venue` into `simFixtureResult` so the `+0.25` flips for away games; determinism-safe (still pure hash, no rng-draw change). Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1648-1663,1938-1947`.
- [x] **PT-119 (stage: manager first-season audit): [low-med] the pre-match team talk (note + personality modulation) is hardwired to the bloodline star even when he is injured or contract-lapsed and therefore NOT in the XI.** `teamTalkNote()` reads `this.starPlayer()` unconditionally (`client/src/main.ts:1888-1895`) and `startSpMatchWith` applies `TALK_SENSITIVE`/`TALK_FIERY` tone modulation from `(this.starPlayer() as any).personality` to the team's `homeBoost`/`conditioning` (`:1927-1938`) — but the guarded XI deliberately excludes the star when injured or lapsed (`starGuarded` `:1090`, the PT-73/PT-91 rule). So in exactly that anticipated state the dressing-room overlay still says "★ NAME thrives on a rev-up before kickoff" for a man who isn't playing, and his personality still swings the whole side's pre-kickoff edge though he's on the treatment table / benched. why it hurts: contradictory UI (a team talk about an absent player) plus a phantom mechanical edge tied to someone not on the pitch — an immersion + fairness break during injury/contract spells the code otherwise handles carefully. fix: source the team-talk note and modulation from the designated captain (or the on-pitch leader) when the star is unavailable — reuse the same `injured.has(starId) || lapsed().has(starId)` guard `starGuarded` already computes; determinism-safe (bounded edge, no rng-draw change). Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1888-1895,1927-1938,1090`.

PT-118 — simFixtureResult gained a per-fixture `homeTerm` (default 0.25 kept for the neutral continental/WC callers), and a new `simEdge(venue)` folds training-ground + backroom staff into a strength delta (both venues) and the fan-zone + venue edge into homeTerm (home only); simRemainingFixtures reads fixtures[i].venue so away games lose the unearned home bias and club investment finally counts when simming. PT-119 — new `talkFocus()` pitches the team talk at the bloodline star only when he's actually in the XI (reusing the injured/lapsed guard), else the on-pitch captain / best starter; teamTalkNote + the startSpMatchWith tone modulation both route through it, so a benched star no longer gets praised or swings the side's edge. All determinism-safe (pure map keying / pure-hash sim / bounded edge — no rng-draw-count change). verify green. -->


<!-- Code audit 2026-08-29 (manager promotion/relegation stage, rotation 23) — deep-read the season-rollover/pyramid-climb path: nextSeason (main.ts:1683-1729) incl. spSeasonReward tier/kind banking, board verdict (deriveExpectation/boardStanding), promo/releg bounds + setClubTier, star ageing→retireStar carry; showSeason tier-move reveal + board line + expectationLabel (main.ts:1250-1302,1750-1760); spTableHtml zones (main.ts:1136-1146); refreshDiary→gaffersDiaryEntry (main.ts:948-965); clubseason.ts TIERS/tierStrength/seededOpponents/liveTable; api.ts spSeasonReward + prestige (473-497,625-640); prestige.ts prestigeScore; achievements.ts. VERIFIED-HELD: PT-24/25/26/27/28 (tier persists to heir via setClubTier before retireStar; reveal banner nextIdx<=2; top-2/bottom-2 zones in spTableHtml + board now promote:2/relegate:2); PT-64/65/66/67 (expectation recomputed from just-finished t.pos, tier clamps in expectationLabel); PT-86/87/88/89/94 (honour tier stored = old tier pre-promotion (correct), cups filed kind!='league' so no season-bump/no highestTierIdx inflation, prestige tier-weights titles); PT-114 (lifetime titles from honours ledger). Bounds guarded: promoted=pos<=2&&tier>1, relegated=pos>=size-1&&tier<TIERS — no promote above tier1 / relegate below basement. Four genuinely-new findings below (PT-120..123), deduped against PT-1..119 + M1–M9. -->
- [x] **PT-120 (stage: manager promotion/relegation audit): [low-med] the Gaffer's Diary narrates promotion/relegation against a tier-blind top-3/bottom-2 rule, contradicting the real top-2/bottom-2 pyramid at every level.** `refreshDiary` feeds `gaffersDiaryEntry` a hard-coded `{ promote: 3, relegate: 2 }` (`client/src/main.ts:958`) regardless of `clubTier()`, and the diary keys its `PROMOTION_PLACES`/`RELEGATION_WATCH` lines off those numbers (`shared/src/gaffersDiary.ts:302-306`: `position <= t.promote` ⇒ promotion places, `position > total - relegate` ⇒ relegation watch). But the actual rule is top-2 up / bottom-2 down and is tier-edge-guarded everywhere else (`main.ts:1712-1713`, `spTableHtml` zones `main.ts:1138-1140`, board now `promote:2/relegate:2` `main.ts:1700`). So in tiers 2–9 a 3rd-placed club is told it's "in the promotion places" when only top-2 go up (a false promise); in the basement (tier 10) a bottom-2 club gets RELEGATION_WATCH prose though relegation is impossible there; and in the top flight a top-3 side is framed as "promotion" when top-3 is the continental zone. This is the exact class of bug PT-28 fixed for `boardStanding`, but the diary caller was missed. why it hurts: the diary sits right beside a correctly-keyed table and board line, so it contradicts them season after season and dangles automatic promotion the finish won't deliver. fix: pass tier-aware spots — `promote: tier===1 ? 3 : (tier>1 ? 2 : 0)` and `relegate: tier<TIERS ? 2 : 0` (mirroring `spTableHtml`/nextSeason) — into the diary table object; pure/presentational, no rng-draw change. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:958`.
- [x] **PT-121 (stage: manager promotion/relegation audit): [medium] the pyramid climb — the stage's core long-haul progression — unlocks ZERO achievements; the achievement ladder is entirely tier-blind.** `ACHIEVEMENTS` (`shared/src/achievements.ts:34-61`) has milestones for titles, cups, generations, seasons, wins and prestige rank, but nothing for reaching a higher division, a first promotion, or reaching the top flight — and `AchSnapshot` (`achievements.ts:9-21`) carries no tier/promotion field at all, so `buildAchSnapshot` (`client/src/main.ts:565-597`) can't express the climb even though `api.prestige()` already computes `highestTierIdx` (`client/src/api.ts:636`) from the honours ledger. why it hurts: promotion is the headline reward of the whole season-rollover loop, yet climbing from the Sunday League to the Premier Division produces no permanent milestone payoff, so the multi-season climb reads flat against a ladder that celebrates every other axis of progress (the brief's "climb reads flat"). fix: thread `highestTierIdx` (already returned by `api.prestige()`) into `AchSnapshot` as e.g. `topTier`, and add tier milestones (first promotion / reached the top flight) to `ACHIEVEMENTS`; pure predicates over the snapshot, determinism-safe. Ref: `shared/src/achievements.ts:34-61`.
- [x] **PT-122 (stage: manager promotion/relegation audit): [low-med] the board's expectation nudge fires on the very season the division changes difficulty, so relegation is double-eased (and promotion double-hardened) — blunting the climb's stakes.** `finishOf` now returns `'promotion'`/`'relegated'` on a tier move (`client/src/main.ts:1737-1740`, the PT-66 fix), and `deriveExpectation` applies `relegated ⇒ idx-2` / `promotion ⇒ idx+1` (`shared/src/board.ts:178-186`). But the nudge lands on the season the club has just DROPPED into a weaker division (`tierStrength` lower by 1, `shared/src/clubseason.ts:83-87`) where its fixed-strength squad is now relatively strongest — so a relegated club gets a −2-eased bar in a division it already dominates, making the board almost impossible to disappoint and the bounce-back a formality with no lasting sting; symmetrically a promoted club gets a +1-raised bar in a tougher division where it's now the weakest, setting the board up to be disappointed right after every promotion. `deriveExpectation`/prestige have no awareness of the tier-strength jump, so the nudge direction compounds with the difficulty change instead of offsetting it. why it hurts: the promotion/relegation swing is the emotional core of the climb, and this makes the drop carry no consequence while making the ascent feel punishing — the opposite of the intended arc. fix: on a tier move, temper rather than amplify — e.g. hold expectation steady (no nudge) the arrival season, or lower the promoted-club bar toward 'survival' and keep the relegated-club bar near 'promotion'; pure change to `deriveExpectation`'s tier-move handling, no rng-draw impact. Ref: `shared/src/board.ts:178-186`.
- [x] **PT-123 (stage: manager promotion/relegation audit): [low] the board's drop-zone penalty sours the verdict for a bottom-2 finish in the basement, where relegation cannot happen.** `nextSeason` passes `relegate: 2` to `boardStanding` unconditionally (`client/src/main.ts:1700`), and `boardScore` applies a flat `-25 dropPenalty` whenever `position > total - relegate` (bottom-2) and `expectation !== 'survival'` (`shared/src/board.ts:69-70`) — independent of tier. In the basement (tier 10) relegation is impossible (`main.ts:1713` guards `tier < TIERS`) and PT-67 only clamped the display LABEL/derivation, not `boardScore`'s raw penalty, so a high-prestige manager who has fallen to the basement and finishes 9th–10th (expectation derived to 'promotion'/'title', not 'survival') is docked 25 points and pushed toward a furious mood for a relegation that can't occur. why it hurts: the board turns unduly hostile at the very bottom of a declining dynasty, contradicting the impossibility of dropping further — a jarring mismatch in a long bloodline save. fix: zero the relegate spots for the basement (pass `relegate: tier < TIERS ? 2 : 0`) so `dropPenalty` can't fire where relegation is impossible; pure, determinism-safe. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1700`.
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-120/121/122/123): PT-120 — refreshDiary now passes tier-aware spots (`promote: dtier===1?3:2`, `relegate: dtier<TIERS?2:0`) so the Gaffer's Diary mirrors spTableHtml's zones + the real top-2/bottom-2 rule instead of a tier-blind top-3/bottom-2 (no more false "promotion places" in tiers 2-9 or relegation-watch in the basement). PT-121 — added a topTier field to AchSnapshot (max of api.prestige's now-exposed highestTierIdx and the live TIERS-clubTier, so a promotion registers immediately) and three climb achievements (Up We Go / Climbing the Pyramid / The Big Time at idx 1/5/9); updated the qa_achievements_fuzz ZERO/MAX/rngSnap literals. PT-122 — deriveExpectation now tempers tier moves instead of amplifying them: just-promoted → nudge DOWN (consolidate in a tougher division), just-relegated → nudge UP (bounce back as a big fish), and 'title' split back out to +1; the old promotion+1/relegation-2 compounded the difficulty swing. PT-123 — the boardStanding call passes `relegate: clubTier()<TIERS?2:0` so boardScore's -25 drop penalty can't fire in the basement where relegation is impossible (PT-67 had only clamped the label). All pure/presentational, determinism-safe. verify green. -->


<!-- Code audit 2026-08-29 (manager transfers/contracts stage, rotation 24) — scope: transfer market buy/sell, incoming-bid banner + accept, contract-talks negotiation (negotiateStar/haggle presets), extend/stake, star card contract panel, nftStatusHtml, backroom staff. VERIFIED HELD: PT-30/31 (buys disabled+reasoned on unaffordable/full squad, main.ts:1174-1178), PT-33/63 (haggle presets span reject 0.8 / counter 0.92 / accept 1.0 / generous 1.18, all bands reachable, totals + affordability + morale shown, main.ts:904-919), PT-32 (negotiateStar charges wage x length, api.ts:354-359), PT-62/81 (franchise bid scaling market.ts:63, ESC cleanup main.ts:1158-1162), PT-60 (sale fee banked atomically in succeed, api.ts:451-454), PT-93/95/96 (varied bid copy + no-wage labels + cup/WC guards). NEW below. -->
- [x] **PT-124 (stage: manager transfers/contracts audit): [medium] the Re-sign/Extend buttons advertise ONE season's wage as the whole price, but the talks charge wage × length — the cost is understated up to ~5×.** Since PT-32, `negotiateStar` charges `wage × length` (`client/src/api.ts:356-359`) and `extendPlayer` routes every re-sign through that negotiation (`main.ts:880`). But the star card button still reads `Re-sign · ${ci.extendCost}c · ${ci.lengthSeasons}y` (`main.ts:760`) and the stars-panel button reads `Re-sign ${ci.extendCost}c` (`main.ts:828`), where `extendCost` is the per-SEASON base wage (`shared/src/tokens.ts:79`, `contracts.ts:65`). So a "Re-sign · 350c · 5y" button actually costs 350×5 = 1750c once you accept the default 5-season "Meet it" (`main.ts:906,916`), and neither button is affordability-gated. why it hurts: the manager reads the headline coin figure as the price, opens talks expecting 350c and is quoted ~1750c — the exact "length is a hidden cost" trap PT-32 set out to kill, just relocated to the card. fix: show the TOTAL on the button — e.g. `${(ci.extendCost * ci.lengthSeasons).toLocaleString()}c over ${ci.lengthSeasons}y` — reusing the already-computed per-season × length, no new rng. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:760`, `client/src/main.ts:828`.
- [x] **PT-125 (stage: manager transfers/contracts audit): [medium] the star card's "or sell ~Xc" is a dead, price-misleading affordance — the star can't be sold at that figure by any control.** The contract panel renders `<span class="pc-sell">or sell ~${ci.sellValue}c</span>` beside Re-sign (`main.ts:761`), and `ci.sellValue` is the `releaseClause` value (`shared/src/tokens.ts:79` → `contracts.ts:66`). But it's a plain span with no data-attr, and the card's click handler only acts on `data-extend`/`data-stake`/close (`main.ts:780-784`), so clicking it does nothing. `sellPlayer` also hard-rejects the token — "the bloodline star can only leave via a transfer offer" (`api.ts:319`). The ONLY real sale is accepting an incoming bid, priced off `transferFee × 2.2/1.8/1.4` (`market.ts:63`), typically ~2× the quoted release-clause number. why it hurts: it dangles a sell price the player can neither act on nor ever actually receive, muddying the genuinely-different bid economics the game wants to headline. fix: drop the "or sell ~Xc" span for the bloodline star (or reword to "leaves only via a rival bid"), since there is no release-clause sale path in single-player. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:761`.
- [x] **PT-126 (stage: manager transfers/contracts audit): [low-med] backroom-staff hire buttons are never affordability-gated, unlike every other spend control.** `staffHtml` always renders `Hire · 💰${s.cost}` with no coin check and no `disabled`/reason (`main.ts:1357-1360`), and clicking an unaffordable one just throws → toast "Not enough coins" (`api.hireStaff` `api.ts:527`, handled `main.ts:1372`). Transfer buys disable + annotate the reason (PT-30/31, `main.ts:1174-1176`) and facilities carry a `canAfford` flag (`api.ts:688`), so staff is the one inconsistent shop. why it hurts: the UI invites a click it will always reject, breaking the "unaffordable = disabled with a reason" contract the rest of the economy screens honour. fix: in `staffHtml` compute `const coins = this.account?.coins ?? 0` and render the Hire button `disabled` with a `title="Not enough coins (need ${s.cost}c)"` when `coins < s.cost` — mirrors the transfer-market gate, no rng touched. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1355`.
- [x] **PT-127 (stage: manager transfers/contracts audit): [low] the orphaned `extendContract` endpoint still charges the pre-PT-32 flat one-season wage — a latent re-entry of the length-is-free exploit.** `api.extendContract` debits `ci.extendCost` ONCE and re-signs a `lengthSeasons` deal (`api.ts:290-293`), which is exactly the flat-wage economics PT-32 removed from `negotiateStar`. No caller reaches it — `extendPlayer` goes through negotiation (`main.ts:880`) — so it's dead today, but any future wiring of it (its name is the obvious "extend" handle) would silently reinstate the dominant-strategy longest-deal bug. why it hurts: a live-looking API surface encodes the old, broken cost model with no guard or comment marking it superseded. fix: either delete `extendContract` (nothing calls it) or align its debit to `ci.extendCost * ci.lengthSeasons` and add a comment pointing at PT-32, so it can't reintroduce the exploit if re-wired. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:281`.

PT-125 — the dead, misleading "or sell ~Xc" span is gone: the bloodline star now reads "leaves only via a rival bid" (no release-clause sale path in single-player), squad players read "worth ~Xc" (info, not a phantom action). All UI/endpoint fixes, determinism-safe. verify green. -->


<!-- Code audit 2026-08-29 (manager continental/World Finals stage, rotation 25) — scope: continentalHtml/playContinentalTie/simContinentalTie/resolveContinental + worldCupHtml/wcRunHtml/followWorldCup/wcTie/simWorldCupTie/resolveWorldCup/concludeWorldCup/showWorldCup + wcEditionDue/wcData/playerPath, shared/src/intl.ts worldCup+playerPath+homeNation, api.spSeasonReward/cupPrize honour-by-kind. VERIFIED HELD: qualification gate `tier===1 && pos<=3` has no off-by-one (main.ts:1733); the WC bracket generates 8 DISTINCT QF qualifiers via cross-bracket win(g)/run(g), no bye/dup, and playerPath's sfOpp/finalOpp are always drawn from the star-free half (intl.ts:143-152,293-298); editions start at season4=Edition1 (no Edition0); cup wins bank with kind:'continental'/'world' and DON'T bump profile.season or corrupt the league ledger (PT-94 holds — api.ts:493-498); wcWins/contTitles read title===1 by kind so a title:0 group/runner-up honour can't inflate them (main.ts:580-584); no reward double-count (followWorldCup pays ONLY the group-exit branch; the qualified run pays once in concludeWorldCup); PT-72's qualified-run review works; realChampion excludes beaten nations (PT-36). NEW distinct issues below. -->
- [x] **PT-128 (stage: manager continental/World Finals audit): [medium] a World-Finals GROUP-STAGE exit never persists `wcEdition`, so its concluded tournament report is permanently unreachable — the exact PT-72 dead-end, still open on the un-qualified branch.** The group-exit branch of `followWorldCup` saves `{ ...m, wcSeen: edition, wcStage: 'done' }` with NO `wcEdition` (`main.ts:1517`), whereas the qualified branch does set it (`main.ts:1521`). But the "View the tournament report" done-surface gates on `m.wcStage === 'done' && m.wcEdition != null` (`main.ts:1488`) and the review handler on `mm.wcEdition != null` (`main.ts:1313`). So after the one-shot `showWorldCup(wc, 'Group stage')` at `main.ts:1519`, returning to the season view renders NO World-Finals card at all (the done-block is skipped for null `wcEdition`, and `wcEditionDue()` returns null since `wcSeen===edition`), and the report can never be re-opened. Since the star's nation fails to escape its group in a large share of editions, this is the common path. why it hurts: PT-72 was filed+fixed precisely so a concluded World Finals stays reviewable, but the fix only covered the qualified run — the most frequent outcome (group exit) still vanishes after a single glance, with no trophy-room trace. fix: set `wcEdition: edition` (and `wcRun: []`, so `deriveWcFinish`→'Group stage') in the group-exit save at `main.ts:1517`; pure state write, determinism-safe. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1517`.
- [x] **PT-129 (stage: manager continental/World Finals audit): [low-med] simming a continental tie diverges from playing it — the away Semi-final is simmed with a +0.25 HOME term, and every facilities/staff edge the played tie applies is discarded.** `simContinentalTie` calls `simFixtureResult(clubLeagueStrength(), oppStr, seed)` with the default `homeTerm = 0.25` for EVERY round (`main.ts:1421,1660`), but `playContinentalTie` sets `venue = round%2===0 ? 'home' : 'away'` (`main.ts:1412`) and the played tie honours it via `iAmHome` (`main.ts:1988`). So the SF (round 1 — an AWAY leg when played) is simmed as a +0.25 home game: simming the semi is materially easier than playing it. The sim also omits the whole club-investment edge the played tie layers on (training-ground/fitness/attack/assistant/fan-zone, `main.ts:1978-1985`; captured for league sims by `simEdge`, `main.ts:1668`) — the exact PT-118 concern (investment must survive a sim), left open on the continental/WC sim path (`simWorldCupTie` same, `main.ts:1541`). why it hurts: the marquee knockout you built facilities for plays out differently, and unfairly easier, the moment you press Sim — the away semi in particular hands the star an unearned home edge. fix: pass a venue-aware `homeTerm` to `simContinentalTie` (0.25 home / 0 away / 0 for the neutral final) and fold `simEdge().strDelta` into the strength for both the continental and WC sims; pure hash-sim, no rng-draw-count change. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1421`.
- [x] **PT-130 (stage: manager continental/World Finals audit): [low-med] the continental Final and every World-Finals tie are labelled "(neutral)" on their cards yet are PLAYED as home games, handing the star the fan-zone home bonus and the engine's home side in the showpiece.** The card prints "(neutral)" for the continental final (`main.ts:1402`, `tie.neutral` true at round 2) and for every WC knockout tie (`main.ts:1509`), but `playContinentalTie` resolves the neutral final's venue to `'home'` (`main.ts:1412`: `tie.neutral ? 'home' : …`) and `wcTie` hardcodes `venue: 'home'` (`main.ts:1533`), so `startSpMatchWith` runs them with `iAmHome = true` (`main.ts:1988`) — seating the star as the match engine's home side AND applying the fan-zone multiplier that is gated to `venue === 'home'` (`main.ts:1980`). The star therefore gets an unearned home advantage in exactly the tie the UI swears is on neutral ground. why it hurts: the prestige final/World-Finals is both mislabelled and quietly tilted toward the player, undercutting the "neutral decider" framing the card sells. fix: carry a `neutral` flag on `spFixture` for these ties and, when set, seat the star as neither home side (skip the `iAmHome`-only fan-zone bonus and pick a neutral `mySide`); the played match already runs off a `Math.random` seed (`main.ts:1991`), so this touches no career rng-draw count. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1412`, `client/src/main.ts:1533`.
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-128/129/130): PT-128 — the group-stage-exit save now persists `wcEdition: edition` + `wcRun: []`, so the concluded World-Finals report stays reviewable on the common un-qualified branch (extends PT-72's qualified-only fix). PT-129 — simContinentalTie now uses a venue-aware homeTerm (QF home 0.25+fanzone, SF away 0, Final neutral 0) and folds simEdge().strDelta (training-ground/staff) into the strength; simWorldCupTie uses homeTerm 0 (neutral) + strDelta — so simming a knockout no longer hands an unearned home edge on the away semi and club investment survives a sim (extends PT-118 to the cup path). PT-130 — added a `neutral` flag to spFixture (set on the continental final + all WC ties) and gated the fan-zone home bonus to `venue==='home' && !neutral`, so the "(neutral)" showpiece no longer secretly gives the star the fan-zone edge. Engine check confirmed the only home advantage is myTeam.homeBoost (fan-zone) — no separate home-side term — so skipping the fan-zone multiplier fully neutralises the tie; the team-talk edge (venue-independent) is intentionally kept. Determinism-safe (played match uses a Math.random seed; sims are pure hash). verify green. -->
