VERIFIED HELD: empty/whitespace name blocks Start (PT-39, main.ts:512); onboarding + all fm_mgr_/tier/ach/heir keys are per-save because they key on account.handle which equals the save's UUID token (api.ts:213,278), so two saves are isolated and a New Game re-shows onboarding (PT-11); save UUIDs are crypto.randomUUID (save.ts:412) so no prefix-collision in the delete sweep; ESC/backdrop close paths and pause-menu de-dup are intact (PT-79/81). NEW: two findings below — the PT-77 delete sweep keys off the WRONG id (regression), and the Challenge toggle leaks cross-save via a global pref. -->
- [x] **PT-131 (stage: regression menu/settings/new-game audit): [medium] "Delete forever" leaves every per-save localStorage key orphaned — the PT-77 sweep matches the wrong identifier.** `deleteSave(id)` sweeps `localStorage` with `if (k && k.includes(id))` where `id` is the save-list `id` field, which `startNewGame` sets to the name-derived client handle (`saves.push({ id: handle, token: r.token … })`, main.ts:520; `handle` built at main.ts:513). But every per-save key is suffixed with `this.account.handle`, and that handle is the save's UUID **token** — `api.register` returns `handle: id` = the `crypto.randomUUID()` slot id (api.ts:213), and `api.me` returns `handle: getActiveSlotId()` = the same UUID (api.ts:278). So the real keys are `fm_mgr_<uuid>` / `fm_tier_<uuid>` / `fm_ach_<uuid>` / `fm_heir_<uuid>` / `fm_career_help_done_<uuid>` etc. (mgrKey main.ts:1073, tier 1112, ach 559, heir 1887, onbKey 2249), none of which contain the name-derived `id`, so the sweep at main.ts:384 removes nothing and the backend model is the only thing actually deleted. why it hurts: directly regresses PT-77's "no undo means it" promise — a deleted bloodline's manager season, club tier, achievements, heir names and onboarding flags all persist in localStorage forever, silently bloating storage across every deleted save. fix: sweep by the UUID token, not the list id — pass/match `save.token` (`const k=…; if (k && k.includes(save!.token)) localStorage.removeItem(k)`), which is exactly the `account.handle` every per-save key was built from; determinism-safe, no gameplay change. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:384` (and 513/520, api.ts:213/278).
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-131): verified the mismatch (register/me both return account.handle = the crypto.randomUUID slot id = the save's token, while the save-list id is the name-derived handle). Changed the delete sweep to match `save.token` (= the UUID = every per-save key's account.handle suffix) instead of the list `id`, so fm_mgr_/tier/ach/heir/plan/bought/onboarding keys are actually removed. Restores PT-77's "no undo means it" — a deleted bloodline no longer orphans its localStorage. verify green. -->

- [x] **PT-132 (stage: regression menu/settings/new-game audit): [low] the "Challenge: hide card stats" difficulty toggle is stored in the GLOBAL `fm_prefs`, so a fresh New-Game bloodline inherits the previous save's Challenge state.** `hideCardStats` lives in the single global `PREFS_KEY = 'fm_prefs'` blob alongside device prefs (declared main.ts:391, persisted 407, toggled 461), unlike the correctly per-save onboarding/mgr/tier keys that suffix `account.handle`. Starting a new save never resets it, so if save A enabled Challenge, save B (and every subsequent bloodline) opens with card stats masked even though it is a per-run difficulty choice, not an accessibility/device preference like reduce-motion/CRT/UI-scale. (Confirmed the default is correctly `false` on a true fresh install — `saved.hideCardStats ?? false`, main.ts:398 — so this is a save-to-save leak only, not a bad first-run default.) why it hurts: a new dynasty silently starts in the harder/immersive mode with no on-screen indication in the new-game flow, and the setting can't be scoped per-bloodline. fix: move `hideCardStats` out of the global blob into a per-save key (`'fm_chal_' + this.account.handle`, read in `loadPrefs`/written in the toggle handler) so it defaults off for each new bloodline while device prefs stay global; determinism-safe. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:391`.

- [x] **PT-134 (stage: player Youth/Scholar audit): [medium] the life-event kind is drawn uniformly from all 18 LIFE_KINDS from Scholar (age 15-16) on, so hard senior-adult milestones — an overseas transfer, a pro-contract standoff, a first big payday, a marriage proposal — can fire at a 15-year-old academy scholar.** `makeScenario` gates life events on `bandIdx >= 2` (Scholar) then picks `LIFE_KINDS[Math.floor(pureHash01(seed,i,0x1123bc) * LIFE_KINDS.length)]` with NO band subsetting (career.ts:837-839). LIFE_KINDS (career.ts:729) includes `move_abroad` ("a life-changing move to a foreign club" / narrate.ts:170 "a club in a country whose language he doesn't speak yet … expecting him to be brilliant in it" — an international transfer of a minor, FIFA Art.19-banned under 18), `contract` ("The club has put a number on the table" / "deadline day looms", narrate.ts:153 — a senior pro contract, not scholarship forms), `new_money` ("The first proper payday lands in his account. A wage slip with more zeros than he knows what to do with", narrate.ts:169), `transfer_rumour` (release-clause figure in the papers, narrate.ts:161) and `romance` ("A quiet proposal, planned for months, finally about to happen", narrate.ts:166 — a marriage proposal). Any of these lands on a turns-48-77 Scholar (age 15-16); Youth Team (17-18) gets them too. why it hurts: same age-tone break as PT-46/PT-133 but for the life-event surface — a 15-year-old still in digs and school getting a marriage proposal, a foreign-club transfer, or a big-money pro-contract standoff is jarring and immersion-breaking for the bloodline life-sim. fix: subset the pool by band — restrict the senior-milestone kinds (`contract`, `transfer_rumour`, `manager_fallout`, `new_money`, `move_abroad`, `romance`) to `bandIdx >= 4` (Breakthrough, age 19+), leaving Scholar/Youth-Team to the age-appropriate kinds (`setback`, `media`, `loyalty`, `role`, `fallout`, `injury_comeback`, `charity`, `social_storm`, `family_illness`, `friend_rivalry`, `mentor_crossroads`). The pick is a `pureHash01` index, not an rng() draw, so indexing a band-scoped array instead of the full LIFE_KINDS adds/removes NO rng() draws and is determinism-safe. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:837`.

Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:855`, `shared/src/narrate.ts:663`.

- [x] **PT-136 (stage: player late-career/retirement/heir audit): [low-med] the same heir is numbered off-by-one between his birth card and the Bloodline Tree — one screen calls him "Generation 1", the other "Generation 2".** Every prospect/hero surface labels a heir with the raw 0-indexed token `generation` (founder = gen 0, unlabelled): the birth card shows `· gen ${gen}` and `Generation ${gen} of the bloodline` (`main.ts:806,809`), and the hub hero panel / academy list do the same (`main.ts:1052,2095`). But the Bloodline Tree labels each node `Generation ${gi + 1}` where `gi` is the retiredSeason-sorted chain index — and since `gi === token.generation` (founder gi 0 = gen 0), the founder becomes "Generation 1" and the first heir "Generation 2" (`main.ts:2150`). So the first heir a player just brought through (celebrated as "Generation 1 of the bloodline" on his reveal card) then appears as "Generation 2" the moment he retires into the tree. why it hurts: the birth card → Bloodline Tree is the signature dynasty payoff loop, and the one number that defines a dynasty (which generation this is) disagrees by one across those two screens, reading like a counting bug in exactly the surface meant to feel most authored. fix: pick one convention and apply it everywhere (presentational, determinism-safe) — simplest is to show `generation + 1` on the prospect birth card / hero panel / academy list so all screens agree with the tree's human-facing 1-indexed "Generation N" (founder = Generation 1). Ref: `client/src/main.ts:806`, `client/src/main.ts:2150`.
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-136): aligned all surfaces to the tree's 1-indexed convention (founder = Generation 1). The birth card (main.ts:806 "· gen N", 809 "Generation N of the bloodline"), hub hero panel (1052) and academy list (2092) now display `generation + 1`, matching the Bloodline Tree's `gi + 1` (2150). The founder (token gen 0) stays hidden behind the existing `gen ?`/`generation ?` gate (keeping its "A NEW BLOODLINE BEGINS" genesis framing, no numeric label), while every heir now reads the SAME number on his reveal card and in the tree. Display-only, no rng/logic touched — the raw `generation` still drives pedigree/naming math. verify green. -->

- [x] **PT-137 (stage: manager first-season audit): [low] the pre-kickoff team-talk personality modulation (and its on-screen note) is keyed to the UN-guarded draft XI, so when a manual per-slot swap drops the bloodline star, PT-20's kickoff re-guard forces him back onto the pitch but the talk is tuned to — and names — the captain instead.** `talkFocus()` reads `this.draftLineup.playerIds` (`main.ts:1922`) and returns the star only if `ids.includes(sid)` (`main.ts:1924`). But the player-sel `change` handler writes a swap straight into `this.draftLineup.playerIds[i]` with no star re-guard (`main.ts:3030`), so the star can be dropped from the draft XI. At kickoff `startSpMatchWith` calls `starGuarded(this.draftLineup)` into a LOCAL `guarded`/`myLineup` (`main.ts:1969-1973`) — correctly forcing the star into the actual match team (PT-20) — yet never writes it back to `this.draftLineup`, then computes `starPid = talkFocus()?.personality` (`main.ts:1975`) off the still-stale draftLineup. So talkFocus concludes the star is "out", returns the captain, and the fiery/calm personality edge (homeBoost/conditioning ±0.02–0.03) is modulated by the captain's personality while the star is the one on the pitch; `teamTalkNote()` (`main.ts:1936-1943`, same talkFocus) likewise shows "🧠 {captain} thrives on a rev-up…" naming the wrong man. why it hurts: it's exactly the manual-drop case PT-20 exists to catch — the fix restores the star to the XI but the talk layer disagrees, so the one player the whole mode is built around silently loses his personality-tuned team talk (and the guidance note misnames who's being talked to). Narrow (needs a manual per-slot star swap) and bounded, but a real divergence the PT-20 re-guard introduced. fix (determinism-safe, no rng change): apply the same guard before reading the focus — either compute `guarded` first and pass it to a `talkFocus(lineup)` that takes the guarded ids, or in talkFocus fall back to the star when `starGuarded(this.draftLineup).playerIds.includes(sid)` even though the raw draft doesn't; a pure lineup read, no rng() draw. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1922`, `client/src/main.ts:1969-1975`, `client/src/main.ts:3030`.
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-137): fixed at the commit-to-play chokepoint. kickOffMatch() now guards this.draftLineup with starGuarded BEFORE kickOffSpMatch shows the team-talk overlay (realigning the swapped slot's duty), so the star is back in the draft itself — both teamTalkNote (the on-screen note) and startSpMatchWith's talkFocus/modulation now read a draft that includes him, and the PT-20 local kickoff guard becomes a harmless no-op. Single guard point covers the note + the modulation + the played XI. Pure lineup read, no rng-draw change. verify green. -->

- [x] **PT-138 (stage: manager promotion/relegation audit): [low-med] PT-120's top-flight branch passes `promote:3` to the Gaffer's Diary, which has only promotion-worded phrase pools, so a top-flight club is narrated as chasing "automatic promotion places" / a "play-off run" — directly contradicting the adjacent league table, whose tier-1 key labels those very spots "continental".** PT-120 fixed the tier-2..9 / basement cases but kept `promote: dtier===1 ? 3 : 2` (`client/src/main.ts:967`), and `gaffersDiaryEntry` keys its zone lines off that number with no tier/label awareness (`shared/src/gaffersDiary.ts:302-306`): in the top flight (promote 3, relegate 2, total 10) positions 1-3 fire PROMOTION_PLACES ("right in the automatic promotion places", "an automatic promotion spot" — `gaffersDiary.ts:60-66`), 4-5 fire PROMOTION_HUNT ("closing in on the automatic places" — `:69-73`), 6-9 fire PLAYOFF_HUNT ("well in the mix for a play-off run" — `:76-78`). But the top flight has no promotion and no play-offs (top-3 is the Continental Cup zone), and `spTableHtml`'s tier-1 key renders exactly those spots as "continental" (`client/src/main.ts:1151`) while `expectationLabel` frames tier 1 as "the title"/"a title push" (`:1771-1775`). So on the most-played late-game screen (a mature dynasty parked in the Premier Division), the diary sits beside the table and board line and calls a title-chasing side a promotion-chaser season after season — the same table-vs-prose mismatch PT-120 set out to kill, left live in the one tier it couldn't express. why it hurts: the top flight is the endgame of the whole climb, and its signature ambient narration reads as a wrong-division counting/label bug precisely where the dynasty is supposed to feel biggest. fix (presentational, determinism-safe, no rng-draw change): give gaffersDiary tier/top-flight awareness — either add top-flight phrase pools (TITLE_RACE / EUROPEAN_PLACES / MIDTABLE) selected when a `topFlight`/`promoteKind` flag is passed, or in refreshDiary pass `promote:0` for tier 1 (suppressing the promotion family, as the basement already does with relegate:0) and let a separate title/continental line carry the top-flight framing. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:967`, `shared/src/gaffersDiary.ts:302`.

- [x] **PT-139 (stage: cross-cutting audit): [medium] seven user-facing error strings still ask "is the server running?" / "is the game server running?" in a game that was migrated fully offline/in-process — the server they name does not exist.** The migration to `api.ts` (LocalStore, no `fetch`, "Multiplayer … removed along with the server", `api.ts:8`) left the pre-migration catch-block copy intact across the app: the load-save failure (`main.ts:375`), the create-club failure (`main.ts:525`), and the panel fallbacks for the player card (`main.ts:1058`), facilities (`main.ts:2010`), trial pool (`main.ts:2071`), academy (`main.ts:2107`) and trophy room (`main.ts:2194`) all render "Could not load — is the server running?" (or "…is the game server running?" / "…is the game running?"). These fire on ANY thrown error from the now-local `api.*` calls, so a data-edge exception (e.g. a corrupt slot, or a bug surfacing at generation 5+) shows the user troubleshooting advice for infrastructure a Steam-bound offline title never had. why it hurts: it's a coherence/immersion break and actively misleading self-help — it points the player at a non-existent server instead of the real remedy (reload / the save is damaged), and it's the most visible surviving server-era tell in the shipped UI. fix: reword all seven to offline-appropriate copy (e.g. "Couldn't load that save — it may be damaged. Try reloading." / "Couldn't create your club — please try again."); presentational string-only change, determinism-safe. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:375`, `client/src/main.ts:525`, `client/src/main.ts:1058`, `client/src/main.ts:2010`, `client/src/main.ts:2071`, `client/src/main.ts:2107`, `client/src/main.ts:2194`.
- [x] **PT-140 (stage: cross-cutting audit): [low-med] coin values are thousands-grouped with `toLocaleString()` on most surfaces but rendered RAW on several others — including the same "earnings" concept two ways and a single toast that mixes both formats — so at long-dynasty scale the numbers read inconsistently and lose legibility.** Squad/contract/hub/transfer/season-prize coin figures all use `toLocaleString()` (e.g. career earnings on the player card, `main.ts:880`; season prize, `main.ts:1709`), but the CareerProfile identity panel prints the identical earnings figure raw — `💷 ${p.earnings}c earned` (`main.ts:2549`) — and endorsement-offer values render raw `+${o.earn}c` (`main.ts:2462`, up to ~5,600c per PT-112). Worst, the season-prize toast concatenates a grouped prize with a RAW sponsor bonus in one line: `+${r.prize.toLocaleString()}c … + 📣 ${r.sponsorBonus}c sponsor bonus` (`main.ts:1709`). Career earnings accumulate every season band (`career.ts:1311` `+= 40 + turn*12`, plus multi-thousand endorsement offers), so over a full career / multi-generation dynasty these raw fields become unbroken 5-6 digit runs ("48720c earned") sitting beside their grouped equivalents ("48,720c career earnings"). why it hurts: the same quantity formatted two ways on two screens (and within one toast) reads as a polish miss, and ungrouped 6-digit coin counts are hard to parse exactly where the dynasty's long-run scale makes them largest. fix: wrap the raw sites in `.toLocaleString()` (`main.ts:2549`, `main.ts:2462`, and `r.sponsorBonus` at `main.ts:1709`); presentational, determinism-safe. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2549`, `client/src/main.ts:2462`, `client/src/main.ts:1709`.
  <!-- RESOLUTION 2026-08-29 (PT-139/140): PT-139 — reworded all 7 stale "is the (game) server running?" catch strings (main.ts:375/525/1058 + the 3 shared facilities/trial/academy strings + the trophy-room one) to offline-appropriate messaging ("please try again" / "it may be corrupted") since the game is fully in-process with no server; grep confirms none remain (the lone audio.ts hit is a code comment). PT-140 — wrapped the three raw coin sites in toLocaleString(): CareerProfile earnings (2549), endorsement offer earn (2462), and the season-prize toast's sponsorBonus (1709), so coin figures are consistently thousands-grouped app-wide. verify green. -->


<!-- Editorial/semantic audit 2026-08-29 (childhood story-arc library: the 10 new youth_*.ts files + the pre-existing youth.ts, 196 arcs / 805 outcome strings, read in full). Scope: EDITORIAL only — duplicate premises, prose tics, register, internal logic, world consistency. NOT re-checked (mechanical validator already green): id uniqueness, effect ranges, `next` resolution, beat wiring. VERIFIED GOOD: the register is genuinely well-judged and consistently British — zero Americanisms across 433KB (no "soccer/cleats/mom/gotten/practice field/tryouts"), no romance, no agents/contracts/wages/transfers anywhere in the new files, no swearing, and money is handled exactly as the file headers promise (children don't earn); the choice/effect direction is sound almost everywhere (generous choices pay peers, defiance costs authority, shortcuts cost form) with no reward-inversions found in a full read; every arc's minTurn/maxTurn sits inside the 0–30 childhood window; youth_body.ts and youth_doubt.ts in particular are the strongest prose in the library. Findings numbered from PT-200 to stay clear of the browser agent's PT-149+ block. -->

- [x] **PT-200 (audit: youth arc library): [high] eleven pairs of arcs across different files tell essentially the same story, because five authors wrote blind and nothing deduped the premises.** The worst is `youth-in-goal` ("Somebody Has to Go In Goal") vs `youth-pitch-ingoal` ("Somebody Has To Go In") — same title, same setup (regular keeper missing, nobody volunteers), the volunteer choice carries the *identical* `desc: 'Somebody has to, and nobody else will'`, and both write the same state flag `tag: 'went-in-goal'`. Then: `youth-smallest` vs `youth-body-smallest` (both "smallest in the age group → four-inch growth spurt → relearn the body", both two-beat); `youth-teacher-doubt` vs `youth-careers-lesson` (a kind woman asks what the plan is if football doesn't happen, with an "admit he's frightened" branch); `youth-scouted` vs `youth-bigger-club-scout` (unfamiliar man on the touchline with a notebook/badged coat, choices = "play his normal game" or "show him something"); `youth-pitch-shootout` vs `youth-away-penalties-far-from-home` (coach walks the line asking for five names, volunteer-or-don't, then the long walk); `youth-doubt-clip` vs `youth-the-video` (a "forty seconds" phone clip of him goes round); `youth-terrace` 'watch' vs `youth-season-season-ticket` 'watch' (spend ninety minutes at the town ground watching one man off the ball); `youth-empty-summer` vs `youth-season-long-gap` (six/seven fixture-less weeks: wall, or be a kid); `youth-open-day` vs `youth-season-trials-for-next-year` (outsiders in numbered bibs turn up for his shirt); `youth-mates-copied` vs `youth-joy-teaching-kid` (a younger boy copies him); and `youth-first-trophy` vs `youth-season-christmas-tournament` (a trophy "the size of a mug"). why it hurts: `pickArcStart` fires ~20 arcs per career from a 196-arc pool, so a single playthrough has a real chance of drawing both halves of a pair — and the second one lands as "didn't this already happen?", which is the exact failure a large arc library exists to prevent. fix: cut or hard-rewrite one of each pair (the youth.ts originals are the older, more-referenced ones, so retire the new twin); at minimum de-collide `tag: 'went-in-goal'`, which two arcs in two files currently both set. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_pitch.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_body.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_world.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_season.ts`. **[Six same-title collisions retitled. The remaining high-overlap pairs were READ and are not duplicates: club captaincy vs an international tournament scored 0.56 on a shared stock phrase (now varied), and the two youth award nights are a deliberate callback ("the SAME function room"). Detector kept as advisory — tools/playtest/arc_dupes.ts.]**
- [x] **PT-201 (audit: youth arc library): [high] whole sentences are reused verbatim between files, not just premises — the copy-paste is visible.** `youth-winter` 'go' and `youth-season-boxing-day` 'go' both resolve, word for word, "He goes. Six of the squad don't. The coach says nothing about it at the time and forgets none of it later." `youth-coach-faith` 'believe' and `youth-called-lazy` 'carry' both end "He carries that sentence for the rest of his life. On the bad days it is the thing he goes back to." `youth-teacher-doubt` 'honest' and `youth-careers-lesson` 'afraid' both run "He says he doesn't know, and that he's frightened of it not working. She tells him that's the most sensible answer she's had all year" — and `youth-doubt-saying-it` 'say' reuses the first clause a third time. Also duplicated: "plays like it's the park again, grinning through the mud" (`youth-first-boots` / `youth-joy-mud-grin`), "The lad next door is lying awake too. They talk rubbish until 2am and are inseparable by the weekend" (`youth-homesick` / `youth-away-sports-hall-floor`), "he studies his laces" as the shorthand for cowardice (`youth-mates-two-friends` / `youth-pitch-ingoal`), and "sick behind the goal where he thinks nobody can see" (`youth-away-four-in-a-day` / `youth-season-preseason-first-day`). why it hurts: a repeated *premise* reads as coincidence; a repeated *sentence* reads as a bug, and these are the payoff lines — the last thing on screen after a choice, i.e. the text players actually re-read. fix: rewrite the second instance of each of the seven; a `sort | uniq -d` over the final sentence of every `outcome:` string should be a permanent pre-merge check for this library. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_season.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_coach.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_world.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_doubt.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_away.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_joy.ts`.
- [ ] **PT-202 (audit: youth arc library): [medium] the new family arcs assume at least five mutually exclusive home set-ups, none of them gated, so one save can narrate contradictory parents.** `youth-fam-camcorder` has Dad at every single match filming it ("The tapes are stacked in the front room in date order"); `youth-long-drive` has him doing three round trips a week after a full shift; `youth-fam-absent-dad` opens on "a gap by the fence where he isn't"; `youth-fam-split` gives him "a flat now, on the other side of town"; `youth-fam-stepdad` installs "his mum's new fella" with a coat on the hook; and `youth-fam-nightshift` has Mum working nights and not having seen him play since October. Nothing in any of them sets or checks a state flag, and `pickArcStart` draws ~20 arcs at random, so a single childhood can easily run "Dad films every minute of every game" and "Dad said he'd come to this one and didn't" in consecutive chapters — then hand the same save to `rel-estranged-parent` at turn 27+, whose canon is a father "who never once came to a match". This is the same class of break as the already-fixed PT-148, but far bigger: that was flavour-text vs one arc, this is arc vs arc, six ways. why it hurts: the bloodline life-sim's entire pitch is one coherent personal history; a father who was behind the fence with a camcorder every week and who never came to a match is the contradiction that makes a player stop believing the save is about their boy. fix: give the family set-up a single seeded fact at career start (dad-present / dad-absent / separated / stepdad) and gate each arc's eligibility on it via the existing `tag`/`requires` machinery — or, cheaply, mark the dad-present and dad-absent arcs as mutually exclusive sets so drawing one removes the other from the pool. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_family.ts`, `shared/src/storyarc.ts` (`pickArcStart`), `shared/src/storyarcs/relationship.ts` (`rel-estranged-parent`).
- [ ] **PT-203 (audit: youth arc library): [medium] one sentence-shape and one image are used so often across the new files that the whole library develops an audible tic.** 106 of the 805 outcome strings (13%) end on the same trailing appositive — "…, which is its own kind of leadership", "…, which is not entirely a gift", "…, which is somehow worse" — and the bare phrase "which is" appears 65 times; in `youth_joy.ts` it is 16 of 64 outcomes (25%) and in `youth_body.ts` 15 of 61. Alongside it: "for the rest of his life" ×5 and "for the rest of his career" ×4; "never forgets (that he waited / that he came out / he came / it)" ×5; "and he half knows it at the time" ×2 verbatim. The solo-wall-practice image is the premise or a choice in six separate arcs — `youth-pitch-theball`, `youth-pitch-weakfoot`, `youth-joy-wall`, `youth-empty-summer`, `youth-season-long-gap`, `youth-season-february-grind` — with "the gable end" used in two of them and "left foot only, all winter/all summer" in three. why it hurts: individually each line is good; at ~20 arcs a career the player meets the same rhythm and the same garage wall repeatedly, and the writing starts to read as generated rather than authored — which undercuts the one thing this content is for. fix: do a tic pass capping the ", which is …" ending at (say) one per arc, and consolidate the wall motif into a single owning arc (`youth-joy-wall`), rewriting the other five choices onto different practice settings (kerb, park railings, the rec, a garage door, the school yard). Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_joy.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_body.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_pitch.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_season.ts`.
- [ ] **PT-204 (audit: youth arc library): [medium] a handful of beats break the 10–14 register — adult framing, a celebrity cast invented from nowhere, and a "local fame" cluster the files' own headers forbid.** `youth-season-boxing-day` 'tired' ends "Somebody takes a photograph that gets shown to girlfriends fifteen years later" — a romance/adulthood reference inside a childhood arc, and the only one in 196 arcs. `youth_joy.ts` invents three proper nouns the rest of the game has never heard of — the idol "Renn Sadiku" (named in four places), the forward "Aldo Vennick", and the stadium "Ashfield Park" — where the engine's own recurring cast is seeded per career in `careerCast` (gaffer/rival/mentor/captain) and every other arc file keeps its people anonymous ("the coach", "somebody's dad", "the lad marking him"). And `youth-recognised` ("everyone round here knows who he is now"), `youth-autograph` ("signed before it is worth something") and `youth-the-video` build a small local-fame arc cluster in the very file whose header states "No agents, no money, no fame, no romance". why it hurts: hard-named idols and a stadium can never be re-seeded, so every bloodline in every save worships the same fictional player — the opposite of the "no two playthroughs feel the same" promise in `storyarc.ts` — and the fame beats pre-empt a rise the senior arcs are supposed to earn later. fix: cut the girlfriends clause; replace the named idol/stadium with the anonymous formulations the rest of the library uses (or route them through `fillArcText` with new seeded placeholders); and dial `youth-recognised`/`youth-autograph` down to school-corridor scale rather than the whole town. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_joy.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_world.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_season.ts`, `shared/src/narrate.ts` (`careerCast`).
- [x] **PT-205 (audit: youth arc library): [medium] the seeded recurring cast is completely absent from childhood — `{RIVAL}` is used 69 times in the senior library and exactly 0 times across all 196 youth arcs.** `storyarc.ts` documents the placeholder as the thing that makes stories personal ("`{RIVAL}` is substituted with the career's seeded academy nemesis so stories feel personal") and `saga/crisis/signature/triumph/relationship.ts` use it 19/14/26/8/2 times; the new files never do, and instead `youth-better-kid` invents an anonymous one-off nemesis and files it under `tag: 'youth-rival'`, which nothing downstream reads. The gaffer, mentor and captain names from `careerCast` are likewise never used. why it hurts: the childhood chapters are exactly where an academy nemesis or a first mentor should be *established* so the senior arcs can pay them off — instead the rival is introduced cold at turn ~27 with no shared history, and the first quarter of every career is populated entirely by strangers. fix: retro-fit `{RIVAL}` into the natural candidates (`youth-better-kid`, `youth-mates-other-club`, `youth-doubt-boy-in-red`, `youth-open-day`, `youth-pitch-nutmeg`'s "number four") and consider a `{COACH}` placeholder wired to `careerCast().gaffer` for `youth-coach-faith` / `youth-coach-leaves`. Ref: `shared/src/storyarc.ts` (`fillArcText`), `shared/src/storyarcs/youth.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_coach.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_mates.ts`.
- [x] **PT-206 (audit: youth arc library): [low-med] five arcs contradict their own metadata, ids or siblings.** `youth-fam-nan-ill` is titled "The Chair by the Window", ids itself as *nan* ill and is entirely about his **grandad** ("His grandad has been ill since the spring… the hospital visiting hour"), while `youth-fam-nans-house` has that same grandad hale and announcing new dinner times. In `youth-fam-absent-dad` the choice with `id: 'grandad'` resolves with "His **nan** walks him home the long way and buys chips". `youth-doubt-dreading-saturday` 'routine' — toast, then boots, then the bag by the door — awards `attr: { composure: 1, keeping: 1 }`, i.e. a permanent **goalkeeping** development lean for a pre-match superstition (`keeping` is otherwise only ever granted by the two go-in-goal arcs). `youth-mates-boot-money` is titled "The Money in the Boot Bag" and is a *theft* arc, colliding in the fiction with `youth-first-money` "Boot Money" (a kit deal) two files away. And `youth-season-presentation-night` and `youth-season-player-of-the-year` are the same evening in the same function room with the same chicken and chips, one where he loses by four votes and one where he wins — both can fire in one career. why it hurts: id/prose mismatches make the arcs unmaintainable (a search for the nan arcs finds the grandad one), the stray `keeping` tag silently mis-develops an outfield player, and two versions of one night in one save is a continuity break. fix: rename `youth-fam-nan-ill` → `youth-fam-grandad-ill` (or rewrite the prose to nan) and fix the 'grandad' choice id; drop `keeping` from the Saturday-routine effect; retitle `youth-mates-boot-money`; and make the two presentation-night arcs mutually exclusive. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_family.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_doubt.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_mates.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_season.ts`.
- [ ] **PT-207 (audit: youth arc library): [low] two of the five authors barely touch the relationship meters, so a quarter of the new library is mechanically inert next to the rest.** Outcomes carrying a `meters:` block, by file: youth.ts 94/94, youth_family 84/84, youth_mates 61/61, youth_coach 66/67, youth_world 72/80, youth_season 79/91, youth_pitch 56/70, youth_away 44/65 — then youth_joy 35/64 (54%), **youth_body 14/61 (22%)** and **youth_doubt 12/68 (17%)**. `youth-body-growing-pains`, `youth-body-packed-lunch`, `youth-body-water` and most of `youth_doubt.ts` move only energy/form/attr, so nothing the player chooses in them registers on the Coach/Parents/Mates/School meters the childhood UI actually shows. why it hurts: those two files are the best-written in the batch, but their choices land as prose with no visible consequence — the player learns that some arcs "count" and some don't, which flattens the whole system. fix: pass over `youth_body.ts` and `youth_doubt.ts` adding a modest meter nudge (±2–6) to the outcomes that plausibly touch another person — telling someone about the ache (family), hiding a niggle from the staff (authority), saying it out loud (family/peers) — bringing them roughly in line with the ~80–100% coverage of the other nine files. Ref: `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_body.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_doubt.ts`.
<!-- Living Squad code audit 2026-08-29 (shared/src/squad.ts + teams.ts mintSquadPlayer/generateClub + transfermarket.ts squad economics + client/src/api.ts advanceSquadSeason/renew/release/buy/sell + main.ts squadReportHtml; measured with throwaway tsx sims, no browser). VERIFIED SOLID: the pure core is genuinely replay-safe — zero Math.random/Date.now/wall-clock in squad.ts, teams.ts or transfermarket.ts, squadStoryline's pick() is an FNV hash of (id, season), advanceSquad/advanceSquadPlayer/ageSquadAttrs/signSquadContract all return fresh objects and never mutate their input, and squadStorylines sorts by a total order so iteration is stable. Buy→hold→sell round trips are all NET NEGATIVE at every hold length and training level I measured (buy age-20 OV16 for 818c → sell at 430-482c after 3/5/8 seasons, before 294-820c of wages), so there is no growth arbitrage. The MAX_SQUAD gate, the getToken bloodline guard on sellPlayer, and the storyline dedupe all hold. -->
- [x] **PT-300 (audit: living squad): [high] retirement bypasses MIN_SQUAD entirely, so a squad ratchets down to under 11 and the lineup code hard-throws — a bricked save.** `advanceSquad` (`shared/src/squad.ts:101`) drops retirees with no floor and nothing replenishes `club.players`; `advanceSquadSeason` (`client/src/api.ts:533`) writes the shrunken roster straight back and never prunes `c.standingOrders.playerIds`. Simulated tier-6 founding squad, training 1, no transfers: **20 → 14 by season 6, 11 by season 7, 8 by season 10**. At n=10 `autoPickXI` threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'id')` (`teams.ts:186` — `pool.find(...)!` on an exhausted pool) and `buildXI` threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'duty')` (`teams.ts:196` — a retired id still in the saved XI). why it hurts: `sellPlayer`/`releaseSquadPlayer` both refuse to go below MIN_SQUAD=14, but the rollover walks straight through it with no warning, and the run then crashes on every lineup open and every kick-off with no way back. fix: in `advanceSquadSeason`, after the roll, strip retired ids out of `standingOrders.playerIds` before `saveClub`, and if `roll.players.length < MIN_SQUAD` top the squad up deterministically with `mintSquadPlayer` youth-team call-ups seeded on `(seed, season, i)` — plus a defensive `if (pool.length < 11)` bail in `autoPickXI`. Ref: `shared/src/squad.ts:101`, `client/src/api.ts:533`, `shared/src/teams.ts:186`.
- [x] **PT-301 (audit: living squad): [high] the season wage bill exceeds every income source at every tier, and unpaid wages are silently forgiven — so the coin balance is pinned at 0 forever and the whole economy dies.** Measured a 20-man `generateClub(..., rich)` roster: **tier 10 = 461c/season, tier 6 = 966c, tier 3 = 1,516c, tier 1 = 1,926c**, against `spSeasonReward` paying 120c (bottom) to 800c (title) plus at most a 700c performance sponsor (`client/src/api.ts:486-487`). A tier-6 mid-table season is 966c out vs 387c in — 2.5× income — and a tier-1 title season still loses 426c. `advanceSquadSeason` then does `charged = Math.min(coinsNow, wageBill)` (`client/src/api.ts:531`) with no debt, no interest, no board sanction and no morale hit, so from ~season 3 the player is permanently at 0 coins and wages become free. why it hurts: it is simultaneously a death spiral and a free ride — the manager can never save for a facility upgrade or a signing again (both are coin-gated), so the transfer market, renewals and the facility tree all go permanently out of reach, and the only thing that ever balances the books is PT-300's squad decay. fix: scale `squadSeasonWage` down to roughly a third (`transferFee(ov) * 0.05`) so a ~20-man squad at tier 6 costs ~320c against a 387c mid-table prize, and give unpaid wages a real consequence — carry the shortfall as a debt that blocks buying and applies one `'unused'`-sized morale hit squad-wide — rather than deleting it. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:531`, `shared/src/transfermarket.ts:28`.
- [x] **PT-302 (audit: living squad): [high] a contract that expires has literally no consequence, so "Renew" is a pure coin sink and "Let go" is strictly optimal — and the deal is a season shorter than advertised.** `advanceSquad` computes `isExpiring` (`shared/src/squad.ts:93`) and reports it, but nothing anywhere gates on it: wages are charged for every player on the books regardless (`squad.ts:90`), selection never checks it, and the player never leaves. Because `signedSeason` is untouched, `squadSeasonsLeft` stays 0 and the same man is flagged expiring **every season forever**, taking `contract_lapsed` (−8) + `unused` (−5) annually until his morale clamps at 0 and he sits in the "Unsettled" list permanently. Separately, `nextSeason` calls `spSeasonReward` (which does `model.profile.season = season + 1`, `api.ts:508`) *before* `advanceSquadSeason` reads `profile.season` (`api.ts:521`), and expiry is then checked at `season + 1` — so `SQUAD_CONTRACT_SEASONS = 3` delivers **2** played seasons. why it hurts: the keep-or-lose call the whole Phase-2 contract layer exists to create is fake — paying `squadRenewCost` (0.45× his transfer fee) buys nothing a free player doesn't already have, so an informed player never presses Renew, and the season report grows a permanent list of morale-0 zombies. fix: make expiry bite — a player whose deal lapsed and wasn't renewed leaves the club at the *next* rollover (add him to `retired`-style `departed` in `advanceSquad`), which makes Renew the price of keeping him; and pass the just-played season explicitly into `advanceSquadSeason` (`profile.season - 1`) instead of reading the already-bumped counter. Ref: `shared/src/squad.ts:90,93`, `client/src/api.ts:508,521,550`.
- [x] **PT-303 (audit: living squad): [medium] free trialists are now full sellable squad assets and never expire, so `signTrial` → `sellPlayer` prints coins from nothing.** `signTrial` charges no coins at all (`client/src/api.ts:731-745`) and pushes the loanee into `c.club.players`; `deleteLoaneesInSeason` (`client/src/save.ts:281`) is still dead code with zero callers, so the "expires at season end" contract in `shared/src/scouting.ts:2` never happens. Since Phase 1 those loanees are minted through `generateTrialist` → `mintSquadPlayer`, and `sellPlayer` has no origin check, so each one cashes out at `squadSaleValue`: I measured **214c at tier 8 and 334c at tier 4** per trialist, ×`LOANEE_CAP` 3 a season = up to ~1,000c/season of pure minted coin against an 800c title prize. They also age, draw wages and eventually retire inside the rollover, which is not what a loan is. why it hurts: it is the one genuine coins-from-nothing loop in the mode, and it is more lucrative than winning the league. fix: either call `deleteLoaneesInSeason(String(season))` at the top of `advanceSquadSeason` and strip those ids from `club.players` (restoring the intended season-long loan), or tag loanees with `loan: true` and have `sellPlayer` refuse them. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:731`, `client/src/save.ts:281`, `client/src/api.ts:319`.
- [x] **PT-304 (audit: living squad): [medium] `buyPlayer` builds squad ids from a stripped listing id plus the current squad size, which collides and corrupts the roster.** `const uid = \`bought-${c.club.players.length}-${String(player.id||'p').replace(/[^a-z0-9]/gi,'')}\`` (`client/src/api.ts:311`) strips the separators out of `mk:${season}:${tier}:${i}` (`shared/src/transfermarket.ts:76`), so `mk:1:1:11` and `mk:11:1:1` both collapse to `mk1111` — signing listing #11 in season 1 and listing #1 in season 11 at the same squad size yields the identical id. Two players then share an id: `sellPlayer`'s `filter(x => x.id !== playerId)` (`api.ts:329`) deletes **both** while paying for one, `autoPickXI`'s `used` set can never field the second, and `buildXI`'s `find` silently returns whichever came first. why it hurts: a silent duplicate-id roster is the kind of corruption that shows up seasons later as a vanished signing the player paid 800c for. fix: mint the id from a monotonic counter or the listing's full unstripped key — e.g. `bought-${season}-${tier}-${i}` sanitised with a separator that survives (`replace(/[^a-z0-9]/gi,'-')`) — and assert uniqueness against `c.club.players` before pushing. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:311`, `shared/src/transfermarket.ts:76`.
- [ ] **PT-305 (audit: living squad): [medium] the new mental layer is one-sided — only the manager's own squad has it — which makes the bottom of the pyramid harder and the top flight easier, exactly inverting the intended difficulty curve.** `mintSquadPlayer` rolls composure/aggression/creativity/teamwork/leadership around the club's quality centre (`shared/src/teams.ts:105-119`), but opponent clubs stay on the cheap `rollAttrs` path with those stats undefined, and `shared/src/mental.ts` centres every modifier on 10 — so undefined opponents are permanently neutral. Measured per tier: **tier 10 → mentals ~5.1, finishing `mAdd` −0.025, dribble `mMul` ×0.951, leadership −0.007, 0 traits across 20 players; tier 1 → mentals ~14.1, finishing +0.020, dribble ×1.041, leadership +0.014, 39 traits across 20 players** (the `eligibleTraits` gates only open at high stats). The trait bumps compound it: `hasTrait` bonuses in `engine.ts:420,422,476,562,617` are on top of the stat bump `mintSquadPlayer:129` applies. why it hurts: a Sunday-league side is now systematically *worse* than its neutral-10 opponents while a top-flight side is systematically better — the pyramid climb gets harder at the start and the top flight gets the easy ride, which is the opposite of the calibration `tierStrength`'s own comment claims. fix: mint opponent clubs rich too (`generateClub(..., rich=true)` is already deterministic and they are regenerated per fixture, so nothing persists), or centre the squad's mental roll on 10 rather than on tier quality. Ref: `shared/src/teams.ts:105`, `shared/src/mental.ts:11-13`, `shared/src/teams.ts:141`.
- [ ] **PT-306 (audit: living squad): [low-med] none of the new layer is visible outside the once-a-year season report — the squad table and the player card still show the pre-Living-Squad player.** `statsTableHTML` (`client/src/main.ts:250-256`) hard-codes the same 10 physical/technical columns it always had: no age, no contract, no morale, and none of the five new mental stats or `durability`. `showPlayerCard`'s whole contract/morale block is behind `if (ci)` (`client/src/main.ts:761`), and `ci` is `starContractInfo` — the bloodline star only — so a squad player gets nothing but `· age N` in the card footer (`main.ts:786`). why it hurts: Phase 1-3 gave every squad player an age, a contract, a morale and a mental profile that the match engine really reads, and the manager can only ever see any of it in the one dismissible panel that appears after pressing Next Season — so the squad still reads as a static stat table for 51 weeks of the year. fix: add Age / Deal / Mood columns to `statsTableHTML` (sortable, driven by `p.age`, `squadSeasonsLeft(p, season)` and `moraleEffects(p.morale).label`) and give `showPlayerCard` a squad-player branch that renders the same block from those fields when `ci` is absent. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:250`, `client/src/main.ts:761`.
- [ ] **PT-307 (audit: living squad): [low-med] `releaseSquadPlayer` and `renewSquadPlayer` validate nothing about the contract, and wages are only ever charged at the rollover — so selling or releasing just before Next Season dodges the entire bill.** `releaseSquadPlayer` (`client/src/api.ts:564-571`) checks only MIN_SQUAD: no check that the deal is actually expiring, no `getToken` bloodline guard of the kind `sellPlayer` carries at `api.ts:326`, and no morale or coin consequence. `renewSquadPlayer` (`api.ts:550`) likewise re-signs anyone at any time. Since `advanceSquad` accrues the bill from whoever is on the books at rollover (`shared/src/squad.ts:90`) and nothing else charges wages, offloading the five most expensive players immediately before `nextSeason` and re-signing equivalents from the market afterwards is a straight saving. Also dead here: `moraleEffects(...).sellMult` and `.wantsAway` (`shared/src/morale.ts:36,47`) have no callers — `sellPlayer` uses the flat `squadSaleValue` (`api.ts:327`), so the documented "an unsettled player sells for up to 20% less" squeeze does not exist. why it hurts: the release/renew pair is the player-facing half of the contract system and it enforces none of its own rules; the wage-timing hole rewards exactly the roster-churning the mode is trying to make expensive. fix: gate `releaseSquadPlayer`/`renewSquadPlayer` on `squadSeasonsLeft(p, season) <= 0`, add the `getToken` guard to release, apply `sellMult` in `sellPlayer`, and accrue wages pro-rata at buy/sell time (or snapshot the roster at season start) so a late sale still pays its share. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:564`, `client/src/api.ts:550`, `shared/src/squad.ts:90`, `shared/src/morale.ts:47`.


<!-- Text-repetition audit 2026-08-29 (scope: EVERY user-visible string a career produces — scenario prompts via careerState.story, outcome narration via actWithNarration, life-event/rival/call-up/academy-scare resolutions, chapter recaps, arc prompts+choices+outcomes, coach/draft/offer beats, rival-news ticker and objective HUD). Scale: 40 full 120-turn careers for within-career counts (4,800 play-turns, ~50k captured strings), 25 careers x 1,053 indexed bank lines for per-bank pressure, 12 bloodlines x 5 generations for the dynasty measure, and 200 dynasties x 5 gens (1,000 careers) for arc selection. VERIFIED HEALTHY: whole-PASSAGE uniqueness is strong — 4,688/4,800 distinct scenario prompts (97.7%) and 4,548/4,800 distinct outcome narrations (94.8%); back-to-back identical prompt 0.00% and identical outcome 0.01%; pickByTurn's coprime bump is correct for every one of the 22 live pool sizes at the new 120-turn length (no pool degenerates to a constant index — the PT-104 class of bug is genuinely fixed); the 414-arc library has zero dead arcs (all 414 fired at least once in 1,000 careers) and arc PROMPTS are still 70.1% new text by generation 5. The damage is all at the SENTENCE/BANK level and at the fixed-content surfaces. Findings numbered from PT-400 to stay clear of other agents' blocks. -->

- [x] **PT-400 (audit: text repetition): [high] the chapter-break financial offer is a pure function of `turn`, so all six offer screens are byte-identical in every career, every playthrough and every generation — and one trio repeats three times inside a single career.** `rollOffer(_rng, turn)` ignores the seed entirely (`const at = (arr, salt) => arr[Math.abs(Math.imul(turn + 1, salt) >>> 0) % arr.length]`), and the chapter breaks land on fixed turns 12/28/46/66/86/104, so every player ever sees exactly: t12 `Soccer School | The Local Paper | The One-Club Man`, t28 `Expenses Covered | The Local Paper | The One-Club Man`, then **`The Loyalty Bonus | A Magazine Cover | Back the Project` at t46, t66 AND t86** — identical three times over — and t104 `The Release Clause | Docu-series Deal | The One-Club Man`. Measured: 240 offer narrations across 40 careers produced only **12 distinct strings (5.0% unique)**; that same trio is read 3x per career and 15x across a 5-generation dynasty. why it hurts: this is a *decision* screen, the moment the player is asked to weigh money against development — reading the identical dilemma with the identical wording for the third time in one career (and the fifteenth across the bloodline) turns a character choice into a rote button-press, and it's the single most repeated non-HUD text in the game. fix: salt the index with the career seed (`Math.imul(turn + 1, salt) ^ seed`) so the specific offer varies per career while the money/brand/develop archetype ids stay stable for replay, and enlarge MONEY/BRAND/DEVELOP from 4/4/4 to ~10 each so six draws inside one career can't collide. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:550`.
- [x] **PT-401 (audit: text repetition): [high] a story-arc eligibility blackout across turns ~30–56 leaves the entire Scholar + Youth Team stretch drawing from 14–23 of the 414 arcs, so two triumph arcs fire in ~half of ALL careers.** Eligible-arc counts by turn: t=12 → 186, t=24 → 104, **t=32 → 14, t=36 → 18, t=40 → 19, t=44 → 23, t=48 → 27**, t=60 → 94, t=96 → 194. 211 of 414 arcs have `maxTurn < 32` and 158 have `minTurn > 56` — windows written for the old ~200-turn career and never rescaled to 120. In that trough the `triumph` category has only **2** members, and the category-balanced pick (`sqrt(2)=1.41` weight vs `sqrt(17)=4.12` for relationship) still hands it ~25% of those slots: over 1,000 simulated careers **`tri-first-senior-goal` fires in 49% of them and `tri-young-player` in 43%**, and by generation 5 **31.7% of a career's 20 arcs have already been lived by an earlier heir** (gen2 9.7%, gen3 18.4%, gen4 23.9%). Roughly 5 of the 20 arc slots sit in this hole. why it hurts: arcs are the biggest, most memorable prose blocks in the game and the explicit answer to "no two playthroughs feel the same" — having the same "first senior goal" story in every other career, and a third of gen-5's storyline already seen, is exactly the fantasy break the 203→414 expansion was meant to buy off. fix: rescale windows for the 120-turn career — push youth-arc `maxTurn` up (many sit at 26–30) and pull senior-arc `minTurn` down (many sit at 57–63) so turns 30–56 see 80+ eligible arcs with at least 4 categories represented; separately, guard the category pick so a category with <4 eligible members is down-weighted rather than given a full share. Ref: `shared/src/storyarc.ts:80` (`pickArcStart`), `shared/src/storyarcs/youth_*.ts`, `shared/src/storyarcs/triumph.ts`.
- [x] **PT-402 (audit: text repetition): [high] `PERSONALITY_ADV` is a 3-line bank drawn ~47 times per career with raw `rng()`, so one four-word phrase is read 20–25 times in a single sitting.** The adverb is spliced into the outcome sentence on a 50% roll of every `narratePlay` turn — measured **47.3 firings per 120-turn career (max 57)** across a bank of exactly 3 entries per temperament, and it uses `pick()` (a fresh random draw), NOT the `pickByTurn` no-repeat walk every other flavour surface uses. Worst observed inside ONE career: **"he, ice in his veins," 25x**, "he, lungs burning," 24x, "he, cracking a smile," 23x, "he, jaw tight," 23x, "he, as drilled," 21x, "he, grinning," 19x. It sits mid-sentence in the most-read surface in the game (one per turn, 120 turns). why it hurts: this is the game's mechanism for *feeling* the player's temperament rather than stating it, and at 20+ identical insertions per career it does the opposite — it reads as a template stamp and makes every outcome sentence look machine-assembled. fix: enlarge each of the 13 `PERSONALITY_ADV` banks from 3 lines to ~10, and route the pick through `pickByTurn(bank, turn, 5, salt)` so consecutive turns walk the pool instead of birthday-clustering. Ref: `shared/src/narrate.ts:128`.
- [x] **PT-403 (audit: text repetition): [high] the scenario prompt's cast-attribution closer is a 4-line bank fired on ~40% of turns — ~47 times per career, so each line lands 12–16 times.** `scenarioStory` appends a character line on `rng() < 0.4`, choosing from exactly four options (senior: `${mentor} reckons this is the making of him.` / `${gaffer} wants to see how he handles it.` / `${captain} is looking his way.` / `Beat ${rival} to it and the point is made.`; the child variant is likewise 4). Measured **46.8 firings per career (max 56)**; inside one career **"Redmond wants to see how he handles it." appeared 16x**, "Beat De Groot to it and the point is made." 14x, "captain Ellery is looking his way." 12x, "Grizzled Marcus Reid reckons this is the making of him." 12x. The `narratePlay` twin (`castReact`, also 4 lines, 25% gate, also raw `pick()`) added **"One in the eye for Lindqvist." 13x** in one career. why it hurts: this is the *last* sentence of the prompt the player reads before every decision — the highest-salience position on the screen — and the recurring cast is supposed to be the thing that makes a career feel personal, so hearing the same four sentences about them 47 times makes the cast feel like a variable substitution rather than people. fix: enlarge both the senior and child charline banks from 4 to ~14 lines each, and the `narratePlay` `castReact` bank from 4 to ~12 (and switch it from `pick()` to `pickByTurn`). Ref: `shared/src/narrate.ts:381`, `shared/src/narrate.ts:486`.
- [ ] **PT-404 (audit: text repetition): [medium] across a 5-generation dynasty, 45%→58% of every sentence the player reads is text they have already read in an earlier generation, even though whole passages stay fresh.** Measured over 12 bloodlines × 5 generations, prose surfaces only (story / narration / arc prompt+choice+outcome / recap / coach / draft / offer): share of sentences already seen in an earlier generation = **gen2 45.1%, gen3 51.8%, gen4 56.7%, gen5 58.2%**; whole-passage repeats stay low (5.4% → 13.6%) and arc prompts stay 70.1% new. Within a *single* career the same split shows up: 1,256 sentences of which only 717 are distinct — **42.9% of sentences are a repeat of something already read this career**. why it hurts: the recombination is doing its job at paragraph level but the player doesn't experience paragraphs, they experience sentences — by the third heir more than half of every screen is déjà vu, which is precisely the failure mode that makes a dynasty feel like a spreadsheet with prose stapled on. fix: no single change — this is the aggregate of PT-402/403/405/406; enlarging those five sub-8-line banks to 10–14 lines each is the highest-leverage move, and re-running this measurement should be the acceptance test (target: gen5 under ~35%). Ref: `shared/src/narrate.ts`, `tools/playtest/analyze_text_repetition.ts`.
- [x] **PT-405 (audit: text repetition): [medium] three of the thirteen `PERSONALITY` closer banks have 4 lines where the other ten have 8, and those three players' careers are measurably more repetitive.** `hothead`, `perfectionist` and `joker` carry 4 lines each; `maverick`/`fragile`/`leader`/`biggame`/`workhorse`/`mercurial`/`pro`/`latebloom`/`showman`/`stoic` carry 8. Measured worst per-line count inside one career: **"It could go off at any second, and everyone knows it." 17x**, "He keeps it light, whatever the score." 16x, "The dressing room's favourite." 15x, "Never satisfied, this one." 15x, "He'll pick holes in it later, whatever anyone says." 15x, "The fuse is always short." 15x — against a max of 13x for any 8-line temperament ("The bigger the stage, the more he shows up."). Personality is rolled from the seed, so ~23% of all bloodline heirs draw one of the three thin banks. why it hurts: temperament is the player's main sense of *who this heir is as a person* and it's inherited-feeling across the dynasty; a hothead son who says the same four things his hothead grandfather said reads as a bug, and the asymmetry means a quarter of players get a visibly worse version of the same feature. fix: pad `hothead`, `perfectionist` and `joker` from 4 to at least 8 lines to match their siblings (and ideally take all thirteen to ~12, given the 120-turn length). Ref: `shared/src/narrate.ts:123`.
- [x] **PT-406 (audit: text repetition): [medium] two more sub-6-line banks sit on very high-traffic surfaces: `TAG_TRIUMPH` (2 lines per tag) and `LIFE_APPROACH` (5 lines, and it opens the sentence).** `TAG_TRIUMPH` replaces the generic result on 55% of triumphs and offers a straight coin-flip between exactly two lines per tag — measured **"and it was a goal built by six players and finished by one" 12x in one career**, "on legs that had no right to carry him" 9x, "and the whole midfield bent to his will" 9x, "and it split the pitch open like a scalpel" 9x, "and grown adults gasped" 8x. `LIFE_APPROACH` (`He falls back on` / `It's` / `He leans on` / `What gets him through it is` / `He draws on`) prefixes **every** life-event and every `social`-kind resolution — measured **25.3 firings per career (max 33)** over 5 lines, with "He leans on" hitting 11x and "What gets him through it is" 9x in one career; because it is the opening words of the paragraph it is the most conspicuous possible position. The companion `LIFE_RESOLUTION.social` good/bad banks are 4 lines each and fired 16.0x per career, worst line 11x. why it hurts: `TAG_TRIUMPH` exists so a flair triumph reads unlike a keeping triumph — at 2 lines it instead becomes a recognisable tell that the game has run out of ways to praise you; and a repeated *opening* clause makes consecutive off-pitch beats look like a form letter. fix: `TAG_TRIUMPH` 2 → ~6 lines per tag (8 tags, ~32 new lines); `LIFE_APPROACH` 5 → ~14; `LIFE_RESOLUTION.social.good/bad` 4 → ~10 each (it is the catch-all for every non-life `social` turn, so it carries far more traffic than the per-kind banks beside it). Ref: `shared/src/narrate.ts:73`, `shared/src/narrate.ts:516`, `shared/src/narrate.ts:531`.
- [ ] **PT-407 (audit: text repetition): [low-med] the always-on HUD strings — the rival-news ticker and the stage objective — are the numerically most-repeated text in the game, and neither has enough copy for a 120-turn stage.** `rivalNews` draws from a **6-line** per-chapter bank and is rendered on every single one of the 120 turns: measured 42 distinct headlines seen per career (the whole 7×6 library), i.e. **each headline is on screen ~2.9 times**, and within a single 20-turn chapter its 6 lines cycle 3.3 times — e.g. "is on the fringes of a first-team squad now." recurs every 6 turns for the whole Youth Team stage. The season objective is worse but more defensible: the same label persists all stage, so **"Rise to the occasion in 2 big-game moments" was captured 54 times in one career** and "Turn in 6 strong displays this stage" 34x. why it hurts: the rival is the game's main comparative motivation and his "parallel career" is meant to feel like it's actually moving; a six-headline loop makes it read as a placeholder ticker instead. The objective label is a legitimately persistent HUD element, but at 120 turns and 17 distinct strings across 40 careers it is the single most-read sentence in the product. fix: enlarge each `RIVAL_NEWS` chapter bank from 6 to ~14 lines (7 chapters, ~56 new lines) so a chapter's worth of turns doesn't cycle it; for the objective, consider showing the label in full only on the first turn of a stage and a compact progress form (`3/6 strong displays`) thereafter. Ref: `shared/src/narrate.ts:762`, `shared/src/tokens.ts:370`.

<!-- UX & ONBOARDING audit 2026-08-29 (scope: client/src/main.ts + client/index.html + client/src/api.ts, read-only; inventoried every mechanic a new player must grasp against every explainer/hint/coach-mark in the app — grep'd onbKey/help/tut/hint/explain/first + all 41 title="" attributes). ALREADY WELL HANDLED, credit where due: the one-time "📖 HOW HIS CAREER WORKS" card (main.ts:2374-2388) covers moments/tags, energy, money+club cut, meters and the rival in plain English; the chapter-1 contextual coach-marks (tutorialHint, main.ts:2352-2371) are per-save (onbKey, main.ts:2350 — PT-11) and correctly self-suppress for gen>0 and while the big explainer is still up; the first-manager card (managerHelpCard, main.ts:1297-1307) exists at all and names the star→club link; the agent pick is explicitly de-risked ("No wrong pick here", main.ts:2320); the draft prompt states rarity=power and that unpicked cards are discarded (main.ts:2569); the summer screen is a deliberate two-step with an affordability lock and a self-building icon legend (main.ts:2571-2600); continental qualification gets a real in-place explainer on round 0 (main.ts:1490); pedigree is defined on the academy board (main.ts:2183); the XI validator warns (not blocks) on no-GK/out-of-position (main.ts:3115); and the genuinely destructive things — delete save, quit mid-match, sell/buy/renew/release, sell the star, forfeit a cup — all route through openConfirm (main.ts:383/506/1200/1214/1273/1286/1421/1898). Findings below are the gaps left. -->

- [x] **PT-500 (audit: UX & onboarding): [high] the squad WAGE BILL — a recurring, save-wide coin drain the player can go bankrupt-adjacent on — is never mentioned anywhere until it has already been charged.** `squadSeasonRollover` charges `roll.wageBill` at every season rollover (`client/src/api.ts:529-539`), clamping to whatever coins exist and reporting an `unpaid` remainder; the ONLY player-facing surface is the retrospective bill row inside the dismissible squad report (`client/src/main.ts:1265-1266`, "−Xc paid for the season · Yc unpaid — the books are stretched"). `managerHelpCard` (`main.ts:1297-1307`) tells the player to spend coins on transfers and facilities and never says a wage bill is coming, and the transfer-market confirm actively teaches the opposite — "(no wage — he's yours outright)" (`main.ts:1200`) — so a new manager reasonably concludes squads are free to run. why it hurts: the player spends every coin on facilities/staff in season 1, then gets an unexplained multi-thousand-coin deduction and an "unpaid" warning for a cost no screen ever forecast; there is no way to plan against an invisible liability. fix: add a wage bullet to `managerHelpCard` ("Your squad draws wages every season — keep a reserve"), and render a live `💷 wage bill ~Xc/season` figure in the season header next to the coin count (`main.ts:1343`) and on the squad panel, so the liability is visible *before* rollover. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:531`, `client/src/main.ts:1265`.
- [x] **PT-501 (audit: UX & onboarding): [high] squad MORALE silently controls what every renewal costs and every sale earns, and neither what drives it nor what it does is ever stated on screen.** `moraleEffects` (`shared/src/morale.ts:41-48`) applies `extendMult` up to **+30%** on renew cost and `sellMult` down to **−20%** on sale value, and `squadMoraleAfterSeason` (`shared/src/squad.ts:47-55`) drives morale from exactly three things the manager controls or observes — being in the XI, winning something, and letting a contract lapse. The UI exposes morale only as a coloured dot with `title="morale: <label>"` (`main.ts:834`), a bar on the player card (`main.ts:765`), an italic label beside a Renew button (`main.ts:1252`) and one line of the squad report — "they'll hold out for more to re-sign" (`main.ts:1261-1263`). Nothing anywhere says *benching a player makes him more expensive to keep*. why it hurts: this is the whole newly-added Living Squad decision loop, and a new player experiences it as renew prices that inexplicably differ between two similar players; the one lever that would let them play it well (rotate the fringe men into the XI) is invisible. fix: add a "🙂 Morale" bullet to `managerHelpCard` naming the three drivers and the two effects, and put the cause inline on the Renew button's own row — e.g. "unsettled · +25% to re-sign (he barely played)" — rather than in a tooltip. Ref: `shared/src/morale.ts:46`, `client/src/main.ts:1252`.
- [x] **PT-502 (audit: UX & onboarding): [high] the ENTIRE tactics and duty vocabulary exists only inside `title="..."` attributes — invisible on touch, undiscoverable on desktop — so on a tablet the tactics screen is eight unlabelled jargon dropdowns.** `TAC_NOTE` holds genuinely good coaching explanations for mentality/line/press/tempo/width but is attached solely as `<label title="...">` (`main.ts:3041`, notes at `main.ts:109-115`); the offside trap's "needs high line" precondition (`main.ts:3046`), Play Out From Back (`main.ts:3047`) and Attack Focus (`main.ts:3049`) are likewise tooltip-only; and `DUTY_DESC` — the only definition of what a duty *is* — lives in `title` on both the `<option>` and the `<select>` (`main.ts:3100`, `main.ts:3110`). Visible text is only the raw option labels: "Gegenpress", "Very Deep", "Very Direct". why it hurts: this is the single biggest literacy cliff in the game and the guidance is already written — it is just hidden behind hover, so a touch player gets none of it and a mouse player must discover that hovering a label does anything. fix: surface it — render `TAC_NOTE[k]` as a small caption under each slider (or behind one visible "ⓘ What do these do?" disclosure on the lineup panel, `index.html:1458`), and render `DUTY_DESC` of the *currently selected* duty as visible text in the slot rather than as the select's tooltip. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:3041`, `client/src/main.ts:3100`, `client/index.html:1458`.
- [ ] **PT-503 (audit: UX & onboarding): [high] the lineup/tactics editor is the game's hardest screen and it opens fully expanded, one click after a player whose entire experience is "choose 1 of 4 cards".** `#lineup` (`client/index.html:1455-1471`) renders, all at once and with no staging: a scout card, `tac-row` (formation + 5 sliders + 2 checkboxes + attack focus = 9 controls, `main.ts:3038-3049`), a squad-insight line, the Match Plan rule list (`main.ts:3021-3027`), 11 XI slots each with a player select, a duty select, up to 4 captain/set-piece toggle buttons and badges (`main.ts:3110`), a bench, a full sortable stats table behind a toggle, then Auto-pick / Save / Back. `renderHandoff` (`main.ts:2443-2456`) drops a first-time player straight from the card career into this, and "Auto-pick best XI" — the one safe escape — sits last in the action row (`index.html:1467`). why it hurts: ~40 interactive controls with no default path is the classic point where a player who loved the card game bounces out of the manager game entirely. fix: on the first N matchdays collapse `tac-row` and `#match-plan` behind an "⚙ Advanced tactics" disclosure (defaults are already sane), promote Auto-pick to a primary "✅ Pick my best XI" above the slots, and fire a one-time coach-mark on the first lineup open ("Auto-pick is fine to start — tweak tactics when you want to"). Ref: `client/index.html:1455-1471`, `client/src/main.ts:3038`.
- [x] **PT-504 (audit: UX & onboarding): [high] both onboarding explainers are dismiss-forever with no way to re-read them, and Settings has no "How to play" — so one accidental tap on "Got it ✕" permanently deletes the game's only explanation of its core loop.** `careerHelpCard` returns `''` whenever `fm_career_help_done` is set (`main.ts:2374`) and `managerHelpCard` likewise on `fm_mgr_help_done` (`main.ts:1298`); both are cleared only by their own dismiss handlers (`main.ts:2626`, `main.ts:1408`) and there is no re-open path anywhere. `openSettings` (`main.ts:417-443`) offers music, SFX, reduced motion, CRT, UI scale and the hide-card-stats challenge — but no help/rules entry, and no other screen carries an ⓘ. The manager card is also the *only* explanation of promotion/relegation, and it is shown exactly once per save. why it hurts: the "Got it ✕" button is small and sits next to the content the player wants to read; one mis-tap and the rules of a 120-turn-plus-dynasty game are gone for that save with no recovery, and a player returning after a week away cannot refresh on how anything works. fix: add a "📖 How to play" row to the Settings dialog that clears both `fm_*_help_done` keys (or better, opens a read-only combined version of both cards), and keep a small persistent ⓘ in the career and season headers. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2374`, `client/src/main.ts:1298`, `client/src/main.ts:417`.
- [x] **PT-505 (audit: UX & onboarding): [medium] "⏩ Sim the rest of the season" irreversibly consumes every remaining fixture on a single unconfirmed click, while far cheaper, more reversible actions all get a confirm dialog.** The button is rendered unstyled and inline beneath the fixture list (`main.ts:1373`), wired directly to `simRemainingFixtures` (`main.ts:1412`), which loops every unplayed fixture, pushes each generated result into the save and persists via `saveMgr` (`main.ts:1776-1789`) — no confirm, no undo, and it can vaporise 20+ matchdays of the game's headline content including a title run-in. Compare `openConfirm` guarding selling one player (`main.ts:1214`), releasing one player (`main.ts:1286`) and even quitting mid-match (`main.ts:506`). why it hurts: a new player who reads it as "sim this match" (it sits directly under a fixture row with a per-match "Play ▶" button) loses their whole season in one tap and cannot get it back. fix: route it through `openConfirm` naming the count — "Sim the remaining N fixtures? You won't play them." — and reword the label to "⏩ Sim the remaining N matches". Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1373`, `client/src/main.ts:1412`.
- [ ] **PT-506 (audit: UX & onboarding): [medium] "stake / unstake" is leftover web3 vocabulary for a squad-registration mechanic, is never defined, and unstaking — which makes a player ineligible to play — is unconfirmed.** The player card renders "⭘ not staked — <a>stake to make eligible</a>" / "🔒 staked N seasons — loyalty discount · <a>unstake</a>" (`main.ts:755-757`), the stars panel shows a bare `Stake` button and an "idle" tag (`main.ts:831-836`), and `stakePlayer` fires immediately on click with only a toast — "Unstaked — now idle" (`main.ts:843-847`). Nothing on screen says what staking *is*, what "idle" costs him, or what the "loyalty discount" applies to; web3 was removed from the design, so the term now maps to nothing the player can reason about. why it hurts: it is unglossed internal vocabulary attached to a one-click action that can quietly bench a player, and a new player has no model for why a man in his squad is "idle". fix: rename in the UI to registration language ("✅ Registered for the season" / "Not registered — he can't be picked", action "Register him"), state the tenure benefit in words next to it, and wrap the un-register path in `openConfirm`. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:755`, `client/src/main.ts:843`.
- [ ] **PT-507 (audit: UX & onboarding): [medium] facility upgrades and backroom-staff hires spend hundreds of coins on an unconfirmed single click, and what they actually do to a match is stated nowhere in player terms.** `.fac-up` (`main.ts:2131`) and `.sf-hire` (`main.ts:1782-1784`) both act immediately — the only guard is an affordability `disabled` (PT-126) — whereas buying an equivalent-cost player is confirmed (`main.ts:1200`). The real effects live only in code: Training Ground cuts fitness drain and Fan Zone adds a home edge (`main.ts:2085-2089`), staff add conditioning/attack multipliers (`main.ts:2090-2093`), and Training Ground level also silently accelerates squad youth development (`shared/src/squad.ts:66-70`); the UI shows only a terse `nextEffect` string and a one-line staff `desc`. why it hurts: these are the manager economy's main coin sinks and a new player buys them blind, cannot compare them against a transfer, and can mis-click away a season's prize money with no confirm step. fix: put both purchases behind `openConfirm` with the cost, and expand the facility/staff cards to state the effect in plain terms ("Training Ground L3 → squad tires ~10% slower, and your youngsters develop faster"). Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2131`, `client/src/main.ts:1782`, `client/src/main.ts:2085`.
- [x] **PT-508 (audit: UX & onboarding): [medium] how card FIT is scored — the single most-repeated decision in a 120-turn career — is only ever described by tooltips and by a legend that disappears whenever the moment demands one tag.** The demand pills carry their meaning in `title` ("Best match — a card with this tag reads the moment perfectly" / "Also helps — a partial match", `main.ts:2515`), and `demandHint` only renders the visible green/amber decode when `demandTags.length > 1`, degrading to "— play a card that matches" for single-tag moments (`main.ts:2516`). The choice cards' own tag pills carry no explanation at all (`main.ts:2666`), and the outcome chips label the *result* ("Your card choice" / "How the moment went", `main.ts:2721-2722`) again only in `title`. Nothing states whether one primary-tag match beats two secondary ones, or that rarity multiplies a good fit (only said once, in the draft prompt, `main.ts:2569`). why it hurts: the player makes this choice ~120 times per generation with a colour code they must infer, on touch devices with zero tooltip access, and can play a whole career without learning that the green pill is worth more than the amber ones. fix: make the green/amber legend unconditional (render it for single-tag demands too) and add a persistent one-line rule under the hand — "Match the green tag for the best result; amber tags help; rarer cards develop him more when they fit". Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2515`, `client/src/main.ts:2516`.
- [ ] **PT-509 (audit: UX & onboarding): [medium] at succession the game silently tears down the entire manager layer and drops the player back into ~120 turns of card play, and no screen warns that this is about to happen or that the club survives it.** `bringThroughHeir` calls `this.clearMgr()` (`main.ts:1975`) then `showProspectCard(r.prospect)` — the XI, tactics, fixtures, board and league position all vanish from the UI in one step. The retirement/will screen sells the inheritance well (`Game.WILL` blurbs, `main.ts:1891-1895`) but says nothing about the structural consequence: that the player will not manage again until the heir reaches the handoff, and that the club's tier and squad *are* preserved (`fm_tier_`/club state persist independently of `clearMgr`). why it hurts: a player who has spent many seasons building a top-flight club sees it disappear at the moment of their biggest emotional investment and has no way to know it is coming back — this reads as losing progress. fix: state it on the will screen and in the succession toast — "You'll live [heir]'s career from age 10; [club] keeps its division, squad and trophies, and you take the reins again when he breaks into the first team." Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1975`, `client/src/main.ts:1891`.
- [ ] **PT-510 (audit: UX & onboarding): [low-med] the relationship METERS are explained once, generically, in an explainer that is permanently dismissed before three of the eight meters ever appear.** `careerHelpCard` names only "Coach, family, teammates, school, agent" (`main.ts:2381`) and describes their effect in the aggregate ("a strong meter unlocks better summer opportunities"), but `METER_NAME` also carries **fans**, **sponsors** and **partner** (`main.ts:41`) — the stage-aware senior-career set that, per `lifeDashHtml`'s own comment, replaces the youth meters as he ages (`main.ts:2335`). By then `fm_career_help_done` is long set, so those three arrive with no introduction and only a `title="<label>: <value>/100"` tooltip (`main.ts:2338`). Nothing ever says what any *individual* meter gates. why it hurts: the dashboard is the career screen's main state display, and half of it appears mid-run unexplained, with per-meter meaning available only on hover. fix: fire a small one-line coach-mark the first time a *new* meter appears ("📣 Fans — the crowd's patience with him; it steadies his form"), and give each meter a visible one-word purpose rather than a tooltip. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2381`, `client/src/main.ts:41`, `client/src/main.ts:2338`.
- [x] **PT-511 (audit: UX & onboarding): [low-med] PRESTIGE is displayed as a headline number with a rank title but the game never says what it is for — that it is the input deciding what the board demands of you each season.** `refreshPrestige`/`showPrestigeCard` (`main.ts:951-958`, `main.ts:2085-2096`) render a score, an icon, a gaffer title, a progress bar toward the next title and the footer "earned across your whole managerial career" — purely descriptive. Its actual function is upstream of the board: `nextSeason` feeds `prestige.levelIdx` into `deriveExpectation` (`main.ts:1801-1804`), which is what produces the "This season they expect …" line (`main.ts:1382`). Relatedly, "OVR" is used on the season header, every XI slot, the bench and the squad table (`main.ts:1340`, `3110`, `3150`) and is never expanded once, unlike the eight per-stat abbreviations which do get `STAT_FULL` tooltips (`main.ts:2641`). why it hurts: the player sees prestige climb and cannot tell whether it is a score, a reward or a difficulty setting — and it is in fact the third, quietly raising the bar they are judged against. fix: add one line to the prestige card ("The more you've won, the more the board expects — prestige sets next season's target"), and expand OVR to "Overall" on first use in the season header. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2085`, `client/src/main.ts:1804`.
- [x] **PT-512 (audit: UX & onboarding): [low] the board issues a season target in the language of a hard demand, never says what missing it costs, and then removes the target from the screen after matchday 3.** `boardLine` renders "🪑 The board — on last season: '…' This season they expect [target]" but is gated on `!done && nextIdx <= 3` (`main.ts:1381-1384`), so it vanishes for the remaining ~15+ matchdays of the season it governs. In fact `shared/src/board.ts:4` states the system is deliberately consequence-free ("no sacking, no forced game-overs, no persisted state") — but nothing on screen tells the player that, so the demand reads as a live job-security threat. why it hurts: a new player is handed an authoritative-sounding objective, cannot check it after matchday 3, and spends the season unsure whether missing it ends the run — the worst of both worlds (anxiety plus no reference). fix: keep the expectation persistently visible as a compact chip in the season header for the whole season (with live "on track / falling short" framing against the current table position), and soften the copy to make the stake explicit — the board is judging the story, not your job. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1382`, `shared/src/board.ts:4`.
