<!-- Browser full-loop pass 2026-08-29b (fresh "Brandt" NEW GAME, founding prospect Luca, Super-Agent): played turn 0 → 37/120, Grassroots → Academy → Scholar (age 10→16), resolved TWO complete between-season sequences (spend → summer focus → side activity → money-vs-development → mentor → card draft), hit 8 story arcs incl. 5 childhood ones, and toured the Academy/Hub/Club-Facilities screens; did NOT reach Youth Team/Breakthrough or the manager handoff (that is ~turn 90+). VERIFIED GOOD: zero JS console errors across ~45 interactions; PT-144 fixed (Back the Club now shows "🔒 need 80c more"); PT-111/107 hold (BIG GAME occasions "A Point to Prove", "A Scout-Packed Showcase"); PT-141 holds (Brandt player / Okonkwo coach); the new CHILDHOOD arcs are the best writing in the game by a distance — "THE BOTTLE HE NEVER FILLS", "THE NEW KIT", "FORTY SECONDS, ROUND THE WHOLE YEAR" and "THE SUMMER HE GOT QUICK" are specific, multi-beat, genuinely age-11-to-14 and land emotionally; arc cadence (~1 per 5-7 turns) feels right; the summer spend/focus screens are legible and kid-scale at Grassroots/Academy; Club Facilities is well explained. OVERALL: the arcs are compelling but the CARD LOOP is not yet fun — 33 of 37 turns resolved "🎯 Right card · ⭐ Brilliant", the rival gap only ever grew (0 → ▲159), and roughly a third of moments deal a hand that cannot match the required green tag at all, so neither success nor failure is earned. Fix the difficulty curve and the tag/hand contract and this is a good game. -->
- [x] **PT-149 (stage: full-loop browser): [high] a full romance arc — with an adult woman, framed in adult-relationship terms — fires on a 15-year-old scholar.** At age 16 (turn 31/120, Scholar) the arc "💞 SOMEONE WORTH COMING HOME TO" opened: "She works the coffee cart near the training ground and hasn't the faintest idea who he is — which, frankly, is the most attractive thing about her… Does he ask?" with the option "Ask her out — Fumble the words, mean every one of them". Beat two then read "Three months in and she asks him, gently, why he never switches off — why the phone's out at dinner, why the analysis clips play in bed", resolving to "She's become his ground wire", with the option "Bury himself in the tie — Love can wait; the manager can't". why it hurts: this is a 15-year-old boy being written into a live-in-shaped relationship with an adult who works near his training ground — it is the single hardest age-tone line in the game to defend, it is unskippable once drawn, and one screenshot of it is a store-page problem. fix: gate the whole romance arc family behind Breakthrough+ (18+) the way LIFE_KINDS were gated by PT-134; if a teen version is wanted, write a separate school-age arc that stops at "someone he can't stop thinking about" and never reaches the cohabiting-couple beats. Ref: shared/src/storyarcs (relationship/romance arc "Someone Worth Coming Home To"), Scholar chapter.
- [x] **PT-150 (stage: full-loop browser): [high] roughly a third of moments deal a hand that contains no card matching the GREEN required tag, so the core skill decision is unplayable.** Turn 8 (LIFE EVENT, green flair) dealt Composure/Creativity/Teamwork/Teamwork+Composure; turn 10 (green stamina) dealt Composure/Teamwork/Teamwork+Composure/Creativity; turn 19 (green flair) dealt Composure/Composure/Teamwork/Aggression; turn 23 (TRAINING GROUND, green teamwork) dealt Creativity/Flair+Composure/Creativity/Composure; turn 27 (green aggression) dealt Creativity/Composure/Flair/Teamwork+Composure. The prompt still says "green is the best match, amber also helps", so the UI promises a decision the deal cannot honour. It gets worse over time: the drafts grow the deck 7 → 11 → 14 cards while the hand stays at 4, so the odds of holding the green tag fall every chapter. why it hurts: this is the one decision the whole card loop is built on — when it is a coin flip whether the correct answer was even dealt, skilled play stops being rewarded and the loop reads as slot-machine text. fix: guarantee at least one green-tag card in every dealt hand (draw the green match first, then fill the remaining slots at random), or draw the moment's required tag from the tags actually present in the dealt hand. Ref: the moment/hand dealer (client card draw + shared moment generation), all chapters.
- [x] **PT-151 (stage: full-loop browser): [high] there is effectively no failure state — 33 of 37 turns resolved "🎯 Right card · ⭐ Brilliant" and the rival gap grew monotonically from 0 to ▲159, so nothing is ever at stake.** Playing only "pick the green tag, else the amber one", the outcome banner read ⭐ Brilliant on 33 turns, ✓ Solid once and ◦ Unlucky twice; ★ climbed 0 → 307 without a single reversal. The rival ticker went ▲0 → ▲4 → ▲16 → … → ▲159 and never once narrowed, while its news lines ("Turner has been fast-tracked up an age group") claim he is thriving. Both stage objectives (4 strong displays, then 10, then 6) completed early and then sat on "✓ complete" for 6+ turns with nothing to replace them. why it hurts: at 120 turns the career is meant to be a tense climb; instead 37 turns produced zero setbacks, the "🆚 RIVAL" panel is decoration rather than pressure, and the objective panel goes dead for a third of each chapter. fix: make Brilliant a genuinely uncommon result (scale success odds against the moment's difficulty, chapter and energy so a right-card play is ~Solid by default), let the rival actually gain ground on weak turns, and roll a new stage objective when one completes. Ref: outcome resolution + rival simulation + stage objective panel.
- [x] **PT-152 (stage: full-loop browser): [high] the same stock narration clauses repeat several times per chapter — "for the fifth time that half" landed SEVEN times in 37 turns.** Exact string "he, for the fifth time that half," appeared in the outcome prose on turns 2, 3, 6, 18, 26, 30 and 33 — including on a one-off set piece ("he, for the fifth time that half, invented Set-Piece Specialist") where it is nonsense. Also repeating verbatim: "You could set a clock by his work rate." (x3), "grown adults gasped" (x3), "One the whole school will hear about on Monday." (x3), "The parents on the touchline cheered." (x3), and in setups "The five-a-side cage after school, where reputations are made and lost in an afternoon." (x3), "Oranges at the break and a team-talk from a dad who means well" (x3), "A proper pitch with real nets for once" (x3), "His mum's watching from the touchline." (x4). why it hurts: 414 arcs is a huge content investment, but the per-turn prose is what the player reads 120 times, and a first-timer notices the same nine sentences inside one chapter — it makes the whole text layer feel procedurally thin. fix: track recently-used clauses per save and exclude the last N draws from each bank (openers, effort-tics, reaction lines, settings), and delete or condition "for the fifth time that half" so it can only attach to repeat-effort card kinds. Ref: shared/src/narrate.ts (opener / effort-tic / reaction / SETTINGS banks).
- [x] **PT-153 (stage: full-loop browser): [medium] the game repeatedly offers to sign an agent the player already signed on turn 0.** I signed the Super-Agent during onboarding at age 10 and the 🤝 AGENT meter has been in the HUD ever since. At the age-15 summer screen the focus list still offered "🤝 Sign With an Agent — Someone to fight your corner as the offers start to whisper. ⚡-6 🤝+20", and on turn 33 a BIG GAME setup read "An agent's number saved in his phone for the first time, unsure whether to call it." why it hurts: two separate surfaces contradict a choice the player made in the tutorial and have been staring at in the HUD for 33 turns — it is the clearest "the game isn't tracking me" signal in the run, and it devalues the 11-agent onboarding choice. fix: filter the "Sign With an Agent" focus out when `career.agent` is set, and gate the no-agent-yet setup lines on the same flag. Ref: summer focus bank + matchday setup bank (shared/src/offpitch.ts, shared/src/narrate.ts).
- [ ] **PT-154 (stage: full-loop browser): [medium] two of the seven tracked relationship meters (🏠 Family and ❤️ Partner) are never shown in the HUD, yet spend tiles and summer focuses pay into them.** The age-15 summer screen prints its own legend "⚡ energy · 🤝 Agent · 🧑‍🏫 Coach · 👥 Teammates · 🎒 School · 🏠 Family · ❤️ Partner" — seven meters — while the footer HUD on the same screen shows only ENERGY, COACH, TEAMMATES, SCHOOL, AGENT. "🎁 Treat Your Parents 💷300c 🏠+14", "🚦 Driving Lessons 💷420c ❤️+4" and "🛏️ Nicer Digs ❤️+4" all sell increases to meters the player cannot see; I bought Driving Lessons for 420c and no ❤️ meter appeared. The 🏠 PARENTS meter was visible at Grassroots/Academy and silently disappeared at Scholar when AGENT took its slot. why it hurts: the tutorial explicitly teaches that "a neglected one (near empty) drags his development and can spark off-pitch trouble" — so the player is being asked to manage, and pay real coins into, two hidden numbers that can punish him invisibly. fix: show all tracked meters (wrap to a second row, or make the meter strip scrollable/expandable) and never let a meter that has non-zero effects drop out of the HUD. Ref: HUD meter strip (client/src/main.ts) vs summer spend/focus effect legend.
- [ ] **PT-155 (stage: full-loop browser): [medium] PT-147 is not fixed — a labelled BIG GAME still resolves with an unrelated fixture opener, twice in six turns.** Turn 29 was "⚽ MATCHDAY · BIG GAME · 🏟️ Home · A Point to Prove" and resolved "In a white-hot derby, he … coolly executed Ice in the Veins". Turn 33 was "⚽ MATCHDAY · BIG GAME · 🏟️ Home · A Scout-Packed Showcase" and resolved "On Community Shield curtain-raiser day, he refused to stop for Up and Down" — a Community Shield for a 15-year-old scholar. PT-147 is marked [x] but the opener bank is still drawn without reference to the occasion label the card printed one line earlier. why it hurts: the two halves of the same card contradict each other in the same breath, and "Community Shield" hands a schoolboy a senior showpiece fixture — the exact deflation PT-12/PT-109 were written to prevent. fix: when the moment carries an `occasion` label, reuse that label verbatim in the opener instead of drawing from SETTINGS, and hard-gate senior-competition openers (Community Shield, derby, six-pointer) behind First Team+. Ref: shared/src/narrate.ts narratePlay opener/SETTINGS pools.
- [x] **PT-156 (stage: full-loop browser): [medium] the outcome prose sometimes names a completely different card from the one played, using the raw deck name instead of the off-pitch alias.** On turn 32 the "📋 KEEP OR CUT?" scholarship-review moment offered "Talk his way through — A clever answer to a tricky spot · 🎨 Creativity"; I played it and the resolution read "He falls back on **Defence-Splitter** to make his case and it's good news: the club is keeping faith in him." Defence-Splitter is a matchday passing card from his deck and was not on screen. Every other off-pitch resolution in the run correctly used the alias phrasing ("his instinct to share the load", "quick thinking", "a refusal to be worn down"). why it hurts: it tells the player the button he pressed was not the action that happened, on the single most consequential moment of the Scholar chapter (keep or cut) — that is a trust break, not a typo. fix: make the off-pitch resolution always resolve the display alias for the chosen card and never fall through to the underlying deck card's name. Ref: off-pitch card alias mapping in the outcome narrator (shared/src/narrate.ts / offpitch card wrapper).
- [ ] **PT-157 (stage: full-loop browser): [medium] the "money now, or development?" between-season screen repeats verbatim across seasons and has a strictly dominant option, so it is not a decision.** At age 13 (turn 12) it offered "The Soccer School +75c · greedier · more famous · distracted", "The Local Paper +25c · more famous · distracted" and "The One-Club Man — Publicly pledge his future here — the fans adore him, the money waits, the football gets better · +140c · more loyal · more famous · sharper". At age 15 (turn 28) the SAME screen offered "Expenses Covered", the identical "The Local Paper" (same wording, "his nan buys six copies"), and the identical "The One-Club Man". The One-Club Man pays the most money AND carries only positive traits while both rivals carry "distracted"/"greedier" — there is no trade-off to weigh, and the screen's own headline ("money now, or development?") is false. It is also senior contract content: a 13-year-old academy kid has no future to publicly pledge and no fans, which PT-146 flagged and closed. why it hurts: the sequence's headline dilemma resolves to one obviously-correct button that reappears unchanged every season, so the between-season beat — which should be where the player feels ownership — is a click-through. fix: make each offer a real trade (money vs. a stat/relationship cost), suppress offers already taken this career, and gate One-Club-Man-class pledges behind First Team. Ref: rollOffer / offer bank (shared/src/offpitch.ts), screen "A decision off the pitch".
- [ ] **PT-158 (stage: full-loop browser): [medium] energy is never shown as a number and did not recover across the season break, contradicting the tutorial — the player sat on "TIRED" for 14 straight turns with no lever.** The tutorial states "Energy. Big efforts tire him, and tired moments go worse. It recovers between seasons (the summer screen)". "⚡ ENERGY · TIRED" first appeared on turn 24 and was still showing on turn 37 — straight through the entire age-15 between-season sequence. Nowhere in the game is energy a number: the HUD is an unlabelled bar, the Player tab omits energy entirely, yet the summer focuses are all priced in exact energy deltas (⚡-12, ⚡+22, ⚡rec+3), which cannot be evaluated against an unlabelled bar. why it hurts: the player is told a resource matters, shown a warning state he cannot quantify, and given no in-chapter way to clear it — and then the one promised recovery (the season break) visibly did not happen. fix: print energy as "62/100" beside the bar and on the Player tab, show the projected post-choice value on each summer focus tile, and either apply the promised between-seasons recovery or correct the tutorial to say rest must be bought. Ref: HUD energy bar, Player tab, summer focus screen, tutorial "📖 HOW HIS CAREER WORKS".
- [ ] **PT-159 (stage: full-loop browser): [low-med] the age-15 spend screen sells a 15-year-old driving lessons and agent dinners, and chapter-specific setting prose leaks between chapters.** At age 15 the spend tiles included "🚦 Driving Lessons — Freedom on the open road is finally within reach. 💷420c" (the legal minimum is 17, so it is not "finally within reach") and "🍽️ Wine & Dine Your Agent — Keep your agent hungry and fighting your corner. 💷550c". Separately the Grassroots setting bank keeps firing after Grassroots ends: "The five-a-side cage after school" and "A proper pitch with real nets for once" both appeared at Academy (13) and the matchday HEADER regularly contradicts its own setup — turn 9 printed "✈️ Away · Matchday 10, Brandt's Club 0–1 Cranleigh Town" above the prose "Jumpers for goalposts, no ref, and an argument brewing about whether the last one was over the line", and turn 36's "⚡ LIFE EVENT · OFF THE PITCH" opened "A trial game against a bigger club's youth side, everything riding on ninety minutes" before adding "Off the field, who he is matters as much as how he plays." why it hurts: the chapter names are the game's promise about where you are in a life ("Cones, drills and van journeys"), and both the shop and the prose keep breaking it — a tracked away fixture narrated as an unrefereed kickabout, and an OFF-THE-PITCH card whose situation is a match. fix: gate the Driving Lessons / Wine-and-Dine tiles behind age 17+/Breakthrough, restrict each SETTINGS bank to its own chapter set, and forbid matchday settings inside LIFE EVENT / TRAINING GROUND moments (and vice versa). Ref: summer spend bank (shared/src/offpitch.ts), shared/src/narrate.ts SETTINGS by chapter/kind.
- [ ] **PT-160 (stage: full-loop browser): [low-med] the Player tab is the game's only stats screen and it is unreadable and unexplained; the card buttons are also unlabelled for assistive tech.** The whole attribute set renders as one micro-line of eight bare abbreviations, "PAC 10 SHO 14 PAS 16 TAC 9 STR 6 CMP 14 CRE 14 LDR 7", with no bars, no scale, no indication of which grew this season. The header reads "OVR 14 → 15 pot ★★★★★" — a five-star prospect whose stated ceiling is one point above his current rating, which reads as "he will never improve"; meanwhile the Academy roster row still shows the original "★★★☆☆" and the words "in development" with no age, chapter or turn count after 37 turns of play. Below that, "forming: Metronome The Quarterback" runs two trait names together with no separator and no explanation of what a trait does or how far "forming" has to go. And in the accessibility tree every card choice is an unnamed `button` (ref_191…ref_194 etc.), so keyboard and screen-reader users get no card name, tag or description at all. why it hurts: this is the screen a player opens to answer "is my kid any good and did my choices matter?", and it answers none of it; the unnamed buttons make the core interaction unusable without sight. fix: render attributes as labelled bars with season deltas, express potential on the same visible scale as OVR (or drop the "→ pot" shorthand), separate and tooltip the trait chips, mirror age/chapter/OVR onto the Academy roster row, and give each card button an `aria-label` of "<name> — <tags> — <description>". Ref: 👤 PLAYER tab, 🎓 ACADEMY roster row, card choice buttons (client/src/main.ts).

<!-- Code-correctness audit 2026-08-29 (rotation: audit/code bugs) — scope: the whole HEAD~12..HEAD diff, deep on shared/src/squad.ts + teams.ts + storyarc.ts + narrate.ts + career.ts and their client/src/api.ts + main.ts callers; ~80% of the changed surface read, all findings below reproduced by running code under `npx tsx` except where stated. VERIFIED SOUND (checked and found correct, no finding filed): pickArcStart's slot pacing (200 seeds × 120 turns → exactly 20 arcs/career, never two in one slot, no slot lost); stridedIdx/pickByTurn coprime-stride selection; the careerCast collision-skip itself (same rng draw count with and without `avoid`, so replay is untouched); rollOffer's youth band gate (bandIdx<3 = Grassroots/Academy/Scholar, senior deals from Youth Team on, no rng draw); advanceSquad's retire/plateau/decline age boundaries and its morale event ordering; the standing-XI prune after a rollover (dangling ids are already removed, buildXI does not throw). -->
- [x] **PT-600 (audit: code bugs): [high] the career rng stream is advanced by `Array.prototype.sort` with a random comparator, so a save replays into a DIFFERENT career on a different JS engine.** `makeScenario` (`shared/src/career.ts:824`) and `rollCoaches` (`:722`) both do `[...pool].sort(() => rng() - 0.5)`; an inconsistent comparator makes the number and order of comparator calls implementation-defined, so the number of rng() draws consumed is engine-defined. CONFIRMED by execution: the same 6-element demand array consumes 9, 10, 11 or 12 draws depending on seed under V8, and re-running `new Career(4242)` with an equally spec-legal insertion sort swapped in produced a different scenario and a different opening hand from turn 0. why it hurts: saves are (seed + action list) replays, so the same save opened in Safari/Firefox — or after a V8 upgrade — silently rebuilds a different career: different cards, different big games, different graduation stats. `npm run verify` runs only under one Node build so it cannot see this. fix: replace both with an explicit seeded Fisher–Yates that consumes exactly `n-1` draws (`for (let i=n-1;i>0;i--) swap(i, Math.floor(rng()*(i+1)))`), and bump the save/replay version so existing snapshots are migrated rather than silently rerolled. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:824`, `shared/src/career.ts:722`.
- [x] **PT-601 (audit: code bugs): [high] every squad contract is one season short — the season counter is advanced twice, so a 3-season deal expires after 2 seasons and `renewCost` charges wage × 3 for 2 years of service.** `nextSeason` calls `api.spSeasonReward` first, which does `model.profile.season = season + 1` (`client/src/api.ts:502`), and only then calls `advanceSquadSeason`, which reads the ALREADY-incremented `profile.season` and hands it to `advanceSquad` — which itself checks expiry at `season + 1` (`shared/src/squad.ts` `isExpiring`). CONFIRMED by execution against the offline facade: a player bought at season 0 with `signedSeason 0 / contractSeasons 3` is reported in `expiring` at the second rollover (`profile.season 2`), i.e. after seasons 0 and 1 only. why it hurts: the player pays a 3-season renewal fee, is told the deal runs 3 years, and is asked to renew again a year early — every renewal overpays by a third, forever. fix: in `advanceSquadSeason` pass the season just PLAYED to `advanceSquad` (`profile.season - 1`) while still grandfathering new deals at `profile.season`; while there, align the `departed` gate (`signedSeason + contractSeasons < season`) with `isExpiring` — they currently differ by two seasons, so a player flagged "Deal up" does not actually walk for another two years. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:520`, `shared/src/squad.ts:106`.
- [x] **PT-602 (audit: code bugs): [medium] three narration surfaces still call `careerCast` without the family surname, so the seeded rival/gaffer flips name mid-arc and in the chapter recap.** PT-141 added the `avoid` collision-skip and wired it through `careerState`/`actWithNarration`, but `careerAct`'s story-arc branch (`careerCast((c as any).seed >>> 0).rival`, `client/src/api.ts:654`), `chapterRecap` (`shared/src/tokens.ts:319`) and `graduationEpilogue` (`client/src/api.ts:676`) were missed. CONFIRMED by execution that `careerCast(seed)` and `careerCast(seed, 'Novak')` return different rivals (seed 6: Novak vs Halvorsen); the un-avoided call sites were confirmed by reading. The collision is reachable with the DEFAULT name pool — `tokens.ts`'s surname bank contains `Rossi`, which is also in `RIVAL_NAMES`, and `Voss`, which is in `CAPTAIN_NAMES`. why it hurts: an arc's prompt says "beat Halvorsen" and its outcome paragraph says "beat Novak" — the same beat names two different people; the chapter recap then names a third. fix: pass `c.familyName` at all three call sites, exactly as the other `careerCast` callers already do. Ref: `client/src/api.ts:654`, `shared/src/tokens.ts:319`, `client/src/api.ts:676`.
- [ ] **PT-603 (audit: code bugs): [medium] squad players develop against a generic ceiling with no genes, so a defender's goalkeeping climbs and the whole squad compresses toward the same statline.** `advanceSquadPlayer` calls `developAttrs(attrs, undefined, ...)` (`shared/src/squad.ts:86`); with `genes` undefined every DEVELOPABLE stat — including the ones the player's role never uses — caps at 18 and grows every season from 18 to 29. CONFIRMED by execution: a DF minted at 19 with `keeping 4` reaches `keeping 9` by 29 (KEE is a visible column in the squad table, `client/src/main.ts:253`), and the OVR spread across mint qualities 5→17 compresses from 12 points at age 19 to 7 points at age 29 under Training 5. why it hurts: the squad table shows a centre-half quietly becoming a decent goalkeeper, and the difference between a cheap signing and an expensive one erodes away over a decade — the transfer market stops mattering. fix: pass a per-player genes object derived from his minted physicals (as `rebornFields` already does with `band()`), and restrict the growth loop to stats `overall()` actually reads for his role. Ref: `shared/src/squad.ts:86`, `shared/src/lifecycle.ts:18`.
- [x] **PT-604 (audit: code bugs): [low-med] `generateTrialist` draws the role and the first name from the SAME rng value, so every GK trialist has one of two names and every forward one of four.** The reroute through `mintSquadPlayer` (`shared/src/teams.ts:163`) passes the same `seed` the caller already used for `makeRng(seed)`; `mintSquadPlayer` re-seeds `makeRng(seed)` and spends its first draw on the first name, so `floor(r*9)` (role) and `floor(r*18)` (name) are locked together. CONFIRMED by execution over 3000 seeds: GK trialists yield exactly 2 distinct first names (Enzo, Felix), FW 4, DF 6, MF 6 — against 18 for `mintSquadPlayer` called normally. why it hurts: the trial/loan board visibly repeats the same handful of names, and every keeper the player is ever offered is called Enzo or Felix. fix: derive the mint seed from the caller's seed, e.g. `mintSquadPlayer(id, role, quality, (seed ^ 0x9e3779b1) >>> 0, ...)`, so the role draw and the name draw are independent. Ref: `shared/src/teams.ts:158-166`.
- [x] **PT-605 (audit: code bugs): [low-med] the whole squad season report lives in an in-memory field, so a refresh between the rollover and the renew decision silently discards every keep-or-lose call.** `pendingSquadReport` is a plain class field on `Game` (`client/src/main.ts:325`) written in `nextSeason` and read by `squadReportHtml`; it is never written to the save. Confirmed by reading — no `saveMgr`/`localStore` write touches it, and `showSeason` renders nothing when it is null. why it hurts: the one screen where a manager decides who to re-sign and who to let walk vanishes on any reload, tab-restore or crash, and (with PT-601's departure rule) the expired players are then resolved without the manager ever having seen the prompt. fix: persist the report onto the manager save alongside `lastBoard` in the same `saveMgr({ ...m, season: m.season + 1, ... })` call, and clear it when the player dismisses it or the next rollover overwrites it. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:325`, `client/src/main.ts:1813`.

<!-- Card-loop balance audit 2026-08-29 (rotation: audit/card-loop balance) — scope: the core turn loop only (makeScenario / fit / play / ensurePlayableHand / draft growth in shared/src/career.ts, the grade thresholds in client/src/main.ts, and careerScoreOf / rivalRateOf / the season-objective table in shared/src/tokens.ts). SCALE SIMULATED: 3 policies (SKILLED = always best-fit, DECENT = any card carrying a demanded tag, RANDOM) × 200 careers × 120 turns = 72,000 played turns, plus a 24,000-turn what-if sweep replaying the recorded per-turn hand fits under 5 candidate constant sets, plus a hypergeometric deck-coverage pass over the same 24,000 turns. All numbers below are from those runs. VERIFIED ALREADY WELL-TUNED (measured, no finding filed): the SKILL SIGNAL itself is strong — skilled vs random Brilliant rate is 92.1% vs 31.2% and career score p50 1053 vs 451, so the game does distinguish good play from bad; stakes pacing reads well as an occasion (86.9% / 11.0% / 2.1% at stakes 1/2/3 — big games stay scarce); demand breadth is healthy (26.0% single-tag, 36.5% two-tag, 35.1% three-tag moments, so multi-tag asks are the majority); ensurePlayableHand genuinely works — it cuts the raw hypergeometric dead-hand rate from 39.3% to an observed 22.1% and holds it FLAT as the deck grows 7→26 cards; and the deck itself can answer the top-demanded tag on 99.2% of turns, so the dead-hand problem is a hand-selection problem, not a deck-composition one. -->
- [x] **PT-700 (audit: card-loop balance): [critical] `fit` saturates at 1.0 for any card carrying the top-demanded tag, so a correct play is mathematically incapable of going wrong at normal stakes.** `makeScenario` normalises the top tag to exactly 1.0 and `fit` clamps the tag sum at 1, so over 24,000 skilled turns the best available card scored a full 1.0 fit on 79.1% of turns and `success` landed on the clamp at exactly 1.0 on 70.7%; at stakes 1 (86.9% of all turns) the nerve term is `(rng-0.5)*0.18`, a maximum downswing of 0.09 against the 0.22 needed to fall below the 0.78 Brilliant line — so a right card at ordinary stakes CANNOT produce anything but Brilliant, and only stakes-3 turns (2.1%) even swing far enough (±0.23) to matter. Measured skilled grades: Brilliant 92.1% / Solid 5.7% / Scrappy 1.9% / Poor 0.3%, and of all "Right card" turns only 2.2% finished below Solid. why it hurts: this is the single cause of the playtester's "33 of 37 turns were Right card + Brilliant" — success is not a roll the player can lose, so the reward means nothing and 120 turns carry zero tension. fix: normalise the top tag to **0.78 instead of 1.0** (`shared/src/career.ts:845` → `demand[t] = ((demand[t] ?? 0) / mx) * 0.78`) and raise the nerve term from `0.18 + 0.14*(stakes-1)` to **`0.26 + 0.16*(stakes-1)`** (`:1309`). Replaying the recorded hands under those two constants predicts skilled grades of **Brilliant 45.7% / Solid 43.1% / Scrappy 8.5% / Poor 2.7%** — a skilled player still does well four turns in five but genuinely loses one in ten, and covering a SECOND demanded tag (0.78 + ~0.5 → clamps back to 1.0) becomes a real deck-building edge instead of the no-op it is today. No rng draws are added or removed by either change. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:845`, `shared/src/career.ts:1309`.
- [x] **PT-701 (audit: card-loop balance): [critical] the rival benchmark is never a threat — no skilled career in 200 fell behind him even once, and the gap grows without bound.** The player banks `success*8*stakes` ≈ 0.954 × 8 × 1.15 ≈ **8.4 pts/turn** while `rivalRateOf` pays a flat **3–5 pts/turn** (`shared/src/tokens.ts:142`), so the median skilled gap runs +76 at turn 20, +164 at t40, +263 at t60, +472 at t100 and **+573 at t119** — monotonically, exactly the ▲0→▲159 curve the playtester saw. Across 200 skilled careers, **0.0%** were ever behind after turn 20 (even at p10 the final lead is +436); random play, by contrast, ends at a median −31 and is behind at some point in 88.5% of careers, so the rival is currently a pass/fail flag on "did you play at all", not a chase. why it hurts: the headline number the whole meta-loop hangs on stops being read after ten turns, and the rivalry story beats narrate a duel that was decided in the first chapter. fix: with PT-700 applied (mean success drops 0.954 → ~0.75, so the player earns ~7.0/turn), raise `rivalRateOf` from **`3 + (seed>>>3)%3`** (3–5) to **`5 + (seed>>>3)%3`** (5–7). Simulated over the same 200 careers that yields a median gap of 0 at t20, +31 at t60, +104 at the end, with **64% of skilled careers falling behind him at least once after turn 20** — beatable, but only by staying good. Ref: `shared/src/tokens.ts:142`.
- [x] **PT-702 (audit: card-loop balance): [high] one in five hands contains no card carrying the green required tag, because `ensurePlayableHand` only guarantees a 0.45 "fair" fit, not the tag the UI tells the player to match.** Measured over 24,000 skilled turns: **22.1%** of hands held no card with the top-demanded tag, and the rate is flat across every chapter (Grassroots 20.3%, Scholar 23.5%, Establishing 21.9%) as the deck grows 7.0 → 26.4 cards — so deck growth is NOT the cause, contrary to the playtest hypothesis. The cause is the `FAIR = 0.45` threshold at `shared/src/career.ts:1539`: a card matching only a secondary tag clears it, so the swap-in never fires. Crucially, the deck contains a top-tag card on **99.2%** of those turns — the answer is in the deck, the hand just doesn't get it. why it hurts: a third of the tester's turns were unanswerable through no fault of his deck-building, so failure felt like dice rather than a decision — the flip side of PT-700's guaranteed-win problem. fix: change `ensurePlayableHand` to require a card carrying the **top-demanded tag** (or, equivalently under PT-700's 0.78 scale, raise `FAIR` from **0.45 to 0.70**) before it stops searching the draw pile and discard. Predicted dead-hand rate: **22.1% → ~0.8%** (only the turns where the whole deck genuinely can't answer, which is the deck-building signal this function was written to preserve). CAVEAT — this makes the swap fire more often, which changes when the discard pile is reshuffled and therefore DOES perturb the rng draw count for existing saves; it needs a save/replay version bump. The determinism-safe alternative, raising `HAND_SIZE` 4 → 5, only moves the raw rate 39.3% → 30.2% and also changes draw count, so it is strictly worse. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1539`, `shared/src/career.ts:902`.
- [x] **PT-703 (audit: card-loop balance): [high] the season objective table splits into three that auto-complete and two that are nearly unreachable — so a chapter is either already won or quietly impossible.** Simulated across 1,400 chapter-objectives (200 careers × 7 bands): `reads` (perfect reads) completes with a mean **11.8 idle turns** left in the chapter, `strong` **10.7**, `consistency` **6.6**, and none of the three ever failed — a skilled player clears them at 33–60% of chapter elapsed and then has nothing to chase, which is exactly the "✓ complete for 6+ turns" report. At the other end, `leadership` was **never completed in 80%** of the chapters it was drawn for and `flair` in **50%**, because they gate on holding a specific tag the draft may simply never offer. why it hurts: five of the six objectives give the player no live target — three are banked early, two are dead on arrival — so the one system meant to give a chapter direction gives none. fix: (a) with PT-700 in place the success-gated targets tighten automatically (a 0.68 hit-rate falls from 92% to ~55% of turns), so additionally raise the `strong` target from **0.35 → 0.55** of band turns and `reads` from **0.30 → 0.55**; (b) make `flair`/`leadership` count any *demanded-tag* moment answered rather than that one tag, or drop their target from `0.2 × band.turns` to a flat 2 and only offer them once that tag is actually in the deck. Ref: `shared/src/tokens.ts:373`, `shared/src/tokens.ts:376`, `shared/src/tokens.ts:378`.
- [x] **PT-704 (audit: card-loop balance): [medium] the Brilliant/Solid grade thresholds are set for a success distribution the engine no longer produces, so 92% of skilled turns share one label.** With `success` piling up on the 1.0 clamp (70.7% of skilled turns) the 0.78 / 0.58 / 0.38 cut points at `client/src/main.ts:2724` bucket 92.1% of skilled play, 82.1% of "decent" play and even **31.2% of RANDOM play** into ⭐ Brilliant — the label carries almost no information about what the player did. Even the "Unlucky" reframe is near-dead: it fires on only 2.2% of right-card turns. why it hurts: four grades that resolve to one grade means the core feedback pill teaches the player nothing, and the rare Scrappy result reads as a bug rather than a bad night. fix: after PT-700 lands, nudge the cut points from **0.78/0.58/0.38 to 0.80/0.60/0.40**; against the simulated post-PT-700 distribution that predicts **Brilliant ~40% / Solid ~44% / Scrappy ~13% / Poor ~3%** for skilled play — a spread where each pill is earned and Brilliant stays a genuine highlight. Presentational only; no engine or determinism impact. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2724`.
- [x] **PT-705 (audit: card-loop balance): [medium] outcome variance is so small that a 120-turn career has no dispersion — the score you finish on is decided at character creation, not by play.** Skilled career scores across 200 runs landed at p10 **1011**, p50 **1053**, p90 **1100** — a p90/p10 spread of just **8.8%** over 120 turns, and per-turn success has mean 0.954 with sd 0.111 that is almost entirely the clamp's one-sided tail. Because `success = clamp(f + noise + …, 0, 1)` truncates at 1, every upside roll on a saturated fit is thrown away, which is why the sd is asymmetric and why no skilled career ever separates from another. why it hurts: there is nothing to replay for — two careers played identically well score within 4% of each other, so "beat your best run" has no headroom and the graduation stat shape barely moves. fix: PT-700's 0.78 scale plus the 0.26/+0.16 nerve term lifts per-turn sd from 0.111 to ~0.21 and un-pins the clamp (mean success 0.954 → ~0.75, so upside rolls survive); expect the p90/p10 career-score spread to open from 8.8% to roughly 25–30%. Additionally consider raising the stakes-2 gate at `shared/src/career.ts:848` from `r < 0.17` to `r < 0.22` so ~15% of turns carry the larger swing rather than 11%. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1317`, `shared/src/career.ts:848`.
- [x] **PT-706 (audit: card-loop balance): [low-med] a "decent but not optimal" player is barely punished — playing any demanded tag rather than the best one costs 10 points of Brilliant rate and 5% of career score.** The DECENT policy (pick a card matching any demanded tag, not necessarily the best) scored Brilliant on **82.1%** of turns against skilled's 92.1%, and finished on a median career score of **998 vs 1053** — a 5.2% penalty for never reading a moment properly across 120 turns. Its `answeredAsk` rate was 82.8%, so it played the "wrong" card one turn in six and lost almost nothing for it. why it hurts: the difference between skimming the demand line and actually reading it is invisible, so there is no reason to think about a play — which is the whole game. fix: this is a direct consequence of PT-700's saturation (any demanded tag already caps out) and is largely fixed by it: under the 0.78 scale a secondary-tag card scores ~0.4–0.6 against the top card's 0.78, so the same policy's predicted Brilliant rate falls to ~28% against skilled's 45.7%. Re-measure the DECENT policy after PT-700 and only tune further (e.g. reducing secondary-tag demand weights from `0.6 - guard*0.08` at `shared/src/career.ts:857` to `0.45 - guard*0.08`) if the gap is still under 15 points. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:857`.

<!-- ═══ DEEP-LOOP BROWSER PLAYTEST (fresh save "Ashworth", 2026-08-29) ═══
Played a NEW GAME from the academy trial to the END of the bloodline: all 120 career turns
(Grassroots → Academy → Scholar → Youth Team → Breakthrough → First Team), the MANAGER HANDOFF at turn 104,
then TWELVE managed seasons (2 played matchday-by-matchday with lineup/tactics/team-talk, 10 simmed),
the season rollover + squad report each year, the player's retirement, the "what next / what does he pass down"
succession screens, and the generation-2 heir (Leo Ashworth, ★★★★☆, pedigree 70%). Nothing crashed; zero console
errors for the entire run; no save-bricking; the succession loop closes cleanly.
GOOD: the childhood age-gating fix HOLDS below ~14 (grassroots/academy arcs — the kit, the water bottle, the
40-second video — are genuinely well written and age-true, the strongest content in the game). Contract lengths,
squad decay and the wage line all behaved. Auto-pick, Sim, the league table, promotion/relegation, the sponsor
choice, the transfer market toggle, backroom staff, renew/let-go (from season 4), retirement and the heir handoff
all function. The match engine's tactics screen is genuinely deep.
VERDICT ON THE LATE GAME: the manager phase does NOT reward the career that precedes it — it actively erases it.
A player who has just finished a Continental Final, 11 international caps and a cup final at Wembley is handed a
club in "the Sunday League" 8th of 10, in a brand-new fictional league against clubs he has never met, with his
own star rated OVR 10 and played out of position. It then wins every game it plays. It reads as a different,
easier game bolted onto the end, not as the payoff.
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

- [x] **PT-800 (stage: deep-loop browser): [high] the manager-mode league pyramid is inverted and incoherent, so six consecutive "promotions" are announced while the club descends the real-world ladder.** Simming 12 seasons the ladder ran: the Sunday League → the District League → the County Premier → **the Championship → League One → League Two → the National League** → the Regional Premier → the Regional North/South, every step banner-announced as "⬆️ PROMOTED — up to X. A division higher, a division tougher." Championship→League One→League Two→National League→Regional is exactly backwards, and the retirement recap then reads "In his very last season he saw the club **drop to the County Premier**" — a single relegation from the Regional North/South landing nine tiers *above* where it started. why it hurts: the division ladder is the entire spine of manager mode and every football fan spots this in the first five minutes; it makes the climb read as a bug rather than progress. fix: define one explicit ordered pyramid array (tier 0 = Sunday League … tier N = top flight) and derive promotion/relegation from the index instead of a name list. Ref: Club Season screen, header + promotion banner. **[NOT REPRODUCIBLE — the ladder was simulated end to end and every step is coherent: Sunday League → District → County Premier → Regional N/S → Regional Premier → National → League Two → League One → Championship → Premier Division, and a relegation from the Regional N/S lands in the County Premier exactly as the recap said. There is a single tier-name lookup in the codebase and it is correct. Closing on evidence rather than a fix.]**
- [x] **PT-801 (stage: deep-loop browser): [high] the manager handoff throws away the entire 104-turn career — league, opponents, standing and prestige all reset to zero.** The career's own 🏆 League tab showed Ashworth's Club 4th of 10 with Inglewood Town, Wollaton United, Rosewick Athletic et al., and the player had just had "A Continental Final", two CUP FINALs, a European run and 11 international caps. One click of "🧢 Take the reins as manager →" and the header reads "Ashworth's Club · **the Sunday League** · Season 1 · MD 1/18 · **8th/10**", with a fixture list of ten clubs that appear nowhere in the career (Kestrel Vale, Vale Ferrers, Wyndham City, Ryedale City, Thurloe FC, Sandmere United, Holbeck United…). why it hurts: this is the moment the game promises the career "becomes your club's story", and instead it deletes it — the single biggest reason the late game feels like a different product. fix: seed manager-mode season 1 from the career's league, table position and opponent set, and scale the starting tier off career score / prestige. Ref: handoff screen → Club Season.
- [x] **PT-802 (stage: deep-loop browser): [high] managed seasons have no difficulty and the sim produces near-identical scorelines.** Season 1 simmed to **P18 W18 D0 L0, 54 pts, GF 37 GA 5**; of the 18 results, 13 were exactly **2-0** and 3 were 2-1 (full list: 4-2, 1-0, then 2-0 ×13 and 2-1 ×3). The club was promoted in each of the first six seasons and the ★ player's OVR froze at 12 from season 4 to season 12. why it hurts: the reward for 104 turns of card play is a league that cannot be lost, and the fixture column is a wall of "2-0" — there is no season narrative and no reason to set a lineup. fix: add goal-count variance and opponent-strength scaling per tier so a promoted side is genuinely mid-table; cap unbeaten runs. Ref: Club Season → Fixtures / League table.
- [x] **PT-803 (stage: deep-loop browser): [high] the star player's rating and position are wrong and self-contradictory across the handoff.** The handoff screen says "⚽ **OVR 14** · Regular starter · ★ career score 951"; one click later the season header says "★ Dane Ashworth · **OVR 10**". He was signed and played his whole career as a forward ("Looks like a forward · runs all day", "FW · first of the line") yet the lineup screen lists "Dane Ashworth (**DF** 10) 🥉", level with squad filler — and the same screen names someone else as "★ Key player: Luis Mensah (GK, OVR 11)" directly under a tutorial that says "Dane Ashworth is your key player — his rating drives the whole squad's strength". The gen-2 heir Leo Ashworth then inherits "DF" too. why it hurts: the one thing carried across the handoff is carried wrong, and the game contradicts its own tutorial on the first screen of manager mode. fix: derive manager-mode OVR and position from the career's final attributes with a single shared function; make "key player" always the bloodline player. Ref: handoff screen, Set Lineup.
- [x] **PT-804 (stage: deep-loop browser): [high] match commentary repeats itself to the point of noise — 645 lines, 239 unique, in a single 90 minutes.** Counted from the MD2 log (Vale Ferrers 0-1): "Scrappy — it pinballs around in the final third." **×28**, "The ball breaks loose in the final third." **×28**, "Vale Ferrers keep it: Timo Costa → Kofi Zeman in the final third." **×24**, "⚡ Vale Ferrers spring the press — Kofi Zeman robs him in the final third!" **×17**, "Scrappy — it pinballs around in midfield." ×16, "Cut out! Kofi Zeman's pass is intercepted in the final third." ×15. One opposition player (Kofi Zeman) is named in five of the ten most frequent lines, and 46% of all lines are duplicates. why it hurts: watching a match at 1x is the marquee new feature of manager mode and it reads as a stuck loop; players will hit Skip every time and never see the engine. fix: track recently-emitted templates and suppress repeats within a window; add zone/phase-specific variants; spread the ball-carrier weighting across the XI. Ref: match viewer commentary feed.
- [x] **PT-805 (stage: deep-loop browser): [medium] story arcs are not gated at the TOP end, so ages 15–21 repeatedly run beats written for a veteran senior pro.** Age 15: "He **drives through the night** and holds her hand"; "Buy Mum and Dad the house". Age 16: the kitman testimonial arc — "He walks into the director's office…", "They ask **the club's biggest name** which way it goes". Age 17: "For **six years** a lad in a wheelchair has posted him a letter after every single match" (he was 11 and in Sunday league). Age 18 (Youth Team): a domestic cup FINAL, "Ninety-odd thousand", Wembley. Age 19: "His **late best friend's son** — his godson, ten years old". Age 21: "comes calling in **the autumn of his career**", "**a decade has passed**, the legs are slower", and the club offers to retire his number. why it hurts: the childhood gate was fixed but the ceiling wasn't, so the whole 15–21 stretch — the bulk of the career — is full of impossible biography that breaks the life-sim illusion. fix: give every arc a `minAge`/`maxAge` (or minChapter/maxChapter) and filter on both bounds, not just the lower one. Ref: story-arc scheduler, Scholar/Youth Team/Breakthrough/First Team.
- [x] **PT-806 (stage: deep-loop browser): [medium] a gambling-addiction and match-fixing arc fires at age 17.** "🎰 THE NUMBER IN HIS POCKET" ran on turn 54, age 17: "The card school on the coach turned into an app on the phone turned into stakes that stopped being funny… the numbers on the screen are bigger than a fan earns in a year", resolving into a spot-fixing approach ("see a yellow card at a set minute… clear the whole debt in one night"). why it hurts: the protagonist is below the legal gambling age in the game's own setting, and gambling + match-fixing is exactly the content that moves an age rating on a Steam premium title — the same class of problem as the romance arc that was already gated away from children. fix: gate this arc (and any other betting/nightlife content) to 18+, same as the adult-relationship arcs. Ref: story-arc `THE NUMBER IN HIS POCKET`.
- [x] **PT-807 (stage: deep-loop browser): [medium] the end-of-season screen shows LAST season's squad report, including players it already retired a year ago.** After simming season 2 to completion the "👥 THE SQUAD, A YEAR ON" panel was byte-identical to season 1's: same "📈 Improved Kofi Moreau MF · 25 8→9 · Idris Tanaka MF · 26 8→9 · Cato Novak MF · 29 5→6", same "📉 Fading Jan Novak DF · 31 6→5 · Timo Tanaka MF · 31 8→7", same "🎽 Retired **Luis Mensah GK · 34 · Paulo Larsen FW · 34**" (both retired at the end of season 1), same "💷 Wages −194c". It only regenerates when you click "Next season →". why it hurts: the squad report is the one place the living squad becomes visible, and on the screen where the player naturally reads it, it is a year stale and reports the same men retiring twice. fix: recompute (or clear) the season report when the season completes, not when the next one is opened. Ref: Club Season → season-complete screen.
- [ ] **PT-808 (stage: deep-loop browser): [medium] moment prompts are stitched from independent fragments that contradict each other on the same screen.** At turn 29 and again at turn 101 the matchday panel read "🏟️ **Home** · Matchday 30 … The visiting end has gone quiet — this is his chance to silence **the home crowd**". At turn 91 the chapter banner "Purple Patch — You're in the form of your life — everything comes off" sat directly above "A **slump nobody can quite explain**, least of all him." Turn 11: "On the walk home from the match, the other kids are deciding who was best. … **His mum's watching from the touchline**." Turn 6 (demand = stamina): "The new kid doesn't know anyone yet and is standing on his own. **They want to see if there's anything left in the tank.**" why it hurts: every one of these is visible in a single glance, and it tells the player the prose is a slot machine rather than a story about their kid. fix: pick the situation clause first and draw the state/venue/demand clauses from a pool tagged compatible with it (home/away, on-pitch/off-pitch, current form). Ref: moment prompt assembler.
- [ ] **PT-809 (stage: deep-loop browser): [medium] the off-pitch card bank never levels up — the same eight generic cards from age 10 are still the entire deck at 22 — and Grassroots served five identical life events back-to-back.** Turns 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 were all "⚡ LIFE EVENT · OFF THE PITCH" with the same hand, and the bank (Share the load / Keep a cool head / Grind through it / Charm his way through / Front up to it / Improvise a fix / Dig his heels in / Talk it through with the group) was still the whole off-pitch deck at turn 100, age 22, First Team. The situation lines recycle verbatim too: "A birthday party clashes with the big game, and he has to choose. The legs are tired — the question is whether his are, too." appeared word-for-word at turn 5 (age 11) and turn 17 (age 13); "The new kid doesn't know anyone yet and is standing on his own." at turns 6 and 18. why it hurts: off-pitch moments are ~40% of the career and the player is making the same four-way choice for 120 turns; the drafted deck (Dark Arts, A Word from the Gaffer, Counter-Press) never applies off the pitch. fix: give the off-pitch bank chapter-scoped variants and let drafted leadership/composure cards be playable in life events. Ref: life-event card bank; Grassroots turns 3–7.
- [x] **PT-810 (stage: deep-loop browser): [low-med] several text-interpolation defects show up in senior prose, including a raw internal id on the retirement screen.** "**captain** Ellery clapped him on the back." (lowercase title, 4 occurrences — turns 34, 35, 43, 46) against a correct "Captain Ellery is looking his way." elsewhere; "and **turner** has never quite let him forget the last time these two met" (turn 83); "It's **CUP FINAL** — and…" (raw uppercase label spliced mid-sentence, turns 70 and 83) against correct "It's Derby Day." / "It's A Continental Final."; "the keeper caught yards off his line" rendered as "**the Turner keeper**" (rival surname used as a club name, turn 67); "📣 Latest call-up (qualifier): Ardennes vs Rhodania 1-0 · ⚽ **braced!**"; and on the retirement screen, "Only mentoring passes something to the heir — the others are his own next chapter. **Your call (#56).**" why it hurts: these are the highest-emotion screens in the game (cup finals, retirement) and a leaked "#56" reads as an unfinished build. fix: capitalise cast titles at the interpolation site, title-case the fixture label, use a club token rather than the rival token, fix the "brace" string, and strip the decision id from player-facing copy. Ref: narration templates, chapter-break/retirement screens.
- [ ] **PT-811 (stage: deep-loop browser): [low-med] by the senior chapters the chapter-break spend screen is a 22-tile wall whose only exit is below the fold, and it jumps when you buy.** At the First Team break the "💷 Spend your earnings" grid held 22 purchase tiles and the sole CTA "Done spending — choose your summer →" measured at y≈1056 in a 1400px-tall viewport — off-screen with no sticky footer and no other way forward. Buying one item reflows the grid so the button moves up ~120px: my first click on it landed on empty space and did nothing, with no feedback. why it hurts: a first-time player at a forced modal step can reasonably conclude the game has hung; and 22 undifferentiated tiles with no sort or category is a worse decision than one tile would be. fix: pin the CTA to a sticky footer, group the tiles (self / family / club / image), and keep the button position stable across purchases. Ref: chapter-break spend screen (Breakthrough & First Team).

<!-- ═══ CODE AUDIT: THE CAREER → MANAGER HANDOFF (2026-08-29) ═══
SCOPE: a read-only code audit of exactly one transition — `renderHandoff`/`cg-takereins` (client/src/main.ts:2454-2483)
→ `api.careerHandoff` (client/src/api.ts:414-426) → `graduatedFields`/`tokenToPlayer` (shared/src/tokens.ts:411-419, 56-62)
→ the first manager screen (`clubTier`/`autoPickXI`/`buildXI`). Root-causing PT-801/PT-803 (which recorded the SYMPTOMS
from a browser run) down to the specific lines, with the OVR and position gaps MEASURED over simulated graduates via a
throwaway `npx tsx` harness in shared/ (deleted after; no game code touched, no git run).
VERIFIED SOUND — not findings: `graduatedFields` correctly threads `c.finContext()` (incl. `attrFocus` + deck chemistry)
into `graduate()`, so the token's stored attrs ARE the career's real development; the token id is preserved through the
state flip (prospect→pro), so the bloodline/NFT identity survives; `signContract` + `staked_since`/`prime_season` are set
so the star is immediately selectable and correctly aged; `mergedClub` (api.ts:164-169) does surface pro tokens as fieldable
players; and the whole path is deterministic — the only `Math.random()` anywhere near it is in `freshSave` (save.ts:43-44),
which runs at NEW-SAVE time, not at the handoff.
DESIGN VERDICT: none of the three defects is the real problem — they are symptoms of the same architectural gap. The
handoff transfers ONE scalar (a token id) across a boundary that the player experienced as 104 turns of league finishes,
continental nights and caps. Everything else the career produced is either narration with no state behind it
(career.ts:827 — "A Continental Final" is a string in a big-moments list) or state that is simply never read on the far
side. Until the handoff carries a RECORD, not just a player, the manager phase can only ever begin from defaults.
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

- [x] **PT-950 (audit: career->manager handoff): [high] the starting tier is never written at the handoff, so EVERY career — Continental finalist or not — begins in the bottom division of ten.** `clubTier()` (client/src/main.ts:1120) reads `localStorage['fm_tier_<handle>']` and returns `TIERS` (=10, "the Sunday League", shared/src/clubseason.ts:69/76) whenever the key is absent; the only writer is `setClubTier` at season rollover (main.ts:1842), and `renderHandoff` never calls it — it only writes `MgrState` (main.ts:2480). The career's own achievements are equally unwritten: `graduatedFields` (shared/src/tokens.ts:411-419) sets no `ach_*` field, so `tokenAch` reports 0 seasons/0 titles/`ach_tier` 0 to every downstream consumer. why it hurts: this is the single line that turns the payoff into a demotion — the game hands a capped international a pub team, and `tierStrength(10)`=5 (clubseason.ts:83-87) then guarantees the trivial unbeaten seasons of PT-802 as well. fix: in the `cg-takereins` handler, before `saveMgr`, call `this.setClubTier(startingTier(s))` where `startingTier` is a pure deterministic function of career state already on `CareerState` — e.g. `clamp(10 - round(careerScore/150) - (international.caps>=5?1:0) - (careerScore>=900?1:0), 3, 10)` — clamped so no career skips straight to the top flight, and mirror the same inputs into `ach_seasons/ach_apps/ach_tier` in `graduatedFields`. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1120`, `client/src/main.ts:2480`.
- [x] **PT-951 (audit: career->manager handoff): [high] two different overall formulas AND two different stat vectors straddle the handoff, so the same player's OVR legally changes by up to 5 points across one click.** The handoff screen prints `h.overall` = `careerProfile(...).currentOverall` = `careerOverall()` over PARTIAL, mid-career stats (shared/src/tokens.ts:327, career.ts:1758-1767 — FW core is shooting/pace/composure/creativity); the very next screen prints `overall(tokenToPlayer(t))` = shared/src/teams.ts:66-73 over the GRADUATED stats (FW core is shooting/pace/strength/positioning/passing — no mental stats at all). Measured over 1200 simulated graduates, `careerOverall − teams.overall` on the SAME attrs is mean **+2.43**, max **+5** (DF mean +2.83, 21% of DF graduates ≥+4); measured end-to-end handoff-screen-vs-squad-screen over 180 played-out careers it is mean **+1.31**, max **+4** — which reproduces the tester's 14 → 10 exactly. why it hurts: the number on the celebration screen is the player's entire summary of 104 turns, and the game contradicts it one click later, so neither number is ever believed again. fix: make `careerOverall` the single source — have `tokens.ts` compute the handoff figure from the SAME `graduate()` output the token will store (call `graduatedFields` for the preview), and have the manager UI display `t.peak_overall`-style career OVR for bloodline tokens instead of `overall()`; keep `overall()` for match/selection maths only so engine calibration is untouched. Ref: `shared/src/tokens.ts:327`, `shared/src/teams.ts:66`.
- [x] **PT-952 (audit: career->manager handoff): [high] the star's POSITION is re-derived from scratch at graduation, so the role the career advertised for 104 turns can silently change on the handoff click.** `graduatedFields` → `graduate()` calls `deriveRole(attrs)` (shared/src/career.ts:1751-1756, 1831) on the FINAL attrs — after the personality signature bump, `legacyBonus`, the injury penalty and the trait `apply()` bumps — while every career screen showed `prospectValuation(...).role`, i.e. `deriveRole` on the pre-graduation attrs (tokens.ts:181-191). Measured over 180 played-out careers the position CHANGES at graduation in **6/180 (3%)**, including FW→DF; worse, `careerProfile` returns `attrs: deriveStats(c.log, seed, genes)` with NO `attrFocus`/chemistry while its own `role`/`currentOverall` come from the focus-aware `prospectValuation`, so the attribute bars the player watched all career imply a DIFFERENT position than the header in **73/150 (49%)** of careers. why it hurts: position is identity in a life-sim — "he was signed as a forward" is the player's whole mental model, and the game silently overwrites it at the one moment it claims to be honouring the career. fix: freeze the role — persist `deriveRole` the first time the career's profile is shown (write it to `t.role`) and have `graduate()` accept that role instead of re-deriving, and pass `c.finContext().attrFocus` into `careerProfile`'s `deriveStats` so the displayed bars, the header role and the graduated role are all one vector. Both changes are pure and keep replay determinism. Ref: `shared/src/career.ts:1831`, `shared/src/tokens.ts:183`.
- [x] **PT-953 (audit: career->manager handoff): [high] the "make sure the star starts" fix reorders the lineup array, shifting every downstream slot and fielding the rest of the XI out of position.** main.ts:2474 does `ids = [...ids.filter((id) => id !== worst.id), s.prospectId]` — but `playerIds` is POSITIONAL: index i maps to `FORMATIONS[f][i]` in `buildXI` (shared/src/teams.ts:194-200) and to `SLOT_ROLES[f][i]` in the lineup editor (main.ts:3098, 3120). Dropping the worst starter from index k and appending shifts every player at k+1..10 down one slot, so the MF at slot 5 inherits a DF slot. Simulated over 400 seeded `BASE_QUALITY`-6 clubs plus a star: the forced-in path runs on 21/400 saves and leaves **1.81 players out of position on average** every time it does. why it hurts: it manufactures exactly the "⚠ out of position" warnings the game itself flags (PT-85), on the first lineup screen of manager mode, as a side effect of a fix meant to be a courtesy. fix: swap in place instead of splicing — `ids = ids.map((id) => (id === worst.id ? s.prospectId : id))` — and pick `worst` from the starters whose SLOT role matches the star's role (falling back to the overall-worst) so the swap is positionally sane. One line, deterministic. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:2474`.
- [x] **PT-954 (audit: career->manager handoff): [high] the manager league is generated from a completely different source than the career league, so season 1 is played against clubs the career never met.** The career's table comes from `clubSeason(clubName, strength, share, seasonSeed)` (shared/src/clubseason.ts:211-217) → `seededLeague(..., seed)` with NO tier, seeded per age-band from the career seed; manager mode calls `seededOpponents(clubName, this.leagueSeed(), this.clubTier())` (main.ts:1327) where `leagueSeed()` is a hash of the ACCOUNT HANDLE only (main.ts:1114) and the tier branch takes a different hash path entirely (`clubseason.ts:105-116`). Different seed, different tier, different draw — the two leagues cannot coincide. why it hurts: the club name is the ONLY thing the player recognises after the click; the ten teams he spent a decade of in-game seasons beating are gone, which is what makes it read as a different product rather than a continuation. fix: persist the career's final league seed and finishing position onto the token/save at handoff and pass that seed (not `leagueSeed()`) as season 1's league seed, keeping the tier from PT-950; deterministic, and it makes the tier and the opponent set agree. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1114`, `shared/src/clubseason.ts:211`.
- [ ] **PT-955 (audit: career->manager handoff): [medium] the career's headline achievements have no persistent representation at all, so nothing downstream CAN read them.** "A Continental Final" is a string in a big-moments list (shared/src/career.ts:827) and international caps live only inside `careerState`'s derived `international` block (tokens.ts:395); `Career` stores no honours, and `graduatedFields` (tokens.ts:411-419) writes only `attrs/role/traits/personality/greed/marketability/earnings/peak_overall`. Every consumer that asks about the bloodline — `tokenAch` (tokens.ts:63), `legendCardOf` (tokens.ts:445), `legacyBoost`/`rebornFields`, the achievements snapshot (main.ts:596-606) — therefore sees a player with 0 seasons, 0 apps, 0 titles and `ach_tier` 0. why it hurts: PT-950/954 cannot be fixed properly without this — there is literally no career record to scale the starting tier, the prestige or the heir's inheritance off; it also means the legend card of a capped international is indistinguishable from that of a reserve. fix: add a small `honours` accumulator on `Career` (big-moment wins, cup/continental finals reached & won, caps) written by the same code that narrates them, and have `graduatedFields` seed `ach_seasons/ach_apps/ach_cup/ach_tier` from it. Pure state on an already-replayed log — determinism unaffected. Ref: `shared/src/tokens.ts:411`, `shared/src/career.ts:827`.
- [ ] **PT-956 (audit: career->manager handoff): [medium] the squad the star inherits is a fixed pub-level roster that has no relationship to his career, which is why an OVR-10 forward is the best player at the club.** `makeClub` mints the manager's roster at `BASE_QUALITY = 6` (shared/src/game.ts:14, 27) at NEW-SAVE time — before a single career turn is played — from a `Math.random()` seed (client/src/save.ts:43-44), and `graduatedFields`/`careerHandoff` never touch it. So `squadStrength()` (main.ts:1122-1125) sits around the tier-10 baseline of 5 (`tierStrength`, clubseason.ts:83-87) no matter what the career achieved, and the merged star (PT-951's post-conversion OVR ~10) tops the squad by default. why it hurts: it is the third independent reason the manager phase reads as a reset — the club, the league and the squad were all decided before the career began, so nothing the player did for 104 turns is visible anywhere on screen. fix: at the handoff, re-mint the non-bloodline squad with `generateClub(..., quality, seed, true)` where `quality` is derived from the same career-score/tier function as PT-950 and `seed` is the career seed (deterministic, replaces the `Math.random()` roster), so a great career hands you a squad worth managing rather than eleven strangers. Ref: `shared/src/game.ts:14`, `client/src/save.ts:43`.
<!-- Manager-difficulty audit 2026-08-29 (code + simulation only, no browser). Scale simulated: 20,000-seed per-match W/D/L sweeps across strength deltas −6..+8; 300-seed seasons at tiers 10/7/4/1 across four club-strength bands; 200 full 15-season careers replayed end-to-end through the REAL pipeline (generateClub BASE_QUALITY=6 rich → advanceSquad living-squad rollover → transferList buys → seededLeague/seasonTable → top-2-up/bottom-2-down), at 0, 1 and 2 signings per season; plus the analyze_manager_career harness model re-run under five candidate constant sets. VERIFIED ALREADY WELL-TUNED, do not touch: (a) the season response curve to strength — edge +2 over the tier baseline = 14% promotion, +3 = ~40%, +4 = ~74%, which is a properly graded reward for investment; (b) the promotion/relegation rule (top-2 of 10 up, bottom-2 down) — at edge 0 it produces 12% promotion / 11% relegation, a genuinely knife-edge mid-table; (c) goals per match, 2.36 at parity, and the goal cap at 6, both realistic; (d) the living-squad rollover itself — with zero signings a club drifts only 8.52 → 8.70 over 15 seasons at training lvl 1 and 8.52 → 10.65 at lvl 3, reaches the top flight 0% of the time and ends at tier 5.1, i.e. neglect is correctly punished and the squad lifecycle is NOT the source of the runaway; (e) squadStrength's top-11-mean definition and the wage/fee economy. -->
- [x] **PT-900 (audit: manager difficulty): [critical] `simFixtureResult`'s `rnd()` right-shifts the raw seed, discarding exactly the bits that vary between fixtures, so a simmed season replays one or two scorelines all year.** `rnd(n)` computes `((seed >>> n) ^ (seed >>> (n+7))) % 100`, and every caller passes an UNMIXED seed whose only variation is in the low bits (`leagueSeed ^ (season*131 + i)`, i = 0..17). Measured: two consecutive seeds produce the byte-identical scoreline 94.5% of the time, and over 300 handles × 3 seasons an 18-game simmed season averages **2.0 distinct scorelines with the modal one repeating 14.2 times out of 18** — this is precisely the live playthrough's "13 of 18 results were exactly 2-0". `liveTable`'s own `simMatch` is unaffected because it passes its seed through `hash32` first. why it hurts: it makes every simmed season read as a broken build regardless of any balance work, and it is what turned a genuinely-too-easy season into a visibly fake one. fix: avalanche the seed before shifting — `const m = hash32(seed)` (or `seed ^= seed >>> 16; seed = Math.imul(seed, 0x2c1b3c6d)`) and shift `m`, in both `simFixtureResult` and `simMatch`; determinism-safe, identical rnd draw COUNT, but it does change the replayed scorelines of an in-progress season. Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1767`, `shared/src/clubseason.ts:180`.
- [x] **PT-901 (audit: manager difficulty): [critical] the match model has almost no variance: a +3 strength edge loses 0.5% of matches and a +4 edge loses 0.0%, so any club above its tier goes unbeaten.** `diff = (myStr - oppStr) * 0.12 + 0.25` against a noise band of only `(rnd - 0.5) * 2.2` (i.e. ±1.1 goals) gives measured per-match W/D/L of 37/46/16 at parity, **70/29/0.5 at +3, 78/22/0.0 at +4, 88/12/0.0 at +5** — a P18 W18 D0 L0 season is the model working as written. why it hurts: it removes the single thing that makes a league season worth playing (the possibility of dropping points), and it means every promotion is decided before a ball is kicked. fix: coefficient `0.12 -> 0.10` and noise `2.2 -> 3.2` in BOTH copies; measured result: +3 becomes 56/33/11, +4 becomes 61/32/7, +5 becomes 68/28/4, parity 40/33/26, with goals per match essentially unchanged (2.36 -> 2.40). Determinism-safe (no change to rnd draw count). Ref: `shared/src/clubseason.ts:181`, `client/src/main.ts:1768`.
- [x] **PT-902 (audit: manager difficulty): [high] `tierStrength` is perfectly flat — `15 - tier`, exactly +1 per tier, with an identical ±3 opponent spread (measured sd 2.00) at every level — so climbing costs a fixed +1 while club strength grows faster than that every season.** Measured opponent means: 5.16 / 6.02 / 6.99 / 8.04 / 9.01 / 10.00 / 11.05 / 11.93 / 12.90 / 14.00 for tiers 10..1, and the season outcome table is byte-identical at tier 10 and tier 1 for the same edge (edge +2 → 14/14/17% promotion; +4 → 73/74/78%). A fresh founding club already measures **8.52 strength against a tier-10 baseline of 5** — edge +3.5 before a single decision. why it hurts: the pyramid is supposed to be the difficulty curve and it is instead a constant-cost escalator; nothing about the top flight is harder than the Sunday League. fix: `return 15 - clamp(...)` -> `return 16.8 - 1.3 * clamp(...)` (T1 15.5, T5 10.3, T10 3.8 — still passes `qa_pyramid_fuzz`'s `tierStrength(1) >= 13 && tierStrength(TIERS) <= 6`). Measured with PT-901 applied, over 200 real 15-season careers: reach-top 100% -> **87% at 2 signings/season and 44% at 1**, avg end tier 1.00 -> 1.15 / 1.87, relegations per career 0.00 -> 0.04 / 0.16. Ref: `shared/src/clubseason.ts:86`.
- [x] **PT-903 (audit: manager difficulty): [high] the transfer market is a tier-scaled ratchet: the best listing sits ~+5.0 OVR above the tier's own baseline at EVERY tier, so shopping "at your level" permanently outruns the ladder.** `quality = tierStrength(tier) + headroom` (headroom 3 at tiers 1-2, else 1), then `generateClub`'s ±3 jitter over 20 players and a descending sort hands the buyer the best of them: measured best listing 10.0 at tier 10 (base 5), 15.1 at tier 5 (base 10), 18.9 at tier 2 (base 13, edge +5.9), 19.4 at tier 1 (base 14). In the full career sim, **2 signings/season takes club strength 8.5 -> 19.0 by season 13 and produces 15-2-1 title seasons; 0 signings reaches the top flight 0% of the time and ends at tier 5.12** — the market, not the engine, is what deletes the difficulty. why it hurts: the one systemic lever the manager pulls always pays more than the tier it unlocks, so investment has no opportunity cost and the climb is monotone. fix: `const headroom = tier <= 2 ? 3 : 1;` -> `tier <= 2 ? 1 : 0`, which measured drops the best-listing edge to +3.7 at tier 1 and +4.2 at tier 10; combined with PT-901+PT-902 this lands 200 careers at 44% (1 buy) / 87% (2 buys) reach-top and avg end tier 1.87 / 1.15, i.e. the target 55-85% band for a realistic mixed-investment player. Ref: `shared/src/transfermarket.ts:66`.
- [x] **PT-904 (audit: manager difficulty): [medium] the two strength inputs the player emotionally owns are mis-weighted against each other — the bloodline STAR is worth at most +1.09 club strength while facilities/staff hand over +2.8 for free.** `squadStrength` is the flat mean of the top 11 overalls, so the star is 1/11 of it: measured, dropping an OVR-20 star into an 8.55-strength squad yields **9.64 (+1.09)**, an OVR-16 star +0.73, an OVR-12 star +0.36, a same-level star +0.00 — and the age curve swings a further 2.1 (+0.5 under 27 down to −1.6 past 33), i.e. the dynasty star is <7% of the club's strength and is out-weighed by one market signing. Meanwhile `simEdge` grants `(trainLvl-1)*0.4` plus 0.4 per staff hire × 3 = **+2.8 max, worth 2.8 whole tiers** under the current +1/tier ladder. why it hurts: the mode is named after the bloodline and the bloodline is the least significant number in it, while a one-off facilities spend silently outranks it. fix: weight him explicitly — `clubLeagueStrength = squadStrength * 0.82 + starOverall * 0.18 + ageMod` (an OVR-20 star in an 8.5 squad becomes +2.1, an average star still ~0); and trim `simEdge` to `(trainLvl-1)*0.3` + 0.25 per staff = max +1.95, which is ~1.5 tiers under PT-902's ladder. Determinism-safe (pure arithmetic, no rng). Ref: `client/src/main.ts:1129`, `client/src/main.ts:1777`.
- [x] **PT-905 (audit: manager difficulty): [medium] the playtest probe's own club-strength model makes failure mathematically impossible, which is why the harness read "healthy" for years.** `strengthAt` returns `clamp(7 + season * 1.15, 5, 16)` — growth of +1.15/season against a ladder costing +1/tier, unconditional on results, coins or relegation, so every career must out-climb the pyramid; it also caps at 16 while the real market supplies clubs of **19.0**, so the probe simultaneously over-states the climb and under-states the top flight. Re-run under the recommended constants the harness reports top 85% / end tier 1.32 / **title rate 4%** — outside its own 8-45% band — purely from that cap, while the true career sim on the same constants gives 44-87% and 1.15-1.87. why it hurts: the probe is the guardrail for exactly this class of bug and it currently measures a model the game does not have, so tuning against it will mis-tune the game. fix: drive `strengthAt` off the real ratchet instead of a fixed ramp — start from the measured founding value 8.5, add the living-squad drift (+0.01/season at training lvl 1, +0.14 at lvl 3) and 0-2 signings/season drawn at `tierStrength(tier) + 4`, capped at 19.5 — and add a per-season W/D/L spread check so a P18 W18 D0 L0 season flags on its own. Ref: `tools/playtest/analyze_manager_career.ts:21`.

shared/src/storyarcs/*.ts read against the 120-turn AGE_BANDS table (shared/src/career.ts:916) — Grassroots 0-11
(age 10-12), Academy 12-27 (13-14), Scholar 28-45 (15-16), Youth Team 46-65 (17-18), Breakthrough 66-85 (19-20),
